Topological 5d 𝒩 = 2 \mathcal{N}=2 Gauge Theories: Mirror Symmetry and Langlands Duality of A ∞ A_{\infty} -categories of Floer Homologies
Arif Er, Meng-Chwan Tan

Abstract
We explain why on certain five-manifolds, topological 5d 𝒩 = 2 \mathcal{N}=2 gauge theory of Haydys-Witten twist with gauge group G G , is dual to that of Geyer-Mülsch twist with gauge group G L {}^{L}G , where G G is a real, compact Lie group with Langlands dual G L {}^{L}G . In turn, via their 2d and 3d gauged A/B-twisted Landau-Ginzburg model interpretations, we can show that (i) a Fukaya-Seidel-type A ∞ A_{\infty} -1-category of an HW 4 -instanton Floer homology of three-manifolds and (ii) a Fueter-type A ∞ A_{\infty} -2-category of an HW 3 -instanton Floer homology of two-manifolds, are dual to (i) an Orlov-type A ∞ A_{\infty} -1-category of a novel G ℍ L {}^{L}G_{\mathbb{H}} -flat Floer homology of three-manifolds and (ii) a Rozansky-Witten-type A ∞ A_{\infty} -2-category of a novel G 𝕆 L {}^{L}G_{\mathbb{O}} -flat Floer homology of two-manifolds, respectively. We also derive their Atiyah-Floer-type correspondences to symplectic categories. Our work, which demonstrates a mirror symmetry and Langlands duality of (higher) A ∞ A_{\infty} -categories of Floer homologies, therefore furnishes purely physical proofs and gauge-theoretic generalizations of the mathematical conjectures by Bousseau [ 7 ] and Doan-Rezchikov [ 9 ] , and more.



Introduction and Motivation, Summary, and Acknowledgements

Introduction and Motivation

Inspired by our work in
[
18
]
where a novel Vafa-Witten (VW) Floer homology of three-manifolds was physically defined via a 4d topological gauge theory with sixteen supercharges that is VW theory
[
23
]
, a program was initiated in
[
10
]
to physically define other novel Floer homologies via other topological gauge theories with sixteen supercharges. Specifically, a 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
topological gauge theory that is Haydys-Witten (HW) theory was considered in
[
10
]
, where it was used to physically define a novel
\text{HW}_{5}
-instanton Floer homology of four-manifolds, as well as other novel Floer homologies of three and two-manifolds.

In fact, because HW theory is five and not four-dimensional, in
[
10
]
, we were furthermore able to physically realize a visionary mathematical construction
[
15
]
by Haydys of a
gauge-theoretic
Fukaya-Seidel-type (FS-type)
A_{\infty}
-category of a novel Floer homology of three-manifolds. This was possible because HW theory could be recast as a 2d gauged A-twisted Landau-Ginzburg (LG) model whose BPS equation is fundamentally characterized by a holomorphic map.

Beyond these results, in
[
10
]
, we were also able to define novel symplectic Floer homologies of Hitchin moduli spaces, which, in turn, allowed us to derive novel Atiyah-Floer-type correspondences that relate gauge-theoretic Floer homologies to symplectic intersection Floer homologies of Hitchin moduli spaces. This was possible because HW theory could also be topologically reduced to a 3d A-twisted sigma model whose BPS equation is a Fueter map, which is a 3d version of a holomorphic map.

In the sequels
[
11
,
12
]
to
[
10
]
, an 8d
\mathcal{N}=1
topological gauge theory that is Acharya-O’Loughlin-Spence (AOS) theory
[
1
]
was considered, where it was used to physically define novel Floer homologies of higher-dimensional manifolds. Similarly, we were able to physically realize (higher)
A_{\infty}
-categories of Floer homologies because AOS theory could be recast as a 4d, 3d, and 2d gauged A-twisted LG model whose BPS equations are fundamentally characterized by a Cauchy-Riemann-Fueter map, Fueter map, and holomorphic map, respectively, where a Cauchy-Riemann-Fueter map is a 4d version of a holomorphic map.

Beyond these results, in
[
11
,
12
]
, we were again able to physically derive novel Atiyah-Floer-type correspondences between various gauge-theoretic Floer homologies and symplectic intersection Floer homologies of instanton moduli spaces, among other things. This was possible because AOS theory could also be topologically reduced to a 4d A-twisted sigma model whose BPS equation is a Cauchy-Riemann-Fueter map.

Clearly, 8d AOS and 5d HW theory can be regarded as “A-twisted” topological gauge theories. Indeed, the BPS equations that define them, which involve a self-dual field strength
F^{+}
and are given by
F^{+}=0
and a generalization thereof, respectively, are of instanton-type, and instantons are mathematically established to be related to holomorphic maps
[
4
]
which characterize our aforementioned A-twisted LG and sigma models.

One might then ask if 8d and 5d “B-twisted” topological gauge theories exist, whence applying the same computations to them would yield a B-version of our results in
[
11
,
12
]
and
[
10
]
. Such 8d and 5d “B-twisted” topological gauge theories ought to have BPS equations which are given by
F=0
or some generalization thereof, i.e., they ought to be of flat-type – from
F=F^{+}+F^{-}=(DA)^{+}+(DA)^{-}
, if instantons
(DA)^{+}=0
are supposed to be related to holomorphic maps
\bar{\partial}\Phi=0
, then flat connections
(DA)^{+}+(DA)^{-}=0
ought to be related to constant maps
d\Phi=0
which characterize B-twisted LG and sigma models.

Fortuitously, such a 5d “B-twisted” topological gauge theory exists, and it is an
\mathcal{N}=2
theory that is Geyer-Mülsch (GM) theory
[
13
]
whose BPS equation is of flat-type given by a flat
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-connection!

In this paper, we will apply the same computations as before to GM theory, and obtain a B-version of our results in
[
10
]
and
[
12
]
via the resulting B-twisted LG and sigma models.

The computational techniques we employ are primarily those of standard Kaluza-Klein reduction; topological reduction as pioneered in
[
5
]
; recasting gauge theories as supersymmetric quantum mechanics as pioneered in
[
6
]
; the physical realization of Floer homology groups via supersymmetric quantum mechanics in infinite-dimensional space as elucidated in
[
18
]
; and a physical realization of
A_{\infty}
-1-categories and
A_{\infty}
-2-categories via a string and membrane theory in infinite-dimensional space as developed in
[
10
]
and
[
12
]
.

In fact, because the computations, just like in HW theory, involve Hitchin moduli spaces which are Calabi-Yau’s with mirrors that are also Langlands dual
[
14
]
, we have a B-mirror to and Langlands dual of our results in
[
10
]
and
[
12
]
. In other words, there is actually a mirror symmetry and Langlands duality between HW and GM theory!

Let us now give a brief plan and summary of the paper.

A Brief Plan and Summary of the Paper

In

§

2
, we discuss general aspects of a topologically-twisted 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
gauge theory on
M_{5}=M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
resulting from the Haydys-Witten (HW) twist, where the gauge group
G
is taken to be a real, simple, and compact Lie group.
We review the following important results from
[
10
]
of HW theory on such a manifold.
When
M_{4}=C\times\Sigma
, with
C
a Riemann surface of genus
g(C)\geq 2
and
\Sigma
an arbitrary Riemann surface, we topologically reduce HW theory along
C
using the Bershadsky-Johansen-Sadov-Vafa (BJSV) method
[
5
]
to obtain a 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
A-model on
\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
with target the
G
-Hitchin moduli space
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
on
C
in complex structure
\mathbf{K}
.
When
M_{4}=M_{3}\times M_{1}
, with
M_{3}
a closed three-manifold and
M_{1}
an arbitrary one-manifold, we obtain, via a Heegaard split of
M_{3}
at
C
, an equivalence between HW theory and the aforementioned 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
A-model on
I\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
.

In

§

3
, we discuss general aspects of another topologically-twisted 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
gauge theory on
M_{5}=M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
resulting from the Geyer-Mülsch (GM) twist, where the gauge group
G
is also taken to be a real, simple, and compact Lie group.
When
M_{4}=C\times\Sigma
, with
C
and
\Sigma
described as in

§

2
, we topologically reduce GM theory along
C
using the BJSV method to obtain a 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
Rozansky-Witten (RW) B-model on
\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
in complex structure
\mathbf{J}
.
When
M_{4}=M_{3}\times M_{1}
, with
M_{3}
and
M_{1}
described as in

§

2
, we obtain, via a Heegaard split of
M_{3}
at
C
, a
novel
equivalence between GM theory and the aforementioned 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
B-model on
I\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
.

Note that Doan-Rezchikov (DR) conjectured in

\lx@sectionsign
2.6]

\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J})
is hyperkähler, the correspondence between (iii) and (iv) coincides with their conjecture.
Thus, we have furnished a purely physical proof of their mathematical conjecture!

In

§

7
, we physically derive a mirror and Langlands duality between the relevant sigma models and gauge theories studied hitherto.
First, because of an enhanced Homological Mirror Symmetry (HMS) between 2d A and B-models in mirror Hitchin moduli spaces, we are able to obtain a
novel
mirror duality between the 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
A-model with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
and B-model with target
\mathcal{M}^{{}^{L}G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
, on
\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
, where
{}^{L}G
is the Langlands dual of
G
, and
\mathcal{M}^{{}^{L}G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
is mirror to
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
.
In turn, since the 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
gauge theories on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
(resp.
C\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
) are equivalent to the 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
sigma models on
I\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
(resp.
\mathbb{R}^{3}
), it would mean that HW theory of
G
-type and GM theory of
{}^{L}G
-type, on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
(resp.
C\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
), are Langlands dual.

In fact, we are able to provide an even more fundamental derivation of the Langlands duality between the 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
gauge theories on

(resp.
C\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
) via Kapustin-Witten’s 4d

]
, from which the above mirror duality between the 3d

We summarize these dualities and their underlying physical derivations in

, where
\widehat{C}

A 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
Haydys-Witten-twisted Topological Gauge Theory

In this section, we will study the 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
gauge theory on a five-manifold
M_{5}
with the Haydys-Witten (HW) twist,
1
1

1

We refer the reader to
[
10
,
2
]
for a detailed description of the HW twist.

whose gauge group is a real, simple, compact Lie group
G
, and whose BPS equations that its path integral localizes onto are the HW equations
[
15
,
26
]
.
We will also consider several specializations of
M_{5}
that allow us to derive a 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
A-model whose spectrum is effectively given by that of a 2d
\mathcal{N}=(4,4)
A-model.
Finally, via topological invariance, we will obtain an equivalence between the 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
HW-twisted gauge theory on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
and a 3d A-model on
I\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
.

2.1
HW Theory on
M_{5}=M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}

The Field Content

The bosonic field content of HW theory on
M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
consists of scalars
\upsilon,\bar{\upsilon}\in\Omega^{0}(M_{4}\times\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G))
, gauge connections
A_{\mu}\in\Omega^{1}(M_{4},\text{ad}(G))\otimes\Omega^{0}(\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G))
and
A_{t}\in\Omega^{0}(M_{4},\text{ad}(G))\otimes\Omega^{1}(\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G))
, and a self-dual two-form
B_{\mu\nu}\in\Omega^{2,+}(M_{4},\text{ad}(G))\otimes\Omega^{0}(\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G))
.
The fermionic field content consists of scalars
\eta,\tilde{\eta}\in\Omega^{0}(M_{4}\times\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G))
, one-forms
\psi_{\mu},\tilde{\psi}_{\mu}\in\Omega^{1}(M_{4},\text{ad}(G))\otimes\Omega^{0}(\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G))
, and self-dual two-forms
\chi_{\mu\nu},\tilde{\chi}_{\mu\nu}\in\Omega^{2,+}(M_{4},\text{ad}(G))\otimes\Omega^{0}(\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G))
.
Here,
\mu\in\{1,\dots,4\}
are the indices on
M_{4}
,
t
is the coordinate along
\mathbb{R}
, and
\text{ad}(G)
is the adjoint bundle of the underlying principal
G
-bundle.

As the supersymmetry generators transform in the same representation as the fermions above, the HW twist will result in two, linearly-dependent,
scalar
supersymmetry generators
\mathcal{Q}
and
\bar{\mathcal{Q}}
; we shall choose
\mathcal{Q}
to define the twisted theory.
The supersymmetry transformations of the twisted fields generated by
\mathcal{Q}

[
10
]
are

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}\upsilon

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}\bar{\upsilon}

\displaystyle=-i\tilde{\eta}\,,

(2.1)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}\eta

\displaystyle=-2iD_{t}\upsilon\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}\tilde{\eta}

\displaystyle=-2[\upsilon,\bar{\upsilon}]\,,

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}A_{\mu}

\displaystyle=i\psi_{\mu}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}A_{t}

\displaystyle=\eta\,,

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}\psi_{\mu}

\displaystyle=-2D_{\mu}\upsilon\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}\tilde{\psi}_{\mu}

\displaystyle=i(F_{t\mu}+D^{\nu}B_{\nu\mu})\,,

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}\chi_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=-i[B_{\mu\nu},\upsilon]\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}\tilde{\chi}_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=-\left(F^{+}_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{4}[B_{\rho\mu},B^{\rho}_{\nu}]-\frac{1}{2}D_{t}B_{\mu\nu}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}B_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=2\chi_{\mu\nu}\,,

where
\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}
denotes a
\mathcal{Q}
-variation;
F^{+}_{\mu\nu}=\frac{1}{2}(F_{\mu\nu}+\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho\lambda}F^{\rho\lambda})
is the self-dual component of
F_{\mu\nu}
; self-duality of the self-dual fields
\Phi\in\{B,\chi,\tilde{\chi}\}
means that
\Phi_{\mu\nu}=\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho\lambda}\Phi^{\rho\lambda}
;
and
[B_{\mu\rho},B_{\nu}^{\rho}]\equiv g^{\rho\varrho}[B_{\mu\rho},B_{\nu\varrho}]
.

Notice that
\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}
is nilpotent up to gauge transformations generated by
\upsilon
when we add auxiliary fields to the theory off-shell.
As we wish to study the theory whereby the relevant moduli space is well-behaved (i.e., no reducible connections), we shall consider the case where
\upsilon
has no zero-modes.

The BPS Equations

The BPS equations of HW theory on
M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
are obtained by setting to zero the
\mathcal{Q}
-variations of the fermions in (
2.1
), i.e.,
2
2

2

As we are only considering the case where
\upsilon
has no zero-modes, we can take it to be zero in the variations of the fermions.

\displaystyle F_{t\mu}+D^{\nu}B_{\nu\mu}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(2.2)

\displaystyle F^{+}_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{4}[B_{\rho\mu},B^{\rho}_{\nu}]-\frac{1}{2}D_{t}B_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=0\,.

These are the HW equations on
M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
.
Configurations in the space
\mathfrak{A}_{5}
of all
(A,B)
fields on
M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
that satisfy (
2.2
),
3
3

3

Since we will ultimately only consider gauge-inequivalent configurations,
\mathfrak{A}_{5}
is more precisely the space of irreducible
(A,B)
fields on
M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
modulo gauge equivalence.
Similar such spaces to appear in later sections should also be understood as spaces of fields modulo gauge equivalence.

constitute a moduli space
\mathcal{M}_{\text{HW}}
that the path integral of HW theory on
M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
will localize onto.

The
\mathcal{Q}
-exact Action

The
\mathcal{Q}
-exact topological action of HW theory on
M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
is
[
10
,
2
]

\displaystyle S_{\text{HW,}\mathcal{Q}}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}

\displaystyle\int_{M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}}dtd^{4}x\,\mathrm{Tr}\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\left|F^{+}_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{4}[B_{\mu\rho},B^{\rho}_{\nu}]-\frac{1}{2}D_{t}B_{\mu\nu}\right|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left|F_{t\mu}+D^{\nu}B_{\nu\mu}\right|^{2}

(2.3)

\displaystyle+2D_{\mu}\upsilon D^{\mu}\bar{\upsilon}+2D_{t}\upsilon D^{t}\bar{\upsilon}-2[\upsilon,\bar{\upsilon}]^{2}+\frac{1}{2}[B_{\mu\nu},\upsilon][B^{\mu\nu},\bar{\upsilon}]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}B^{\mu\nu}\{\tilde{\eta},\chi_{\mu\nu}\}-\frac{1}{2}B^{\mu\nu}\{\eta,\tilde{\chi}_{\mu\nu}\}-B^{\mu\nu}\{\psi_{\mu},\tilde{\psi}_{\nu}\}-B^{\mu\nu}\{\tilde{\chi}_{\mu\rho},\chi^{\rho}_{\nu}\}

\displaystyle-i\tilde{\eta}D_{\mu}\psi^{\mu}-i\eta D_{\mu}\tilde{\psi}^{\mu}-2i\tilde{\psi}_{\mu}D_{\nu}\chi^{\mu\nu}-2i\psi_{\mu}D_{\nu}\tilde{\chi}^{\mu\nu}-\tilde{\eta}D_{t}\eta-\tilde{\psi}_{\mu}D_{t}\psi^{\mu}-\tilde{\chi}_{\mu\nu}D_{t}\chi^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle-i\upsilon\{\tilde{\eta},\tilde{\eta}\}-i\bar{\upsilon}\{\eta,\eta\}+i\upsilon\{\tilde{\psi}_{\mu},\tilde{\psi}^{\mu}\}+i\bar{\upsilon}\{\psi_{\mu},\psi^{\mu}\}-i\upsilon\{\tilde{\chi}_{\mu\nu},\tilde{\chi}^{\mu\nu}\}-i\bar{\upsilon}\{\chi_{\mu\nu},\chi^{\mu\nu}\}\bigg)\,.

Note that as we will ultimately only be interested in the the
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology spectrum of HW theory, we can (i) subtract from (
2.3
)
\mathcal{Q}
-exact terms
\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}(\eta D^{t}\bar{\upsilon})
,
\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}(\bar{\eta}[\upsilon,\bar{\upsilon}])
, and
\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}(\chi^{\mu\nu}[B_{\mu\nu},\bar{\upsilon}])
, and (ii) via the self-duality of
B
, topological terms involving
B
and bilinear combinations of fermions.
This leaves us with

S_{\text{HW}}=\frac{1}{2e^{2}}\int_{M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}}dtd^{4}x\,\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left|F_{t\mu}+D^{\nu}B_{\nu\mu}\right|^{2}+\left|F^{+}_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{4}[B_{\rho\mu},B^{\rho}_{\nu}]-\frac{1}{2}D_{t}B_{\mu\nu}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

(2.4)

where the “
\dots
” contain the terms involving
\upsilon,\bar{\upsilon}
and fermions in the action.

A Balanced TQFT

One important fact about the HW equations (
2.2
) (modulo gauge equivalence) is that on
M_{4}\times S^{1}
, they are elliptic
[
15
,
26
]
.
In other words, the differential operator
\nabla_{\text{HW}}
associated to the HW equations is an elliptic operator.
4
4

4

The HW equations are nonlinear (differential) equations.
However, the differential operator associated to a system of nonlinear (differential) equations is elliptic if the differential operator associated to its linearization, is.
Therefore, the ellipticity of the HW equations is determined by the ellipticity of the differential operator
\nabla_{\text{HW}}
associated to the linearization of the HW equations, which is a first-order linear differential operator in momentum space.
Let us denote by
\sigma_{\text{HW}}(p)
the principal symbol of
\nabla_{\text{HW}}
, and
\sigma^{\intercal}_{\text{HW}}(p)
its transpose; these are matrix-valued linear functions of momentum
p
.
For
\nabla_{\text{HW}}
to be elliptic,
\sigma_{\text{HW}}(p)
must be invertible for all nonzero real values of
p
.
At any rate, we see in (
2.4
) that a mod-square of the HW equations (
2.2
), after expansion, furnishes the terms in the action of (the bosonic sector of) a (twisted) 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
SYM.
This means that the principal symbol
\sigma_{\text{5d-SYM}}(p)
of the differential operator
\nabla_{\text{5d-SYM}}
associated to the linearized second-order equations (modulo gauge equivalence) of 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
SYM, is equal to
\sigma^{\intercal}_{\text{HW}}(p)\sigma_{\text{HW}}(p)
.

\nabla_{\text{5d-SYM}}
is certainly elliptic, i.e., invertible, as
\sigma_{\text{5d-SYM}}(p)
is the identity matrix multiplied by the principal symbol
f(p)=p^{2}
of the Laplace operator acting on scalars with momentum
p
(which is indeed invertible for all nonzero real values of
p
).
This invertibility of
\sigma^{\intercal}_{\text{HW}}(p)\sigma_{\text{HW}}(p)
therefore implies the invertibility of
\sigma_{\text{HW}}(p)
, and thus the ellipticity of
\nabla_{\text{HW}}
and the HW equations.

Since
\nabla_{\text{HW}}
is an elliptic operator, its index can be used to calculate the difference in the number of fermion zero-modes of HW theory on
M_{4}\times S^{1}
-- it is 0.
5
5

5

The index of an elliptic operator on an odd-dimensional compact manifold is 0
[
3
, Prop. 9.2]
.

Such a TQFT whose difference in the number of fermion zero-modes is 0 is called a balanced TQFT.
As this difference is the same for HW theory on
M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
and
M_{4}\times S^{1}
, where
\mathbb{R}
and
S^{1}
are the directions of time,
6
6

6

Note that fermion zero-modes in a TQFT have zero energy, i.e., they are static and therefore time-invariant. In particular, they have no
t
dependence.
Hence, they are insensitive to the geometry of the
t
-direction.
So, if we were to replace
S^{1}
with
\mathbb{R}
, the fermion zero-modes would not be affected – specifically, their number, and thus difference, would not change.

HW theory on
M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
is also a balanced TQFT.
This will be relevant in later sections.

2.2
A 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
A-model and a 2d
\mathcal{N}=(4,4)
A-model

Consider the specialization
M_{4}=C\times\Sigma
, where
C
and
\Sigma
are Riemann surfaces, and the genus of
C
is
g(C)\geq 2
.
We will perform a topological reduction of HW theory on
M_{5}=C\times\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
along
C
using the Bershadsky-Johansen-Sadov-Vafa (BJSV) reduction method
[
5
]
, as was done in
[
10
,
\lx@sectionsign
6]
.
Let us give a brief description of this BJSV reduction.

A 3d Sigma Model

The BJSV reduction process is effected by writing the metric on
M_{5}
as a block diagonal with a vanishing scale parameter
\varepsilon
, i.e.,
g_{M_{5}}=\varepsilon g_{C}\oplus g_{\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}}
, and taking the vanishing limit of
\varepsilon
.
In taking this vanishing limit, there will be terms in the action (
2.4
) that will diverge due to having negative powers of
\varepsilon
; they need to be set to zero to ensure the finiteness of the resulting 3d theory – we find that these finiteness conditions are the
G
-Hitchin equations on
C
.
At the same time, there will be terms in the action (
2.4
) that will vanish due to having positive powers of
\varepsilon
; they do not play any role in the resulting 3d theory.

Finally, terms in the action (
2.4
) with no power of
\varepsilon
will remain as the only terms that are relevant in the action of the resulting 3d theory; integrating out the auxiliary fields and performing the integration over
C
, we will arrive at the action of a 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
sigma model with target the
G
-Hitchin moduli space
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
of
C
in complex structure
\mathbf{K}

[
10
,
\lx@sectionsign
6]
,
7
7

7

The complex structure of the target space in our sigma model can be determined as follows.
The space of all
(A_{C},B_{C})
fields on
C
, which are the
(A,B)
fields restricted to
C
, is an infinite-dimensional affine space.
Let us denote by
\mathfrak{d}
the exterior derivative on this space; the basis of the
G
-Hitchin moduli space
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C)
of
C
is spanned by
(\mathfrak{d}A_{C},\mathfrak{d}B_{C})
.
Note that
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C)
is a hyperkähler manifold, i.e., it is equipped with three complex structures
(\mathbf{I},\mathbf{J},\mathbf{K})
satisfying the quaternionic relations
\mathbf{I}^{2}=\mathbf{J}^{2}=\mathbf{K}^{2}=-1
and
\mathbf{I}\mathbf{J}=\mathbf{K}
, and three Kähler two-forms
(\omega^{(\mathbf{I})},\omega^{(\mathbf{J})},\omega^{(\mathbf{K})})
.
From the pullback of the Kähler two-form of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C)
onto
\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
in the final expression of our sigma model’s action, we find that this particular Kähler two-form is defined (up to a scaling factor) as
\omega=\int_{C}\mathfrak{d}A_{C}\wedge\mathfrak{d}B_{C}
, which, according to Kapustin-Witten’s (KW) convention in
[
17
, eqn. (4.8)]
, is the definition for
\omega^{(\mathbf{K})}
.
We will use KW’s convention throughout this paper.
Therefore, the complex structure of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C)
in our sigma model is
\mathbf{K}
, so we shall henceforth denote it as
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
.

i.e.,

\displaystyle S_{\text{3d}}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}}dt|dw|^{2}g_{\bar{\imath}j}\,\bigg(\partial_{t}X^{i}\partial_{t}X^{\bar{\jmath}}+\partial_{t}Y^{i}\partial_{t}Y^{\bar{\jmath}}-\lambda^{i}\nabla_{t}\bar{\eta}^{\bar{\jmath}}-\rho^{i}\nabla_{t}\bar{\zeta}^{\bar{\jmath}}

(2.5)

\displaystyle+\partial_{w}X^{i}\partial_{\bar{w}}X^{\bar{\jmath}}+\partial_{w}Y^{i}\partial_{\bar{w}}Y^{\bar{\jmath}}+2i\left(\lambda^{i}\nabla_{w}\bar{\zeta}^{\bar{\jmath}}+\bar{\lambda}^{\bar{\imath}}\nabla_{\bar{w}}\zeta^{j}+\rho^{i}\nabla_{\bar{w}}\bar{\eta}^{\bar{\jmath}}+\bar{\rho}^{\bar{\imath}}\nabla_{w}\eta^{j}\right)\bigg)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}}\mathfrak{R}^{\bar{\imath}j}_{\bar{k}l}\left(\bar{\lambda}_{\bar{\imath}}\lambda_{j}-\bar{\rho}_{\bar{\imath}}\rho_{j}\right)\left(\bar{\eta}^{\bar{k}}\eta^{l}-\bar{\zeta}^{\bar{k}}\zeta^{l}\right)\,.

Here, (i)
w
and
\bar{w}
are complex coordinates of
\Sigma
;
(ii)
g_{\bar{\imath}j}
is the metric on
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
;
and (iii)
\mathfrak{R}^{\bar{\imath}j}_{\bar{k}l}
is the Riemann curvature tensor of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
.
The bosonic worldsheet scalars
(X,Y)
are maps
\Phi:\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
corresponding to the
(A_{C},B_{C})
fields on
C
.
The fermions are sections
(\eta,\zeta,\lambda,\rho)\in\Gamma(\Sigma\times\mathbb{R},\Phi^{*}\mathcal{T})
and
(\bar{\eta},\bar{\zeta},\bar{\lambda},\bar{\rho})\in\Gamma(\Sigma\times\mathbb{R},\Phi^{*}\overline{\mathcal{T}})
where
\mathcal{T}\coloneq T\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
is the tangent bundle of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
and
\overline{\mathcal{T}}
is its complex conjugate.
Covariant derivatives of the fermions are defined as
\nabla_{M}\Psi^{i}=\partial_{M}\Psi^{i}+\varGamma^{i}_{jk}\Psi^{j}\partial_{M}(X^{k}+Y^{k})
and
\nabla_{M}\Psi^{\bar{\imath}}=\partial_{M}\Psi^{\bar{\imath}}+\varGamma^{\bar{\imath}}_{\bar{\jmath}\bar{k}}\Psi^{\bar{\jmath}}\partial_{M}(X^{\bar{k}}+Y^{\bar{k}})
, where
M\in\{t,w,\bar{w}\}
and
\varGamma
is the affine connection of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
.

This 3d sigma model is invariant under the following supersymmetric transformations:

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}X^{i}

\displaystyle=\eta^{i}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}\eta^{i}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}\lambda^{i}

\displaystyle=-\partial_{t}X^{i}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\bar{w}}Y^{i}+i\varGamma^{i}_{jk}\lambda^{j}\left(\eta^{k}+\zeta^{k}\right)\,,

(2.6)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}X^{\bar{\imath}}

\displaystyle=\bar{\eta}^{\bar{\imath}}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}\bar{\eta}^{\bar{\imath}}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}\bar{\lambda}^{\bar{\imath}}

\displaystyle=-\partial_{t}X^{\bar{\imath}}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{w}Y^{\bar{\imath}}+i\varGamma^{\bar{\imath}}_{\bar{\jmath}\bar{k}}\bar{\lambda}^{\bar{\jmath}}\left(\bar{\eta}^{\bar{k}}+\bar{\zeta}^{\bar{k}}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}Y^{i}

\displaystyle=\zeta^{i}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}\zeta^{i}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}\rho^{i}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}Y^{i}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{w}X^{i}+i\varGamma^{i}_{jk}\rho^{j}\left(\eta^{k}+\zeta^{k}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}Y^{\bar{\imath}}

\displaystyle=\bar{\zeta}^{\bar{\imath}}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}\bar{\zeta}^{\bar{\imath}}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}\bar{\rho}^{\bar{\imath}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}Y^{\bar{\imath}}+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\bar{w}}X^{\bar{\imath}}+i\varGamma^{\bar{\imath}}_{\bar{\jmath}\bar{k}}\bar{\rho}^{\bar{\jmath}}\left(\bar{\eta}^{\bar{k}}+\bar{\zeta}^{\bar{k}}\right)\,.

In short, topological reduction of HW theory on
C\times\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
along
C
results in a 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
topological sigma model on
\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
, whose action is (
2.5
), and is invariant under the supersymmetric transformations (
2.6
).

An Effective 2d A-model

The 3d sigma model on
\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
can be equivalently recast as a 1d SQM on
\mathbb{R}
with target the space of maps
\mathfrak{M}(\Sigma,\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K}))
from
\Sigma
to
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
, which is a Riemannian manifold.
8
8

8

In our 3d sigma model,
\mathfrak{M}(\Sigma,\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K}))
is more precisely the space of all
X,Y
maps from
\Sigma
to
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
; it is an infinite-dimensional affine space.
Let us denote by
\delta
the exterior derivative on
\mathfrak{M}(\Sigma,\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K}))
.
The flat Riemannian metric of
\mathfrak{M}(\Sigma,\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K}))
can be defined as
g_{\alpha\beta}=\int_{\Sigma}g_{i\bar{\jmath}}\left(\delta_{\alpha}X^{i}\wedge\star\delta_{\beta}X^{\bar{\jmath}}+\delta_{\alpha}Y^{i}\wedge\star\delta_{\beta}Y^{\bar{\jmath}}+(\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta)\right)
, where
\wedge
and
\star
are the wedge product and Hodge star operator on
\Sigma
, respectively.

Generically, the
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology of this equivalent 1d SQM is given by the de Rham cohomology of
\mathfrak{M}(\Sigma,\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K}))
.
However, the actual
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology that its partition function localizes onto corresponds to the de Rham cohomology of the space of
holomorphic
maps
\mathfrak{M}^{\text{hol}}(\Sigma,\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K}))
from
\Sigma
to
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})

[
10
,
\lx@sectionsign
6.3]
.
Since the 3d sigma model on
\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
and the 1d SQM on
\mathbb{R}
are equivalent, this in turn means that the
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology which the partition function of the 3d sigma model localizes onto can also be described by the de Rham cohomology of
\mathfrak{M}^{\text{hol}}(\Sigma,\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K}))
.

Note that the only QFT whose
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology is described by the de Rham cohomology of
\mathfrak{M}^{\text{hol}}(\Sigma,\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K}))
is a 2d A-model on
\Sigma
with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
.
This therefore means that the
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology of our 3d sigma model on
\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
is
effectively
the
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology of a 2d A-model on
\Sigma
.

The 2d A-model from Another Perspective

We can also understand this as follows.
The 3d sigma model is a
topological
sigma model.
This means that its Hamiltonian is zero, i.e., its spectrum of states (given by its
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology) are necessarily spanned by supersymmetric ground states that are time-invariant.
Therefore, to study the spectrum of the 3d sigma model, it suffices to consider a time-invariant hyperslice along
\mathbb{R}
, i.e., consider an instant in time of the 3d sigma model.
We do this by picking a specific value of
t
along
\mathbb{R}
and treat the fields as being constant in time, i.e., by inserting a delta-function
\delta(t-T_{0})
and setting
\partial_{t}\rightarrow 0
in (
2.5
).
The action on the hyperslice becomes

\displaystyle S_{\text{2d}}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\Sigma}|dw|^{2}g_{\bar{\imath}j}\bigg(\partial_{w}X^{i}\partial_{\bar{w}}X^{\bar{\jmath}}+\partial_{w}Y^{i}\partial_{\bar{w}}Y^{\bar{\jmath}}+2i\left(\lambda^{i}\nabla_{w}\bar{\zeta}^{\bar{\jmath}}+\bar{\lambda}^{\bar{\imath}}\nabla_{\bar{w}}\zeta^{j}+\rho^{i}\nabla_{\bar{w}}\bar{\eta}^{\bar{\jmath}}+\bar{\rho}^{\bar{\imath}}\nabla_{w}\eta^{j}\right)\bigg)

(2.7)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\Sigma}\mathfrak{R}^{\bar{\imath}j}_{\bar{k}l}\left(\bar{\lambda}_{\bar{\imath}}\lambda_{j}-\bar{\rho}_{\bar{\imath}}\rho_{j}\right)\left(\bar{\eta}^{\bar{k}}\eta^{l}-\bar{\zeta}^{\bar{k}}\zeta^{l}\right)\,,

and is left invariant under the following supersymmetric transformations:

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}X^{i}

\displaystyle=\eta^{i}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}\eta^{i}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}\lambda^{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\bar{w}}Y^{i}+i\varGamma^{i}_{jk}\lambda^{j}\zeta^{k}\,,

(2.8)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}X^{\bar{\imath}}

\displaystyle=\bar{\eta}^{\bar{\imath}}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}\bar{\eta}^{\bar{\imath}}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}\bar{\lambda}^{\bar{\imath}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{w}Y^{\bar{\imath}}+i\varGamma^{\bar{\imath}}_{\bar{\jmath}\bar{k}}\bar{\lambda}^{\bar{\jmath}}\bar{\zeta}^{\bar{k}}\,,

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}Y^{i}

\displaystyle=\zeta^{i}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}\zeta^{i}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}\rho^{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{w}X^{i}+i\varGamma^{i}_{jk}\rho^{j}\eta^{k}\,,

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}Y^{\bar{\imath}}

\displaystyle=\bar{\zeta}^{\bar{\imath}}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}\bar{\zeta}^{\bar{\imath}}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}\bar{\rho}^{\bar{\imath}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\bar{w}}X^{\bar{\imath}}+i\varGamma^{\bar{\imath}}_{\bar{\jmath}\bar{k}}\bar{\rho}^{\bar{\jmath}}\bar{\eta}^{\bar{k}}\,.

Notice that we can regard the fermions
(i)
(\eta,\zeta)\in\Omega^{0}(\Sigma,\Phi^{*}\mathcal{T})
and
(\bar{\eta},\bar{\zeta})\in\Omega^{0}(\Sigma,\Phi^{*}\overline{\mathcal{T}})
as zero-forms on
\Sigma
;
(ii)
\bar{\lambda}\in\Omega^{(1,0)}(\Sigma,\Phi^{*}\overline{\mathcal{T}})
and
\rho\in\Omega^{(1,0)}(\Sigma,\Phi^{*}\mathcal{T})
as
(1,0)
-forms on
\Sigma
;
and (iii)
\lambda\in\Omega^{(0,1)}(\Sigma,\Phi^{*}\mathcal{T})
and
\bar{\rho}\in\Omega^{(0,1)}(\Sigma,\Phi^{*}\overline{\mathcal{T}})
as
(0,1)
-forms on
\Sigma
.
It is then clear that (
2.7
) is the action of a 2d
\mathcal{N}=(4,4)
A-model on
\Sigma
with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
.

In short, the spectrum of the 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
topological sigma model on
\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
is indeed given by the spectrum of a 2d
\mathcal{N}=(4,4)
topological A-model on
\Sigma
with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
.

A 3d A-model in
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})

Hence, we can regard the 3d sigma model as an
A-model
.
In other words, BJSV reduction of HW theory on
C\times\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
along
C
results in a 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
topological A-model on
\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
.

2.3
An Equivalence Between HW Theory on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
and the 3d A-model on
I\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}

Consider now HW theory on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
, where
M_{3}
is a closed three-manifold.
In
[
10
,
\lx@sectionsign
7]
, we showed how this HW theory is equivalent to the 3d A-model on
I\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
via a Heegaard split of
M_{3}
along a Heegaard surface
C
, i.e.,
M_{3}=M_{3}^{\prime}\bigcup_{C}M_{3}^{\prime\prime}
(illustrated in
1(a)
).
Let us briefly describe the process.

C
M_{3}^{\prime}
M_{3}^{\prime\prime}

(a)

M_{3}
as a connected sum of three-manifolds
M_{3}^{\prime}
and
M_{3}^{\prime\prime}
along the Riemann surface
C
.

\mathscr{B}
M_{5}^{\prime}
M_{5}^{\prime\prime}

(b)

M_{5}
splits into five-manifolds
M_{5}^{\prime}
and
M_{5}^{\prime\prime}
along their common boundary
\mathscr{B}
.

Figure 1
:
Heegaard splits of
M_{3}
and
M_{5}
.

First, a Heegaard split of HW theory on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
along the Heegaard surface
C
results in two copies of HW theory, one on
M_{5}^{\prime}=M_{3}^{\prime}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
and another on
M_{5}^{\prime\prime}=M_{3}^{\prime\prime}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
, glued along their common boundary
\mathscr{B}
.
Topological invariance of HW theory means that we can perform a Weyl rescaling of the five-manifolds’ metric, whence we can regard
M_{5}^{\prime}=C\times I^{\prime}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
,
M_{5}^{\prime\prime}=C\times I^{\prime\prime}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
, and thus
\mathscr{B}=C\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
.
This allows us to interpret HW theory on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
as the gluing of two copies of HW theory along
\mathscr{B}
.
This is illustrated in
1(b)
.

Next, we perform a topological reduction of the two copies of HW theory along
C
to get 3d A-models with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
on
I^{\prime}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
and
I^{\prime\prime}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
.
These 3d A-models can be equivalently interpreted as 2d A-models on
I^{\prime}\times\mathbb{R}
and
I^{\prime\prime}\times\mathbb{R}
with target the space of maps
\mathfrak{M}(M_{1},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K}))
from
M_{1}
to
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
, with branes
(\mathscr{L}^{\prime}_{0},\mathscr{L}^{\prime}_{1})
and
(\mathscr{L}^{\prime\prime}_{0},\mathscr{L}^{\prime\prime}_{1})
, respectively.
The gluing of the two copies of HW theory along their common boundary
\mathscr{B}
is realized as the union of the two 2d A-models along their common boundary
\mathscr{R}=\mathscr{L}^{\prime}_{1}=\mathscr{L}^{\prime\prime}_{0}
.
Here, the union can be interpreted as a concatenation of the intervals
I^{\prime}
and
I^{\prime\prime}
of the two 2d A-models into a single interval
I=I^{\prime}\oplus I^{\prime\prime}
of a 2d A-model on
I\times\mathbb{R}
with target
\mathfrak{M}(M_{1},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K}))
.
This is illustrated in
Fig. 2
.
In turn, this final 2d A-model can be equivalently interpreted as a 3d A-model on
I\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
.

\mathscr{L}^{\prime}_{0}

\mathscr{L}^{\prime}_{1}
