\mathscr{L}^{\prime\prime}_{1}

\mathbb{R}\times I

Figure 2
:
Union of 2d A-models along their common boundary
\mathscr{R}
.

Lastly, since HW theory is invariant under the topological reduction along
C
, we have an equivalence between HW theory on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
and the 3d A-model on
I\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
.

A 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
Geyer-Mülsch-twisted Topological Gauge Theory

In this section, we will study the 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
gauge theory on a five-manifold
M_{5}=M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
with the Geyer-Mülsch (GM) twist,
9
9

9

We refer the reader to
[
13
]
for a detailed description of the GM twist.

whose gauge group is a real, simple, compact Lie group
G
, and whose BPS equations that its path integral localizes onto are flat
G_{\mathbb{C}}
connections on
M_{5}

[
13
]
.
We will also consider several specializations of
M_{5}
that allow us to derive a 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
B-model whose spectrum is effectively given by that of a 2d
\mathcal{N}=(4,4)
B-model.
Finally, via topological invariance, we will obtain an equivalence between the 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
GM-twisted gauge theory on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
and a 3d B-model on
I\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
.

3.1
GM Theory on
M_{5}=M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}

The Field Composition

The bosonic field content of GM theory on
M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
consists of an auxiliary scalar
H\in\Omega^{0}(M_{5},\text{ad}(G))
, gauge connections
A_{\mu}\in\Omega^{1}(M_{4},\text{ad}(G))\otimes\Omega^{0}(\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G))
and
A_{t}\in\Omega^{0}(M_{4},\text{ad}(G))\otimes\Omega^{1}(\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G))
, and one-forms
\phi_{\mu}\in\Omega^{1}(M_{4},\text{ad}(G))\otimes\Omega^{0}(\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G))
and
\phi_{t}\in\Omega^{0}(M_{4},\text{ad}(G))\otimes\Omega^{1}(\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G))
.
The fermionic content of the theory consists of a scalar
\eta\in\Omega^{0}(M_{4}\times\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G))
, one-forms
\psi_{\mu}\in\Omega^{1}(M_{4},\text{ad}(G))\otimes\Omega^{0}(\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G))
and
\psi_{t}\in\Omega^{0}(M_{4},\text{ad}(G))\otimes\Omega^{1}(\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G))
, and two-forms
\chi_{\mu\nu}\in\Omega^{2}(M_{4},\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{C}}))\otimes\Omega^{2}(\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{C}}))
and
\Lambda_{\mu}\in\Omega^{1}(M_{4},\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{C}}))\otimes\Omega^{1}(M_{4},\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{C}}))
.
Here,
G_{\mathbb{C}}
is the complexification of the real Lie group
G
.

As the supersymmetry generators transform in the same representation as the fermions above, the GM twist will result in a single
scalar
supersymmetry generator
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
.
The supersymmetry transformations of the twisted fields generated by
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}

[
13
]
are

\displaystyle\delta_{\hat{Q}}A_{t}

\displaystyle=\psi_{t}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\hat{Q}}\phi_{t}

\displaystyle=-i\psi_{t}\,,

(3.1)

\displaystyle\delta_{\hat{Q}}A_{\mu}

\displaystyle=\psi_{\mu}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\hat{Q}}\phi_{\mu}

\displaystyle=-i\psi_{\mu}\,,

\displaystyle\delta_{\hat{Q}}\mathcal{A}_{t}

\displaystyle=2\psi_{t}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\hat{Q}}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{t}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\delta_{\hat{Q}}\mathcal{A}_{\mu}

\displaystyle=2\psi_{\mu}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\hat{Q}}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\delta_{\hat{Q}}\psi_{t}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\hat{Q}}\psi_{\mu}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\delta_{\hat{Q}}\Lambda_{\mu}

\displaystyle=-i\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{t\mu}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\hat{Q}}\chi_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=-i\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}\,,

\displaystyle\delta_{\hat{Q}}\eta

\displaystyle=H\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\hat{Q}}H

\displaystyle=0\,,

where
\delta_{\hat{\mathcal{Q}}}
denotes a
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-variation;
(Ia)
\mathcal{A}_{\mu}\coloneq A_{\mu}+i\phi_{\mu}\in\Omega^{1}(M_{4},\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{C}}))\otimes\Omega^{0}(\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{C}}))
,
(Ib)
\mathcal{A}_{t}\coloneq A_{t}+i\phi_{t}\in\Omega^{0}(M_{4},\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{C}}))\otimes\Omega^{1}(\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{C}}))
,
(Ic)
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu}\coloneq A_{\mu}-i\phi_{\mu}\in\Omega^{1}(M_{4},\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{C}}))\otimes\Omega^{0}(\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{C}}))
,
and (Id)
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{t}\coloneq A_{t}-i\phi_{t}\in\Omega^{0}(M_{4},\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{C}}))\otimes\Omega^{1}(\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{C}}))
are
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-connections;
and (II)
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{t\mu}=\partial_{t}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu}-\partial_{\mu}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{t}+[\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{t},\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu}]
and
\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}=\partial_{\mu}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu}+[\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu},\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\nu}]
are field strengths of
\overline{\mathcal{A}}
.

Notice that unlike
\delta_{\mathcal{Q}}
in

§

2.1
,
\delta_{\hat{\mathcal{Q}}}
is nilpotent.

The BPS Equations

The BPS equations of GM theory on
M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
are obtained by setting to zero the
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-variations of the fermions in (
3.1
), i.e.,

\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{t\mu}=0\,,\qquad\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}=0\,.

(3.2)

These are equations defining flat
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-connections on
M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
.
(We shall henceforth refer to similar such equations as
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-flat equations.)
Configurations in the space
\mathfrak{B}_{5}
of all
\overline{\mathcal{A}}
fields on
M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
that satisfy (
3.2
), constitute a moduli space
\mathcal{M}_{\text{GM}}
that the path integral of GM theory on
M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
will localize onto.

The
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-exact Action

The
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-exact topological action of GM theory on
M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
is
[
13
]

\displaystyle S_{\text{GM}}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}}dtd^{4}x\,\mathrm{Tr}\bigg(

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{t\mu}\mathcal{F}^{t\mu}+\frac{1}{4}\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}\mathcal{F}^{\mu\nu}-i\Lambda^{\mu}(\mathcal{D}_{t}\psi_{\mu}-\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\psi_{t})-i\chi^{\mu\nu}\mathcal{D}_{\mu}\psi_{\nu}

(3.3)

\displaystyle-i\psi^{t}\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{t}\eta-i\psi^{\mu}\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}\eta-HD^{t}\phi_{t}-HD^{\mu}\phi_{\mu}-\frac{1}{2}H^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{8}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\left(\chi_{\mu\nu}\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{t}\chi_{\rho\sigma}+2\Lambda_{\mu}\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}\chi_{\rho\sigma}+2\chi_{\rho\sigma}\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}\Lambda_{\mu}\right)\bigg)\,.

Note that as we will ultimately only be interested in the
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-cohomology spectrum of GM theory, we can subtract
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-exact terms
\delta_{\hat{\mathcal{Q}}}(\eta D^{t}\phi_{t})
,
\delta_{\hat{\mathcal{Q}}}(\eta D^{\mu}\phi_{\mu})
, and
\delta_{\hat{\mathcal{Q}}}(\eta H/2)
.
This leaves us with

\displaystyle S_{\text{GM}}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}}dtd^{4}x\,\mathrm{Tr}\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\left|\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{t\mu}\right|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\left|\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}\right|^{2}+\dots\bigg)\,,

(3.4)

where the “
\dots
” contain the fermion terms in the action.

A Balanced TQFT

Recall the explanations in

§

2
which led to the ellipticity of the HW equations (
2.2
) (modulo gauge equivalence) and thus interpretation of HW theory on
M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
as a balanced TQFT.
Applying it to the
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-flat equations (
3.2
) (modulo gauge equivalence), we find that GM theory on
M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
, just like HW theory on
M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
, is also a balanced TQFT.
This will be relevant in later sections.

3.2
A 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
B-model and a 2d
\mathcal{N}=(4,4)
B-model

Consider the specialization
M_{4}=C\times\Sigma
, where
C
and
\Sigma
are Riemann surfaces, and the genus of
C
is
g(C)\geq 2
.
We will perform a topological reduction of GM theory on
M_{5}=C\times\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
along
C
using the BJSV reduction method.

A 3d Sigma Model

The BJSV reduction process
[
5
]
is effected in the same manner as described in

§

2.2
, where we write the metric on
M_{5}
as a block diagonal with a vanishing scale parameter
\varepsilon
, i.e.,
g_{M_{5}}=\varepsilon g_{C}\oplus g_{\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}}
.
The finiteness condition we obtain by setting to zero the divergent terms (in the vanishing limit of
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
) in the action (
3.4
) is the
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-flat equation on
C
.

The terms in the action (
3.4
) with no power of
\varepsilon
will remain as the only terms that are relevant in the action of the resulting 3d theory; integrating out the auxiliary fields and performing the integration over
C
, we will arrive at the action of a 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
sigma model with target
\mathcal{M}^{G_{\mathbb{C}}}_{\text{flat}}(C)\equiv\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
,
10
10

10

The moduli space
\mathcal{M}^{G_{\mathbb{C}}}_{\text{flat}}(C)
of flat
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-connections on
C
is equivalent to
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C)
in a certain complex structure, which can be determined as follows.
The space of
(\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{C},\mathcal{A}_{C})
fields on
C
, which are the
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-connections
(\overline{\mathcal{A}},\mathcal{A})
restricted to
C
, is an infinite-dimensional affine space.
Let us denote by
\hat{\mathfrak{d}}
the exterior derivative on this space; the basis of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C)
is thus spanned by
(\hat{\mathfrak{d}}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{C},\hat{\mathfrak{d}}\mathcal{A}_{C})
.
Since
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C)
is a hyperkähler manifold, i.e., a holomorphic symplectic manifold, it is equipped with three holomorphic symplectic two-forms
(\Upomega^{(\mathbf{I})},\Upomega^{(\mathbf{J})},\Upomega^{(\mathbf{K})})
.
The holomorphic symplectic two-form that appears in the following action is defined (up to a scaling factor) as
\Upomega\coloneq\int_{C}\hat{\mathfrak{d}}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{C}\wedge\hat{\mathfrak{d}}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{C}
, which, according to KW’s convention in
[
17
, eqn. (4.9)]
, is the definition for
\Upomega^{(\mathbf{J})}
.
Therefore, the complex structure of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C)
in our sigma model is
\mathbf{J}
, so we shall henceforth denote it as
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
.

i.e.,

\displaystyle S_{\text{3d}}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}}dtd^{2}x\,g_{i\bar{\jmath}}\,\left(\frac{1}{2}\partial_{M}\overline{\mathcal{Z}}^{i}\partial^{M}\mathcal{Z}^{\bar{\jmath}}-i\varrho_{M}^{i}\nabla^{M}\varsigma^{\bar{\jmath}}\right)

(3.5)

\displaystyle+\frac{i}{2e^{2}}\int_{\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}}\,\Upomega_{ij}\left(\varrho^{i}\wedge\nabla\varrho^{j}+\frac{1}{3}\mathfrak{R}^{j}_{kl\bar{m}}\varrho^{i}\wedge\varrho^{k}\wedge\varrho^{l}\wedge\varsigma^{\bar{m}}\right)\,.

Here, (i)
M
is the index on
\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
;
(ii)
g_{\bar{\imath}j}
is the metric on
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
;
(iii)
\Upomega_{ij}
is the holomorphic symplectic two-form of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
,
and (iv)
\mathfrak{R}^{j}_{kl\bar{m}}
is the Riemann curvature tensor of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
.
The bosonic worldsheet scalars
(\overline{\mathcal{Z}},\mathcal{Z})
are maps
\Phi:\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
corresponding to the
(\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{C},\mathcal{A}_{C})
fields on
C
, respectively.
The fermions are
\varrho\in\Omega^{1}(\Sigma\times\mathbb{R},\Phi^{*}\mathcal{T})
and
\varsigma\in\Omega^{0}(\Sigma\times\mathbb{R},\Phi^{*}\overline{\mathcal{T}})
, where
\mathcal{T}\coloneq T\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
is the tangent bundle of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
and
\overline{\mathcal{T}}
is its complex conjugate.
Covariant derivatives of the fermions are defined as
\nabla_{M}\varrho^{i}_{N}=\partial_{M}\varrho^{i}_{N}+\varGamma^{i}_{jk}(\partial_{M}\overline{\mathcal{Z}}^{j})\varrho^{k}_{N}
and
\nabla_{M}\varsigma^{\bar{\imath}}=\partial_{M}\varsigma^{\bar{\imath}}+\varGamma^{\bar{\imath}}_{\bar{\jmath}\bar{k}}(\partial_{M}\mathcal{Z}^{\bar{\jmath}})\varsigma^{\bar{k}}
, where
\varGamma
is the affine connection of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
.

This 3d sigma model is invariant under the following supersymmetric transformations:

\displaystyle\delta_{\hat{\mathcal{Q}}}\overline{\mathcal{Z}}^{i}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\hat{\mathcal{Q}}}\mathcal{Z}^{\bar{\imath}}

\displaystyle=\varsigma^{\bar{\imath}}\,,

(3.6)

\displaystyle\delta_{\hat{\mathcal{Q}}}\varrho^{i}

\displaystyle=d\phi^{i}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{\hat{\mathcal{Q}}}\varsigma^{\bar{\imath}}

\displaystyle=0\,,

where
d
is the exterior derivative on
\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
.
Together, (
3.5
) and (
3.6
) describe the 3d Rozansky-Witten (RW) topological sigma model on
\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}

[
21
,
16
]
.

In short, topological reduction of GM theory on
C\times\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
along
C
results in a 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
RW topological sigma model on
\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
, whose action (
3.5
) is invariant under the supersymmetric transformations (
3.6
).

An Effective 2d B-model

The 3d RW model on
\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
can be equivalently recast as a 1d SQM on
\mathbb{R}
with target the space of maps
\mathfrak{M}(\Sigma,\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J}))
from
\Sigma
to
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
, which is a Kähler manifold.
11
11

11

In our 3d RW model,
\mathfrak{M}(\Sigma,\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J}))
is more precisely the space of all
\overline{\mathcal{Z}},\mathcal{Z}
maps from
\Sigma
to
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
, where
\overline{\mathcal{Z}}
is complex conjugate to
\mathcal{Z}
with respect to the complex structure of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
.
In other words,
\mathfrak{M}(\Sigma,\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J}))
is a complex infinite-dimensional affine space; let us denote by
\hat{\delta}
the exterior derivative on this space.
The flat Hermitian metric of
\mathfrak{M}(\Sigma,\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J}))
can be defined as
h_{\alpha\beta}=\int_{\Sigma}g_{i\bar{\jmath}}(\hat{\delta}_{\alpha}\overline{\mathcal{Z}}^{i}\wedge\star\hat{\delta}_{\beta}\mathcal{Z}^{\bar{\jmath}}+(\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta))
, and the Kähler two-form can be defined as
\omega_{\alpha\beta}=\int_{\Sigma}g_{i\bar{\jmath}}(\hat{\delta}_{\alpha}\overline{\mathcal{Z}}^{i}\wedge\star\hat{\delta}_{\beta}\mathcal{Z}^{\bar{\jmath}}-(\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta))
, where
\wedge
and
\star
are the wedge product and Hodge star operator on
\Sigma
, respectively.

Generically, the
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-cohomology of this equivalent 1d SQM is given by the Dolbeault cohomology of
\mathfrak{M}(\Sigma,\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J}))
.
However, the actual
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-cohomology that its partition function localizes onto corresponds to the Dolbeault cohomology of the space of
constant
maps
\mathfrak{M}^{\text{const}}(\Sigma,\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J}))
from
\Sigma
to
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
.
Since the 3d RW model on
\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
and the 1d SQM on
\mathbb{R}
are equivalent, this in turn means that the
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-cohomology which the partition of the 3d sigma model localizes onto can also be described by the Dolbeault cohomology of
\mathfrak{M}^{\text{const}}(\Sigma,\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J}))
.

Note that the only QFT whose
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-cohomology is described by the Dolbeault cohomology of
\mathfrak{M}^{\text{const}}(\Sigma,\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J}))
is a 2d B-model on
\Sigma
with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
.
This therefore means that the
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-cohomology of our 3d RW model on
\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
is
effectively
the
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-cohomology of a 2d B-model on
\Sigma
.

2d B-model from Another Perspective

We also can understand this as follows.
The 3d RW model is a
topological
sigma model.
This means that its Hamiltonian is zero, i.e., its spectrum of states (given by its
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-cohomology) are necessarily spanned by supersymmetric ground states that are time-invariant.
Therefore, just like the 3d A-model in

§

2.2
, to study the spectrum of states of the 3d RW model, it suffices to consider a time-invariant hyperslice along
\mathbb{R}
by inserting a delta-function
\delta(t-T_{0})
and setting
\partial_{t}\rightarrow 0
in (
3.5
).
The action on the hyperslice becomes

\displaystyle S_{\text{2d}}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\Sigma}d^{2}x\,g_{\bar{\imath}j}\left(\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\mu}\overline{\mathcal{Z}}^{i}\partial^{\mu}\mathcal{Z}^{\bar{\jmath}}-i\tilde{\varrho}^{i}_{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}\varsigma^{\bar{\jmath}}\right)

(3.7)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\Sigma}g_{\bar{\imath}j}\left(\vartheta^{\bar{\imath}}\wedge\nabla\tilde{\varrho}^{j}+\frac{1}{3}\mathfrak{R}^{j}_{kl\bar{m}}\vartheta^{\bar{\imath}}\wedge\tilde{\varrho}^{k}\wedge\tilde{\varrho}^{l}\wedge\varsigma^{\bar{m}}\right)\,,

where (i)
\mu
is the index on the hyperslice
\Sigma
;
(ii)
\varsigma\in\Omega^{0}(\Sigma,\Phi^{*}\overline{\mathcal{T}})
;
(iii)
\vartheta^{\bar{\imath}}\coloneq g^{\bar{\imath}k}\Upomega_{lk}\varrho^{l}_{t}\in\Omega^{0}(\Sigma,\Phi^{*}\overline{\mathcal{T}})
(
\varrho_{t}
being the
t
-component of
\varrho
in the 3d RW model);
and (iv)
\tilde{\varrho}\in\Omega^{1}(\Sigma,\Phi^{*}\mathcal{T})
is the pullback of
\varrho
in the 3d RW model onto
\Sigma
.
This is the action of a 2d topological B-model on
\Sigma
with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
that is invariant under the following supersymmetric transformations:

\begin{gathered}\delta_{\hat{\mathcal{Q}}}\overline{\mathcal{Z}}^{i}=0\,,\qquad\delta_{\hat{\mathcal{Q}}}\mathcal{Z}^{\bar{\imath}}=\varsigma^{\bar{\imath}}\,,\\
\delta_{\hat{\mathcal{Q}}}\varrho^{i}=d\phi^{i}\,,\qquad\delta_{\hat{\mathcal{Q}}}\varsigma^{\bar{\imath}}=0\,,\qquad\delta_{\hat{\mathcal{Q}}}\vartheta^{\bar{\imath}}=0\,,\end{gathered}

(3.8)

with
d
now being understood as the exterior derivative on
\Sigma
.

In short, the spectrum of the 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
RW model on
\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
is indeed given by the spectrum of a 2d
\mathcal{N}=(4,4)
topological B-model on
\Sigma
with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
.

A 3d B-model in
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})

Hence, we can regard the 3d RW model as a
B-model
.
In other words, BJSV reduction of GM theory on
C\times\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
along
C
results in a 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
B-model on
\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
.

3.3
An Equivalence Between GM Theory on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
and the 3d B-model on
I\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}

Consider now GM theory on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
, where
M_{3}
is a closed three-manifold.
We can repeat the process described in

§

2.3
of
(i) Heegaard splitting this GM theory along a Heegaard surface
C
to obtain two copies of GM theory on
C\times I^{\prime}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
and
C\times I^{\prime\prime}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
glued along their common boundary
\mathscr{B}=C\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
,
and (ii) BSJV reducing along
C
to get two copies of the 3d B-model with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
on
I^{\prime}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
and
I^{\prime\prime}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
“concatenated” into a single 3d B-model on
I\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
, where
I=I^{\prime}\oplus I^{\prime\prime}
.
Since GM theory is invariant under the BJSV reduction along
C
, this means that GM theory
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
is equivalent to a 3d B-model on
I\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
.

Like in

§

2.3
, we have an equivalence between a 5d topological gauge theory and a 3d topological sigma model.

Novel Floer Homologies From GM Theory

In this section, we will study GM theory on
M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
, where
M_{4}
is a closed and possibly decomposable four-manifold.
By recasting it as a 1d SQM, we will physically realize novel gauge-theoretic Floer homologies of four, three, and two-manifolds.

4.1
A
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-flat Floer Homology of Four-Manifolds

Let us consider GM theory on
M_{5}=M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
, where the direction along
\mathbb{R}
is to be understood as a temporal direction.

GM Theory as a 1d SQM

The action of this theory is given by (
3.4
).
In the
real
temporal gauge, i.e.,
A_{t}=0
,
12
12

12

We can appeal to this gauge because when recast as a 1d SQM, the real gauge field in “time” will eventually be non-dynamical and thus integrated out to furnish the terms that contribute to the Christoffel connection in the kinetic terms of the fermions
[
10
]
.

the action can be expressed as

\displaystyle S_{\text{GM},A_{t}=0}

(4.1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}}dtd^{4}x\,\mathrm{Tr}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left|\dot{\overline{\mathcal{A}}^{\nu}}\mathcal{E}_{\nu\mu}-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\overline{\mathcal{D}}^{\nu}\overline{\mathcal{B}}^{\rho\sigma}\right|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\left|\dot{\overline{\mathcal{B}}}^{\rho\sigma}\mathcal{E}_{\rho\kappa}\mathcal{E}^{\kappa}_{\;\sigma}\mathcal{E}_{\mu\nu}+\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\overline{\mathcal{F}}^{\rho\sigma}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

where
(i)
\dot{\overline{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mu}\coloneq\partial_{t}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu}
and
\dot{\overline{\mathcal{B}}}_{\mu\nu}\coloneq\partial_{t}\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{\mu\nu}
;
(ii)
\mathcal{E}_{\mu\nu}\in\Omega^{2}(M_{4})\otimes\Omega^{0}(\mathbb{R})
is a unit two-form on
M_{4}
whose components are
all
equal, i.e.,
\mathcal{E}_{\mu\nu}\equiv 1
for
\mu<\nu
;
(iii)
\frac{1}{2}(\text{Re}(\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{t})-\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{t})\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\mathcal{E}^{\rho\sigma}=\frac{i}{2}\phi_{t}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\mathcal{E}^{\rho\sigma}\eqcolon\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{\mu\nu}\in\Omega^{2}(M_{4},\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{C}}))\otimes\Omega^{0}(\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{C}}))
,
and (iv)
\dot{\overline{\mathcal{B}}}^{\rho\sigma}\mathcal{E}_{\rho\kappa}\mathcal{E}^{\kappa}_{\;\sigma}\mathcal{E}_{\mu\nu}
is trivially zero due to the identity
\epsilon^{\rho\sigma\pi\lambda}\mathcal{E}_{\pi\lambda}\mathcal{E}_{\rho\kappa}\mathcal{E}^{\kappa}_{\;\sigma}\equiv 0
.

After suitable rescalings, we can recast (
4.1
) as a 1d SQM model, where its action will now read

S_{\text{SQM},\mathfrak{B}_{4}}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}dt\left(\left|\frac{d\overline{\mathcal{A}}^{\alpha}}{dt}+g_{\mathfrak{B}_{4}}^{\alpha\beta}\frac{\partial{V_{4}}}{\partial{\overline{\mathcal{A}}^{\beta}}}\right|^{2}+\left|\frac{d\overline{\mathcal{B}}^{\alpha}}{dt}+g_{\mathfrak{B}_{4}}^{\alpha\beta}\frac{\partial{V_{4}}}{\partial{\overline{\mathcal{B}}^{\beta}}}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,.

(4.2)

Here, (i)
(\overline{\mathcal{A}}^{\alpha},\overline{\mathcal{B}}^{\alpha})
and
(\alpha,\beta)
are coordinates and indices, respectively, on the space
\mathfrak{B}_{4}
of irreducible
(\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu},\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{\mu\nu})
fields on
M_{4}
;
(ii)
g_{\mathfrak{B}_{4}}
is the metric on
\mathfrak{B}_{4}
;
and (iii)
V_{4}(\overline{\mathcal{A}},\overline{\mathcal{B}})
is the potential function.

The
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-cohomology of GM Theory

In a TQFT, the Hamiltonian vanishes in the
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-cohomology of the theory.
This means that the non-vanishing operators that span the relevant
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-cohomology of states in GM theory are actually the supersymmetric ground states that are therefore time-invariant.
In particular, for GM theory on
M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
, its relevant spectrum is associated only with
M_{4}
.

With
M_{5}=M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
,
M_{4}
is the far boundary of the five-manifold, and one needs to specify “boundary conditions” on
M_{4}
to compute the path integral.
This can be done by first defining a restriction of the fields to
M_{4}
, for which we shall define as
\Psi_{M_{4}}
, and then specifying boundary values for these restrictions.
Doing this is equivalent to inserting in the path integral, an operator functional
F_{4}(\Psi_{M_{4}})
that is non-vanishing in the
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-cohomology (so that the path integral will continue to be topological).
This means that the corresponding partition function of GM theory can be computed as
[
25
, eqn. (4.12)]
13
13

13

GM theory is a balanced TQFT, whence just as in Vafa-Witten theory, one can define its partition function.

\langle 1\rangle_{F_{4}(\Psi_{M_{4}})}=\int_{\mathcal{M}_{\text{GM}}}F_{4}(\Psi_{M_{4}})\,e^{-S_{\text{GM}}}\,.

(4.3)

Since we have seen that GM theory on
M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
can be expressed as a 1d SQM in
\mathfrak{B}_{4}
, we can thus write the partition function as

\mathcal{Z}_{\text{GM},M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}}(G)=\langle 1\rangle_{F_{4}(\Psi_{M_{4}})}=\sum_{u}\mathfrak{F}^{G}_{\text{GM}}(\Psi_{M_{4}}^{u})\,.

(4.4)

Here,
\mathfrak{F}^{G}_{\text{GM}}(\Psi_{M_{4}}^{u})
, in the
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-cohomology of GM theory, is the
u^{\text{th}}
contribution to the partition function that depends on the expression of
F_{4}(\Psi_{M_{4}})
in the fields on
M_{4}
, evaluated over the corresponding solutions to the
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-flat equation in (
3.2
) restricted to
M_{4}
. The summation in ‘
u
’ is over all presumably isolated and non-degenerate configurations on
M_{4}
in
\mathfrak{B}_{4}
that the equivalent SQM localizes onto.
14
14

14

This presumption that the configurations are isolated and non-degenerate will be justified in
footnote 15
.

Let us now ascertain what the
\mathfrak{F}^{G}_{\text{GM}}(\Psi_{M_{4}}^{u})
’s correspond to.
To this end, we have to first determine the configurations that the SQM localizes onto.
These are configurations that minimize the SQM action (
4.2
), i.e., configurations that set the squared terms therein to zero.
They are therefore given by

\boxed{\frac{d\overline{\mathcal{A}}^{\alpha}}{dt}=-g_{\mathfrak{B}_{4}}^{\alpha\beta}\frac{\partial{V_{4}}}{\partial{\overline{\mathcal{A}}^{\beta}}}\qquad\frac{d\overline{\mathcal{B}}^{\alpha}}{dt}=-g_{\mathfrak{B}_{4}}^{\alpha\beta}\frac{\partial{V_{4}}}{\partial{\overline{\mathcal{B}}^{\beta}}}}

(4.5)

where the squaring argument
[
6
]
means that both the LHS and RHS of both equations are
simultaneously
set to zero.
In other words, the configurations that the SQM localizes onto are fixed (i.e., time-invariant) critical points of the potential
V_{4}(\overline{\mathcal{A}},\overline{\mathcal{B}})
in
\mathfrak{B}_{4}
.

G_{\mathbb{C}}
-BF Configurations on
M_{4}
as Critical Points of the 1d SQM

To determine the explicit form of
V_{4}
, note that the squared term in (
4.2
) originates from the squared term in (
4.1
).
Indeed, setting the expression within the squared terms in (
4.1
) to zero minimizes the underlying 5d action, and this is consistent with setting the expression within the squared terms in (
4.2
) to zero to minimize the equivalent SQM action.
Therefore, we can deduce the explicit form of
V_{4}
by comparing (
4.5
) with (
4.1
).
Specifically, setting to zero the expression within the squared terms in (
4.1
) would give us

\dot{\overline{\mathcal{A}}^{\nu}}\mathcal{E}_{\nu\mu}=\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\overline{\mathcal{D}}^{\nu}\overline{\mathcal{B}}^{\rho\sigma}\,,\qquad\dot{\overline{\mathcal{B}}}^{\rho\sigma}\mathcal{E}_{\rho\kappa}\mathcal{E}^{\kappa}_{\;\sigma}\mathcal{E}_{\mu\nu}=-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\overline{\mathcal{F}}^{\rho\sigma}\,.

(4.6)

Comparing (
4.6
) with (
4.5
), we find that

\boxed{V_{4}(\overline{\mathcal{A}},\overline{\mathcal{B}})=\int_{M_{4}}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\overline{\mathcal{B}}\wedge\overline{\mathcal{F}}\right)}

(4.7)

Thus, the summation in ‘
u
’ in (
4.4
) is over all isolated and non-degenerate critical points of (
4.7
) in
\mathfrak{B}_{4}
that are also fixed.
15
15

15

As we will explain next, the aforementioned critical points correspond to
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-BF configurations on
M_{4}
.
For them to be isolated, the actual dimension of their moduli space needs to be zero.
This is indeed possible with an appropriate choice of
G
and
M_{4}

[
8
,
\lx@sectionsign
V]
; we shall assume such a choice of
G
and
M_{4}
henceforth.
As for their non-degeneracy, a suitable perturbation of
V_{4}(\overline{\mathcal{A}},\overline{\mathcal{B}})
, which can be effected by introducing physically-trivial
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-exact terms to the action, would ensure this
[
10
, footnote 5]
.

Critical points of
V_{4}(\overline{\mathcal{A}},\overline{\mathcal{B}})
are configurations in
\mathfrak{B}_{4}
that set the RHS’s of (
4.5
) to zero, which in turn, correspond to configurations on
M_{4}
that set the RHS’s of (
4.6
) to zero.
Such configurations span the space of solutions to the 4d
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-BF equations on
M_{4}
, for which we shall henceforth refer to as
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-BF configurations.

In summary, the partition function (
4.4
) is an algebraic sum of
fixed

G_{\mathbb{C}}
-BF configurations on
M_{4}
in
\mathfrak{B}_{4}
.

The
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-flat Floer Homology

Notice that (
4.5
) is a gradient flow equation, and that it governs the classical trajectory of the 1d SQM model from one time-invariant
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-BF configuration to another on
M_{4}
, in
\mathfrak{B}_{4}
.
Hence, just as in
[
25
,
18
,
10
,
11
]
, the equivalent 1d SQM model will physically realize a gauge-theoretic Floer homology.

Specifically, the
time-invariant
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-BF configurations on
M_{4}
in
\mathfrak{B}_{4}
, i.e., time-independent solutions to the 4d equations

\boxed{\overline{\mathcal{D}}\overline{\mathcal{B}}=0\qquad\overline{\mathcal{F}}=0}

(4.8)

will generate the chains of a Floer complex with
Morse functional

V_{4}(\overline{\mathcal{A}},\overline{\mathcal{B}})
in (
4.7
).
The
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-flat flow lines, described by time-varying solutions to the
gradient flow equation
(
4.5
), are the Floer differentials such that the number of outgoing flow lines at each time-invariant configuration obeying (
4.8
) is the degree
d_{u}
of the corresponding chain in the Floer complex.

In other words, we can also write (
4.4
) as

\boxed{\mathcal{Z}_{\text{GM},M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}}(G)=\sum_{u}\mathfrak{F}^{G}_{\text{GM}}(\Psi_{M_{4}}^{u})=\sum_{u}\text{HF}^{\text{flat}}_{d_{u}}(M_{4},G_{\mathbb{C}})=\mathcal{Z}^{\text{Floer}}_{\text{flat},M_{4}}(G_{\mathbb{C}})}

(4.9)

where each
\mathfrak{F}^{G}_{\text{GM}}(\Psi_{M_{4}}^{u})
can be identified with a
novel
Floer homology class
\text{HF}^{\text{flat}}_{d_{u}}(M_{4},G_{\mathbb{C}})
, that we shall henceforth name a
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-flat Floer homology class, assigned to
M_{4}
defined by (
4.5
), (
4.7
), (
4.8
), and the description above.

4.2
A
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-flat Floer Homology of Three-Manifolds

Next, let us specialize to
M_{4}=M_{3}\times S^{1}
, and perform a Kaluza-Klein (KK) dimensional reduction of GM theory by shrinking the
S^{1}
circle to be infinitesimally small.

The Quaternionified BPS Equations of GM
4
Theory

Let
x^{4}
be the coordinate of the
S^{1}
circle.
The conditions that minimize GM theory when we KK reduce along
S^{1}
are effectively obtained by KK reduction of the conditions that minimize the action (
4.1
), i.e., by the KK reduction of the BPS equation of GM theory on
M_{3}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
in
real
temporal gauge.
We wish to write the reduced BPS equations in a form that resembles (
4.6
).

To this end, let us first temporarily restore the real temporal gauge field.
Performing a KK reduction of the
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-flat equations (
3.2
) on
M_{3}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
along the
S^{1}
circle, we get

\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}=0\,,\qquad\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{4}=0\,,

(4.10)

where now
\{\mu,\nu\}\in\{t,1,2,3\}
, and
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{4}\equiv A_{4}-i\phi_{4}
, which can then be interpreted as a
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-scalar on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
.

Second, let us introduce a unit one-form
\mathcal{H}\in\Omega^{1}(M_{3}\times\mathbb{R})
whose components are
all
equal, i.e.,
\mathcal{H}_{\mu}\equiv 1
.
This one-form allows us to define
\Omega^{1}(M_{3}\times\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{C}}))\ni\overline{\mathcal{C}}\coloneq\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{4}\mathcal{H}
whose components are also all equal.
This allows us to rewrite the BPS equations on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
as

\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}=0\,,\qquad\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\nu}=0\,,

(4.11)

since component-wise, it is
equal
to (
4.10
).
As the BPS equations of a theory are the classical equations of motion of the theory, the fact that the interpretation of a scalar
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{4}
as a unit one-form
\overline{\mathcal{C}}
reproduces the same classical equations of motion means that this interpretation is also a physically equivalent one.

Third, let us complexify the difference of the LHS’s of (
4.11
) as follows:
16
16

16

Since all components of
\overline{\mathcal{C}}
are equal, the
\epsilon^{\mu\nu}[\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mu},\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\nu}]
term in the following expression is trivially zero.

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{\mu\nu}\left(\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}-[\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mu},\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\nu}]\right)-j\epsilon^{\mu\nu}\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\nu}

(4.12)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{\mu\nu}\left(\partial_{\mu}(\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\nu}-j\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\nu})-\partial_{\nu}(\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu}-j\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\nu})+[\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu}-j\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\mu},\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\nu}-j\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{\nu}]_{\mathbb{H}}\right)

\displaystyle\eqcolon\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{\mu\nu}\left(\partial_{\mu}\overline{\mathscr{A}}_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}\overline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mu}+[\overline{\mathscr{A}}_{\mu},\overline{\mathscr{A}}_{\nu}]_{\mathbb{H}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{\mu\nu}\overline{\mathscr{F}}_{\mu\nu}\,,

where
(i)
\overline{\mathscr{A}}\coloneq\overline{\mathcal{A}}-j\overline{\mathcal{C}}
is a complexification of
\overline{\mathcal{A}}
and
\overline{\mathcal{C}}
;
(ii)
j
satisfies the quaternionic relations with
i
, i.e.,
ij=-ji\eqcolon k
and
i^{2}=j^{2}=k^{2}=-1
;
(iii)
[I_{a}\cdot,I_{b}\cdot]_{\mathbb{H}}\coloneq I_{a}[\cdot,I_{b}\cdot]\equiv I_{b}[I_{a}\cdot,\cdot]=-[I_{b}\cdot,I_{a}\cdot]_{\mathbb{H}}
, where
I_{*}\in\{1,i,j,k\}
, while regular brackets without the “
\mathbb{H}
” subscript are standard commutator brackets;
and (iv)
\overline{\mathscr{F}}
is the field strength of
\overline{\mathscr{A}}
.

Just as
\overline{\mathcal{A}}
is understood as a
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-connection, i.e., a connection valued in the adjoint bundle
\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{C}})
of the underlying
complexified
principal
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-bundle,
\overline{\mathscr{A}}
can be understood as a
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-connection, i.e., a connection valued in the adjoint bundle
\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{H}})
of the underlying
quaternionified
principal
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-bundle.
Since the RHS’s of (
4.11
) are all zero, (
4.12
) tells us that the BPS equation of the 4d theory resulting from the KK reduction of GM theory along an
S^{1}
circle – henceforth referred to as GM
4
theory – is the
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-flat equation on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
, i.e.,
\overline{\mathscr{F}}_{\mu\nu}=0
.

Finally, working in the
real
temporal gauge, the
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-flat equation on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
can be expressed as

\dot{\overline{\mathscr{A}}}^{n}\mathscr{E}_{nm}=\epsilon_{mpq}\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{p}\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{q}\,,\qquad\dot{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}^{n}\mathscr{E}_{n}^{\;p}\mathscr{E}_{p}^{\;q}\mathscr{E}_{qm}=-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{mpq}\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{pq}\,,

(4.13)

where (i)
\{m,n,p,q\}\in\{1,2,3\}
are indices on
M_{3}
;
(ii)
\mathscr{E}\in\Omega^{2}(M_{3})\otimes\Omega^{0}(\mathbb{R})
is a unit two-form on
M_{3}
whose components are
all
equal, i.e.,
\mathscr{E}_{mn}\equiv 1
for
m<n
;
(iii)
\frac{1}{2}(\text{Re}(\overline{\mathscr{A}}_{t})-\overline{\mathscr{A}}_{t})\epsilon_{mpq}\mathscr{E}^{pq}=\frac{1}{2}(i\phi_{t}+jA_{4}+k\phi_{4})\epsilon_{mpq}\mathscr{E}^{pq}\eqcolon\overline{\mathscr{B}}_{m}\in\Omega^{1}(M_{3},\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{H}}))\otimes\Omega^{0}(\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{H}}))
,
and (iv)
\dot{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}^{n}\mathscr{E}_{n}^{\;p}\mathscr{E}_{p}^{\;q}\mathscr{E}_{qm}
is trivially zero due to the identity
\epsilon^{npq}\mathscr{E}_{pq}\mathscr{E}_{nm}\equiv 0
.
Notice that (
4.13
) has the same form of equations as (
4.6
), but with the gauge group complexified from
G_{\mathbb{C}}
to
G_{\mathbb{H}}
.

In other words, GM theory on
M_{3}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
, upon a KK reduction along
S^{1}
to GM
4
theory on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
, localizes onto
\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{H}})
-valued configurations that obey (
4.13
).

GM
4
Theory as a 1d SQM in
\mathfrak{B}_{3}

The 4d action can thus be written (in the
real
temporal gauge) as

\displaystyle S_{\text{GM${}_{4}$},A_{t}=0}

(4.14)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}}dtd^{3}x\mathrm{Tr}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left|\dot{\overline{\mathscr{A}}}^{n}\mathscr{E}_{nm}-\epsilon_{mpq}\overline{\mathscr{D}}^{p}\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{q}\right|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\left|\dot{\overline{\mathscr{B}}}^{n}\mathscr{E}_{n}^{\;p}\mathscr{E}_{p}^{\;q}\mathscr{E}_{qm}+\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{mpq}\overline{\mathscr{F}}^{pq}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,.

After suitable rescalings, we can recast (
4.14
) as a 1d SQM model, where its action will now read

S_{\text{SQM},\mathfrak{B}_{3}}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}dt\left(\left|\frac{d\overline{\mathscr{A}}^{\alpha}}{dt}+g_{\mathfrak{B}_{3}}^{\alpha\beta}\frac{\partial{V_{3}}}{\partial{\overline{\mathscr{A}}^{\beta}}}\right|^{2}+\left|\frac{d\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\alpha}}{dt}+g_{\mathfrak{B}_{3}}^{\alpha\beta}\frac{\partial{V_{3}}}{\partial{\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\beta}}}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,.

(4.15)

Here, (i)
(\overline{\mathscr{A}}^{\alpha},\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\alpha})
and
(\alpha,\beta)
are coordinates and indices, respectively, on the space
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
of irreducible
(\overline{\mathscr{A}}_{m},\overline{\mathscr{B}}_{m})
fields on
M_{3}
;
(ii)
g_{\mathfrak{B}_{3}}
is the metric on
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
;
and (iii)
V_{3}(\overline{\mathscr{A}},\overline{\mathscr{B}})
is the potential function.

Localizing onto Time-invariant
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-BF Configurations on
M_{3}

By the squaring argument
[
6
]
applied to (
4.15
), the configurations that the equivalent SQM localizes onto are those that set the LHS’s and RHS’s of (
4.13
)
simultaneously
to zero.
Such configurations in
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
correspond to time-invariant configurations that span the space of solutions to the 3d
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-BF equations on
M_{3}
.
We shall, in the rest of this paper, refer to such configurations as
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-BF configurations on
M_{3}
.

In summary, the equivalent SQM localizes onto time-invariant
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-BF configurations on
M_{3}
in
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
.

The
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-flat Floer Homology

Since the resulting 4d theory on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
can be interpreted as a 1d SQM in
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
, its partition function can, like in (
4.4
), be written as

\mathcal{Z}_{\text{GM},M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}}(G)=\langle 1\rangle_{F_{3}(\Psi_{M_{3}})}=\sum_{v}\mathfrak{F}^{G}_{\text{GM${}_{4}$}}(\Psi_{M_{3}}^{v})\,,

(4.16)

where
\mathfrak{F}^{G}_{\text{GM${}_{4}$}}(\Psi_{M_{3}}^{v})
, in the
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-cohomology of GM
4
theory, is the
v^{\text{th}}
contribution to the partition function that depends on the expression
F_{3}(\Psi_{M_{3}})
in the bosonic fields on
M_{3}
, and the summation in ‘
v
’ is over all isolated and non-degenerate
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-BF configurations on
M_{3}
in
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
that the equivalent SQM localizes onto.
17
17

17

This presumption that the configurations will be isolated and non-degenerate is justified because the (
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-cohomology of) GM theory is topological in all directions and therefore invariant when we shrink the
S^{1}
circle.
Thus, if
M_{3}
(where
M_{3}\times S^{1}=M_{4}
) and
G
are chosen such as to satisfy the conditions spelt out in
footnote 15
,
\mathcal{Z}_{\text{GM},M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}}(G)
will be a discrete and non-degenerate sum of contributions, just like
\mathcal{Z}_{\text{GM},M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}}(G)
.
We shall henceforth assume such a choice of
M_{3}
whence the presumption would hold.

Let us now ascertain what the
\mathfrak{F}^{G}_{\text{GM${}_{4}$}}(\Psi_{M_{3}}^{v})
’s correspond to.
Repeating here the analysis in

§

4.1
with (
4.15
) as the action for the equivalent SQM model, we find that we can also write (
4.16
) as

\boxed{\mathcal{Z}_{\text{GM},M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}}(G)=\sum_{v}\mathfrak{F}^{G}_{\text{GM${}_{4}$}}(\Psi_{M_{3}}^{v})=\sum_{v}\text{HF}^{\text{flat}}_{d_{v}}(M_{3},G_{\mathbb{H}})=\mathcal{Z}^{\text{Floer}}_{\text{flat},M_{3}}(G_{\mathbb{H}})}

(4.17)

where each
\mathfrak{F}^{G}_{\text{GM${}_{4}$}}(\Psi_{M_{3}}^{v})
can be identified with a
novel
gauge-theoretic Floer homology class
\text{HF}^{\text{flat}}_{d_{v}}(M_{3},

G_{\mathbb{H}})
, that we shall henceforth name a
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-flat Floer homology class, of degree
d_{v}
, assigned to
M_{3}
.

Specifically, the
time-invariant
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-BF configurations on
M_{3}
in
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
that obey the simultaneous vanishing of the LHS and RHS of the
gradient flow equations

\boxed{\frac{d\overline{\mathscr{A}}^{\alpha}}{dt}=-g_{\mathfrak{B}_{3}}^{\alpha\beta}\frac{\partial{V_{3}}}{\partial{\overline{\mathscr{A}}^{\beta}}}\qquad\frac{d\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\alpha}}{dt}=-g_{\mathfrak{B}_{3}}^{\alpha\beta}\frac{\partial{V_{3}}}{\partial{\overline{\mathscr{B}}^{\beta}}}}

(4.18)

will generate the chains of the
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-flat Floer complex with
Morse functional

\boxed{V_{3}(\overline{\mathscr{A}},\overline{\mathscr{B}})=\int_{M_{3}}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\overline{\mathscr{B}}\wedge\overline{\mathscr{F}}\right)}

(4.19)

in
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
.
The
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-flat flow lines, described by time-varying solutions to (
4.18
), are the Floer differentials such that the degree
d_{v}
of the corresponding chain in the
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-flat Floer complex is counted by the outgoing flow lines at each time-invariant
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-BF configuration on
M_{3}
in
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
.
Such a configuration corresponds to a time-independent solution to the 3d equations

\boxed{\overline{\mathscr{D}}\overline{\mathscr{B}}=0\qquad\overline{\mathscr{F}}=0}

(4.20)

4.3
A
G_{\mathbb{O}}
-flat Floer Homology of Two-Manifolds

Let us now further specialize to
M_{3}=M_{2}\times S^{1}
, and perform a KK dimensional reduction of GM
4
theory by shrinking the
S^{1}
circle to be infinitesimally small.

The Octonionified BPS Equations of GM
3
Theory

The KK reduction of the
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-flat BPS equation of GM
4
theory on
M_{2}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
, is algorithmically the same as the KK reduction of the
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-flat BPS equation of GM theory on
M_{3}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
in

§

4.2
.
Before we say more about reduction process, let us first relabel the quaternionic imaginary numbers
(i,j,k)
as
(\mathbf{e}_{01},\mathbf{e}_{02},\mathbf{e}_{03})
, and introduce an imaginary number
e_{0}
that satisfies the octonionic relations with
(\mathbf{e}_{01},\mathbf{e}_{02},\mathbf{e}_{03})
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathbf{e}_{0i}\mathbf{e}_{0j}

\displaystyle=-\delta_{ij}+\epsilon_{ijk}\mathbf{e}_{0k}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\mathbf{e}_{0}\mathbf{e}_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{e}_{0i}=\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{ijk}\mathbf{e}_{j}\mathbf{e}_{k}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\mathbf{e}_{0}\mathbf{e}_{0i}=-\mathbf{e}_{i}\,,

(4.21)

\displaystyle\mathbf{e}_{i}\mathbf{e}_{j}

\displaystyle=-\delta_{ij}-\epsilon_{ijk}\mathbf{e}_{0k}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\mathbf{e}_{i}\mathbf{e}_{0j}

\displaystyle=\delta_{ij}\mathbf{e}_{0}-\epsilon_{ijk}\mathbf{e}_{k}\,.

This will be useful shortly.

Let
x^{3}
be the coordinate of the
S^{1}
circle.
The reduction process produces a
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-scalar
\overline{\mathscr{A}}_{3}\equiv\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{3}-\mathbf{e}_{02}\overline{\mathcal{C}}_{3}\equiv A_{3}-\mathbf{e}_{01}\phi_{3}-\mathbf{e}_{02}A_{4}-\mathbf{e}_{03}\phi_{4}
that, when multiplied by a unit one-form
\mathscr{H}_{m}
, can be equivalently interpreted as a one-form
\overline{\mathscr{A}}_{3}\mathscr{H}_{m}\eqcolon\overline{\mathscr{C}}_{m}\in\Omega^{1}(M_{2}\times\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{H}}))
whose components are
all
equal, where
m\in\{t,1,2\}
.

The BPS equations of the 3d theory resulting from the KK reduction of GM
4
theory on
M_{2}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
along the
S^{1}
circle – henceforth referred to as GM
3
theory – can then be complexified
à la
(
4.12
) to become the
G_{\mathbb{O}}
-flat equation,
\overline{\mathsf{F}}_{mn}=0
, on
M_{2}\times\mathbb{R}
.
Here
\overline{\mathsf{F}}
is the field strength of
\overline{\mathsf{A}}\coloneq\overline{\mathscr{A}}-\mathbf{e}_{0}\overline{\mathscr{C}}
, a complexification of
\overline{\mathscr{A}}
and
\overline{\mathscr{C}}
which can be understood as a
G_{\mathbb{O}}
-connection, i.e., a connection valued in the adjoint bundle
\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{O}})
of the underlying
octonionified
principal
G_{\mathbb{O}}
-bundle.

Finally, working in the
real
temporal gauge, the
G_{\mathbb{O}}
-flat equation on
M_{2}\times\mathbb{R}
can be expressed as

\dot{\overline{\mathsf{A}}}^{N}\mathsf{E}_{NM}=\epsilon_{MN}\overline{\mathsf{D}}^{N}\overline{\mathsf{B}}\,,\qquad\dot{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}\mathsf{E}^{M}_{\;N}\mathsf{E}^{N}_{\;P}\mathsf{E}^{P}_{\;M}=-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{MN}\overline{\mathsf{F}}^{MN}\,,

(4.22)

where (i)
\{M,N,P\}\in\{1,2\}
are indices on
M_{2}
;
(ii)
\mathsf{E}_{MN}\in\Omega^{2}(M_{2})\otimes\Omega^{0}(\mathbb{R})
is a unit two-form on
M_{2}
, i.e.,
\mathsf{E}_{12}=1=-\mathsf{E}_{21}
;
(iii)
\frac{1}{2}(\text{Re}(\overline{\mathscr{A}}_{t})-\overline{\mathscr{A}}_{t})\epsilon_{MN}\mathsf{E}^{MN}=\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{e}_{01}\phi_{t}+\mathbf{e}_{02}A_{4}+\mathbf{e}_{03}\phi_{4}+\mathbf{e}_{0}A_{3}+\mathbf{e}_{1}\phi_{3}+\mathbf{e}_{2}A_{4}+\mathbf{e}_{3}\phi_{4})\epsilon_{MN}\mathsf{E}^{MN}\eqcolon\overline{\mathsf{B}}\in\Omega^{2}(M_{2},\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{O}}))\otimes\Omega^{0}(\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{O}}))
,
and (iv)
\dot{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}\mathsf{E}^{M}_{\;N}\mathsf{E}^{N}_{\;P}\mathsf{E}^{P}_{\;M}=\dot{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}\mathsf{E}^{M}_{\;M}
is trivially zero due to its anti-symmetricity.
This set of equations has the same form as (
4.13
), just with the gauge group complexified from
G_{\mathbb{H}}
to
G_{\mathbb{O}}
.

In other words, GM
4
theory on
M_{2}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
, upon a KK reduction along
S^{1}
to GM
3
theory on
M_{2}\times\mathbb{R}
, localizes onto
\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{O}})
-valued configurations that obey (
4.22
).

GM
3
Theory as a 1d SQM in
\mathfrak{B}_{2}

The 3d action can thus be written (in the
real
temporal gauge) as

\displaystyle S_{\text{GM${}_{3}$},A_{t}=0}

(4.23)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{M_{2}\times\mathbb{R}}dtd^{2}x\,\mathrm{Tr}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left|\dot{\overline{\mathsf{A}}}^{N}\mathsf{E}_{NM}-\epsilon_{MN}\overline{\mathsf{D}}^{N}\overline{\mathsf{B}}\right|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\left|\dot{\overline{\mathsf{B}}}\mathsf{E}^{M}_{\;N}\mathsf{E}^{N}_{\;P}\mathsf{E}^{P}_{\;M}+\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{MN}\overline{\mathsf{F}}^{MN}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,.

After suitable rescalings, we can recast (
4.23
) as a 1d SQM model, where its action will now read

S_{\text{SQM},\mathfrak{B}_{2}}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}dt\left(\left|\frac{d\overline{\mathsf{A}}^{\alpha}}{dt}+g_{\mathfrak{B}_{2}}^{\alpha\beta}\frac{\partial{V_{2}}}{\partial{\overline{\mathsf{A}}^{\beta}}}\right|^{2}+\left|\frac{d\overline{\mathsf{B}}^{\alpha}}{dt}+g_{\mathfrak{B}_{2}}^{\alpha\beta}\frac{\partial{V_{2}}}{\partial{\overline{\mathsf{B}}^{\beta}}}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,.

(4.24)

Here, (i)
(\overline{\mathsf{A}}^{\alpha},\overline{\mathsf{B}}^{\alpha})
and
(\alpha,\beta)
are coordinates and indices, respectively, on the space
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
of irreducible
(\overline{\mathsf{A}}_{M},\overline{\mathsf{B}})
fields on
M_{2}
;
(ii)
g_{\mathfrak{B}_{2}}
is the metric on
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
;
and (iii)
V_{2}(\overline{\mathsf{A}},\overline{\mathsf{B}})
is the potential function.

Localizing onto Time-invariant
G_{\mathbb{O}}
-BF Configurations on
M_{2}

By the squaring argument
[
6
]
applied to (
4.24
), the configurations that the equivalent SQM localizes onto are those that set the LHS’s and RHS’s of (
4.22
)
simultaneously
to zero.
Such configurations in
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
correspond to time-invariant configurations that span the space of solutions to the 2d

-BF equations on

.
We shall, in the rest of this paper, refer to such configurations as

-BF configurations on
M_{2}
.

In summary, the equivalent SQM localizes onto time-invariant
G_{\mathbb{O}}
-BF configurations on
M_{2}
in
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
.

The
G_{\mathbb{O}}
-flat Floer Homology

Since the resulting 3d theory on
M_{2}\times\mathbb{R}
can be interpreted as a 1d SQM in
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
, its partition function can, like in (

), be written as

\mathcal{Z}_{\text{GM},M_{2}\times\mathbb{R}}(G)=\langle 1\rangle_{F_{2}(\Psi_{M_{2}})}=\sum_{w}\mathfrak{F}^{G}_{\text{GM${}_{3}$}}(\Psi_{M_{2}}^{w})\,,

where
\mathfrak{F}^{G}_{\text{GM${}_{3}$}}(\Psi_{M_{2}}^{w})
, in the

-cohomology of GM

theory, is the

contribution to the partition function that depends on the expression

in the bosonic fields on
M_{2}
, and the summation in ‘

’ is over all isolated and non-degenerate
G_{\mathbb{O}}
-BF configurations on
M_{2}
in
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
that the equivalent SQM localizes onto.
18
18

18

This presumption that the configurations will be isolated and non-degenerate is justified because the (
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-cohomology of) GM theory is topological in all directions and therefore invariant when we shrink another
S^{1}
circle from GM
4
theory.
Thus, if
M_{2}
(where

,

Let us now ascertain what the

\boxed{\mathcal{Z}_{\text{GM},M_{2}\times\mathbb{R}}(G)=\sum_{w}\mathfrak{F}^{G}_{\text{GM${}_{3}$}}(\Psi_{M_{2}}^{w})=\sum_{w}\text{HF}^{\text{flat}}_{d_{w}}(M_{2},G_{\mathbb{O}})=\mathcal{Z}^{\text{Floer}}_{\text{flat},M_{2}}(G_{\mathbb{O}})}

(4.26)

where each
\mathfrak{F}^{G}_{\text{GM${}_{3}$}}(\Psi_{M_{2}}^{w})
can be identified with a

\text{HF}^{\text{flat}}_{d_{w}}(M_{2},

, that we shall henceforth name a

, assigned to
M_{2}
.

Specifically, the

-BF configurations on

in

that obey the simultaneous vanishing of the LHS and RHS of the

\boxed{\frac{d\overline{\mathsf{A}}^{\alpha}}{dt}=-g_{\mathfrak{B}_{2}}^{\alpha\beta}\frac{\partial{V_{2}}}{\partial{\overline{\mathsf{A}}^{\beta}}}\qquad\frac{d\overline{\mathsf{B}}^{\alpha}}{dt}=-g_{\mathfrak{B}_{2}}^{\alpha\beta}\frac{\partial{V_{2}}}{\partial{\overline{\mathsf{B}}^{\beta}}}}

(4.27)

will generate the chains of the
G_{\mathbb{O}}
-flat Floer complex with

\boxed{V_{2}(\overline{\mathsf{A}},\overline{\mathsf{B}})=\int_{M_{2}}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\overline{\mathsf{B}}\wedge\overline{\mathsf{F}}\right)}

(4.28)

in
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
.
The
G_{\mathbb{O}}
-flat flow lines, described by time-varying solutions to (

), are the Floer differentials such that the degree

of the corresponding chain in the
