.
Such a configuration corresponds to a time-independent solution to the 2d equations

\boxed{\overline{\mathsf{D}}\overline{\mathsf{B}}=0\qquad\overline{\mathsf{F}}=0}

(4.29)

A Novel Orlov-type
A_{\infty}
-1-category of Three-Manifolds

In this section, we will study GM theory on
M_{5}=M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, where
M_{3}
is a closed three-manifold.
We will recast it as a 2d gauged B-twisted Landau-Ginzburg (LG) model and a 1d LG SQM.
Following what we did in our previous work
[
10
,
\lx@sectionsign
9]
, we will, via the 5d GM partition function and its equivalent 2d gauged B-twisted LG model, be able to physically realize a novel gauge-theoretic Orlov-type
A_{\infty}
-1-category that categorifies the
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-flat Floer homology of
M_{3}
from

§

4.2
.
Also, via a Heegaard split at
M_{3}
along a Riemann surface
C
, we will be able to physically realize an RW
A_{\infty}
-2-category and a Kapustin-Rozansky-Saulina (KRS) 2-category of complex-Lagrangian branes in
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J})
.

5.1
GM Theory on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
as a 2d Model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
or 1d SQM

GM Theory on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
as a 2d Gauged Sigma Model

Let
t,\tau
be the coordinates along
\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
The action of this theory is given by

\displaystyle S_{\text{GM}}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}dtd\tau d^{3}x\,\mathrm{Tr}\bigg(

\displaystyle\left|F_{t\tau}-iD_{t}\phi_{\tau}+iD_{\tau}\phi_{t}-[\phi_{t},\phi_{\tau}]\vphantom{\overline{\mathcal{F}}}\right|^{2}+\left|\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{mn}\right|^{2}

(5.1)

\displaystyle+\left|D_{t}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{m}-\partial_{m}A_{t}+i\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{m}\phi_{t}\right|^{2}+\left|D_{\tau}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{m}-\partial_{m}A_{\tau}+i\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{m}\phi_{\tau}\right|^{2}+\dots\bigg)\,,

where the conditions that minimize the action (
5.1
) are identified to be

\displaystyle F_{t\tau}-iD_{t}\phi_{\tau}+iD_{\tau}\phi_{t}-[\phi_{t},\phi_{\tau}]

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad D_{t}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{m}-\partial_{m}A_{t}+i\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{m}\phi_{t}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(5.2)

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{mn}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad D_{\tau}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{m}-\partial_{m}A_{\tau}+i\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{m}\phi_{\tau}

\displaystyle=0\,.

Notice that we can complexify (
5.2
) in a similar manner as was done for the BPS equations in

§

4.2
.
The complexified conditions are

\displaystyle(D_{\tau}+jD_{t})\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{m}\mathscr{E}_{mn}-D_{\tau}\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{m}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}_{mn}+F_{t\tau}\mathscr{H}^{m}\mathscr{E}_{mn}-\partial^{m}(A_{\tau}+jA_{t})\mathscr{E}_{mn}

\displaystyle=j\epsilon_{npq}\widetilde{\mathscr{D}}^{p}\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{q}\,,

(5.3)

\displaystyle D_{\tau}\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{m}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}_{mn}+(D_{\tau}+jD_{t})\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{n}\mathscr{E}_{n}^{\;p}\mathscr{E}_{p}^{\;q}\mathscr{E}_{qm}-\partial^{m}A_{\tau}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}_{mn}

\displaystyle=-\frac{j}{2}\epsilon_{npq}\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}^{pq}\,,

where
(i)
\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}_{m}\coloneq\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{m}-k\phi_{\tau}\mathscr{H}_{m}
,
(ii)
\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}_{m}\coloneq\frac{1}{2}(i\phi_{t}+k\phi_{\tau})\epsilon_{mpq}\mathscr{E}^{pq}
,
and (iii)
\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}_{mn}=\epsilon_{mnp}\mathscr{H}^{p}
.
19
19

19

Note that when compared to the fields in

§

4.2
,
\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}_{m}=\overline{\mathscr{A}}_{m}+jA_{\tau}\mathscr{H}_{m}
and
\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}_{m}=\overline{\mathscr{B}}_{m}-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{mpq}jA_{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{pq}
.
We choose to use the
\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}
and
\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}
fields in this subsection because we want the real gauge fields of
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, i.e.,
A_{t}
and
A_{\tau}
, to be explicit in the equations.

Moreover, noting that we are physically free to rotate
\mathbb{R}^{2}
about the origin, (
5.3
) becomes

\displaystyle(D_{\tau}+jD_{t})\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{m}\mathscr{E}_{mn}-e^{j\theta}(\cos(\theta)D_{\tau}+\sin(\theta)D_{t})\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{m}\mathscr{H}_{mn}+e^{j\theta}F_{t\tau}\mathscr{H}^{m}\mathscr{E}_{mn}+p_{n}(\theta)

\displaystyle=0\,,

(5.4)

\displaystyle e^{j\theta}(\cos(\theta)D_{\tau}+\sin(\theta)D_{t})\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{m}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}_{mn}+(D_{\tau}+jD_{t})\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{n}\mathscr{E}_{n}^{\;p}\mathscr{E}_{p}^{\;q}\mathscr{E}_{qm}+q_{n}(\theta)

\displaystyle=0\,,

and

\displaystyle p_{n}(\theta)

\displaystyle=-\partial^{m}(A_{\tau}+jA_{t})\mathscr{E}_{mn}-je^{j\theta}\epsilon_{npq}\widetilde{\mathscr{D}}^{p}\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{q}\,,

(5.5)

\displaystyle q_{n}(\theta)

\displaystyle=-e^{j\theta}\partial^{m}\Big(A_{\tau}\cos(\theta)+A_{t}\sin(\theta)\Big)\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}_{mn}+\frac{je^{j\theta}}{2}\epsilon_{npq}\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}^{pq}\,.

Thus, we can also write (
5.1
) as

\displaystyle S_{\text{GM}}

(5.6)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}dtd\tau d^{3}x\,\mathrm{Tr}\bigg(\left|e^{j\theta}(\cos(\theta)D_{\tau}+\sin(\theta)D_{t})\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{m}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}_{mn}+(D_{\tau}+jD_{t})\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{n}\mathscr{E}_{n}^{\;p}\mathscr{E}_{p}^{\;q}\mathscr{E}_{qm}+q_{n}(\theta)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+\left|(D_{\tau}+jD_{t})\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{m}\mathscr{E}_{mn}-e^{j\theta}(\cos(\theta)D_{\tau}+\sin(\theta)D_{t})\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{m}\mathscr{H}_{mn}+e^{j\theta}F_{t\tau}\mathscr{H}^{m}\mathscr{E}_{mn}+p_{n}(\theta)\right|^{2}+\dots\bigg)\,.

After suitable rescalings, we can recast (
5.6
) as a 2d model, where the action is
20
20

20

To arrive at the following expression, we have employed Stokes’ theorem and the fact that
M_{3}
has no boundary to omit terms with
\partial_{m}A_{\{t,\tau\}}
as they will vanish when integrated over
M_{3}
.

\displaystyle S_{\text{2d},\mathfrak{B}_{3}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}dtd\tau\,\bigg(\left|e^{j\theta}(\cos(\theta)D_{\tau}+\sin(\theta)D_{t})\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}+(D_{\tau}+jD_{t})\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}+q^{a}(\theta)\right|^{2}

(5.7)

\displaystyle+\left|(D_{\tau}+jD_{t})\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}-e^{j\theta}(\cos(\theta)D_{\tau}+\sin(\theta)D_{t})\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}+e^{j\theta}F_{t\tau}+p^{a}(\theta)\right|^{2}+\dots\bigg)\,.

Here,
(\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a},\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a})
and
a
are coordinates and indices on the space
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
of irreducible
(\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}_{m},\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}_{m})
fields on
M_{3}
, and

p^{a}=-je^{j\theta}(\widetilde{\mathscr{D}}\widetilde{\mathscr{B}})^{a}\,,\qquad q^{a}=je^{j\theta}\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}^{a}\,,

(5.8)

with (i)
(\widetilde{\mathscr{D}}\widetilde{\mathscr{B}})^{a}
and (ii)
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}^{a}
corresponding to (i)
\epsilon_{npq}\widetilde{\mathscr{D}}^{p}\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{q}
and (ii)
\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{npq}\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}^{pq}
, respectively, in the underlying 5d theory.
In other words, GM theory on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
can be regarded as a 2d gauged sigma model along the
(t,\tau)
-directions with target
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
and action (
5.7
).

The 2d Model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
with Target
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
as a 1d SQM

Singling out
\tau
as the direction in “time”, one can, after suitable rescalings, recast (
5.7
) as the following equivalent SQM action
21
21

21

In the resulting SQM, as
A_{\tau}
has no field strength and is thus non-dynamical, it will be integrated out to furnish the Christoffel connection for the fermions in the SQM
[
10
]
, leaving us with an SQM without
A_{\tau}
.
We have again applied Stokes’ theorem and the fact that the fields corresponding to the finite-energy real gauge field
A_{t}
would vanish at
t\rightarrow\pm\infty
, to arrive at the following expression.

S_{\text{SQM},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{B}_{3})}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int d\tau\left(\left|\frac{d\varphi_{+}^{\alpha}}{d\tau}+g^{\alpha\beta}_{\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{B}_{3})}\frac{\partial{h_{3}}}{\partial{\varphi_{+}^{\beta}}}\right|^{2}+\left|\frac{d\varphi_{-}^{\alpha}}{d\tau}+g^{\alpha\beta}_{\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{B}_{3})}\frac{\partial{h_{3}}}{\partial{\varphi_{-}^{\beta}}}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

(5.9)

where
\varphi_{+}^{\alpha}\coloneq e^{j\theta}\cos(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{\alpha}+\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{\alpha}
and
\varphi_{-}^{\alpha}\coloneq\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{\alpha}-e^{j\theta}\cos(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{\alpha}-e^{j\theta}(A_{\tau})^{\alpha}
.
Here,
(\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{\alpha},\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{\alpha},(A_{\tau})^{\alpha})
and
(\alpha,\beta)
are coordinates and indices on the path space
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{B}_{3})
of maps from
\mathbb{R}
to
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
with
(A_{\tau})^{\alpha}
corresponding to
A_{\tau}
in the underlying 2d model;

g_{\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{B}_{3})}^{\alpha\beta}
is the metric on
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{B}_{3})
;
and
h_{3}(\varphi_{+},\varphi_{-})
is the potential function.

In other words, GM theory on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
can also be regarded as a 1d SQM along
\tau
in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{B}_{3})
whose action is (
5.9
).

5.2
Non-constant Paths, Strings, and
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-BF Configurations

\theta
-deformed, Non-constant Paths in the SQM

The squaring argument
[
6
]
applied to (
5.9
) tells us that the equivalent SQM localizes onto configurations that set both the LHS and RHS of the expression within the squared term
simultaneously
to zero, i.e., the SQM localizes onto
\tau
-invariant critical points of
h_{3}(\varphi_{+},\varphi_{-})
that obey

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(e^{j\theta}\sin(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}+j\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}\right)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=-je^{j\theta}\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}^{\alpha}\,,

(5.10)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(j\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}-e^{j\theta}\sin(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}\right)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=je^{j\theta}(\widetilde{\mathscr{D}}\widetilde{\mathscr{B}})^{\alpha}\,.

These are
\tau
-invariant,
\theta
-deformed, non-constant
paths in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{B}_{3})
.

\mathfrak{B}^{\theta}_{3}
-strings in the 2d Gauged Model

By comparing (
5.9
) with (
5.7
), we find that such
\tau
-invariant,
\theta
-deformed, non-constant paths in the SQM defined by (
5.10
), will correspond, in the 2d gauged sigma model with target
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
, to configurations defined by

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(e^{j\theta}\sin(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}+j\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}\right)=

\displaystyle-\left[A_{t},e^{j\theta}\sin(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}+j\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}\right]_{\mathbb{H}}

(5.11)

\displaystyle-\left[A_{\tau},e^{j\theta}\cos(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}+\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}\right]_{\mathbb{H}}-q^{a}(\theta)\,,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(j\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}-e^{j\theta}\sin(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}+e^{j\theta}A_{\tau}\right)=

\displaystyle-\left[A_{t},j\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}-e^{j\theta}\sin(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}+e^{j\theta}A_{\tau}\right]_{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle-\left[A_{\tau},\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}-e^{j\theta}\cos(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}\right]_{\mathbb{H}}-p^{a}(\theta)\,.

These are
\tau
-invariant,
\theta
-deformed strings along the
t
-direction in the 2d gauged sigma model.
We shall henceforth refer to such strings as
\mathfrak{B}^{\theta}_{3}
-strings.

\tau
-independent,
\theta
-deformed GM Configurations in GM Theory

By comparing (
5.7
) with (
5.6
), we find that the 2d configurations defined by (
5.11
), will correspond, in GM theory, to 5d configurations defined by

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(e^{j\theta}\sin(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{m}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}_{mn}+j\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{n}\mathscr{E}_{n}^{\;p}\mathscr{E}_{p}^{\;q}\mathscr{E}_{qm}\right)

(5.12)

\displaystyle=-\left[A_{t},e^{j\theta}\sin(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{m}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}_{mn}+j\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{n}\mathscr{E}_{n}^{\;p}\mathscr{E}_{p}^{\;q}\mathscr{E}_{qm}\right]_{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle-[A_{\tau},e^{j\theta}\cos(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{m}\widetilde{\mathscr{H}}_{mn}+\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{n}\mathscr{E}_{n}^{\;p}\mathscr{E}_{p}^{\;q}\mathscr{E}_{qm}]_{\mathbb{H}}-q_{n}(\theta)\,,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(j\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{m}\mathscr{E}_{mn}-e^{j\theta}\sin(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{m}\mathscr{H}_{mn}+e^{j\theta}A_{\tau}\mathscr{H}^{m}\mathscr{E}_{mn}\right)

\displaystyle=-\left[A_{t},j\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{m}\mathscr{E}_{mn}-e^{j\theta}\sin(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{m}\mathscr{H}_{mn}+e^{j\theta}A_{\tau}\mathscr{H}^{m}\mathscr{E}_{mn}\right]_{\mathbb{H}}

\displaystyle-\left[A_{\tau},\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{m}\mathscr{E}_{mn}-e^{j\theta}\cos(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{m}\mathscr{H}_{mn}\right]_{\mathbb{H}}-p_{n}(\theta)\,.

These are
\tau
-independent,
\theta
-deformed GM configurations on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

GM Configurations,
\mathfrak{B}^{\theta}_{3}
-strings, and Non-constant Paths

In short, these
\tau
-independent,
\theta
-deformed GM configurations on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
that are defined by (
5.12
), will correspond to the
\mathfrak{B}^{\theta}_{3}
-strings defined by (
5.11
), which, in turn, will correspond to the
\tau
-invariant,
\theta
-deformed, non-constant paths in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{B}^{\theta}_{3})
defined by (
5.10
).

\mathfrak{B}^{\theta}_{3}
-string Endpoints Corresponding to
\theta
-deformed
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-BF Configurations on
M_{3}

Consider now the fixed endpoints of the
\mathfrak{B}^{\theta}_{3}
-strings at
t=\pm\infty
, where we also expect the finite-energy 2d gauge fields
A_{t},A_{\tau}
to decay to zero.
They are given by (
5.11
) with
\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}=0=\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}
and
A_{t},A_{\tau}\rightarrow 0
, i.e., (via (
5.8
))

e^{j\theta}(\widetilde{\mathscr{D}}\widetilde{\mathscr{B}})^{a}=0\,,\qquad e^{j\theta}\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}^{a}=0\,.

(5.13)

In turn, they will correspond, in GM theory, to
(t,\tau)
-independent,
\theta
-deformed configurations that obey

e^{j\theta}\epsilon_{npq}\widetilde{\mathscr{D}}^{n}\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{q}=0\,,\qquad e^{j\theta}\epsilon_{npq}\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}^{pq}=0\,.

(5.14)

Notice that (
5.14
) can also be obtained from (
5.12
) and (
5.5
) with
\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{m}=0=\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}_{mn}
and
A_{t},A_{\tau}\rightarrow 0
.

The equations in (
5.14
) are a
\theta
-deformed version
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-BF configurations on
M_{3}
.
At
\theta=0,\pi
, they become the regular
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-BF configurations on
M_{3}
.
We will also assume choices of
M_{3}
and
G
satisfying
footnote 17
whereby such configurations are isolated and non-degenerate.
22
22

22

At
\theta=0
, the moduli space of such configurations is the moduli space of undeformed
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-BF configurations on
M_{3}
.
For such a choice of
M_{3}
and
G
(where
G
is the gauge group that the underlying GM theory is originally defined with), this moduli space will be made of isolated and non-degenerate points.
Therefore, at
\theta=0
, the endpoints of the
\mathfrak{B}^{\theta}_{3}
-strings will be isolated and non-degenerate.
As the physical theory is symmetric under a variation of
\theta
, this observation about the endpoints of the
\mathfrak{B}^{\theta}_{3}
-strings will continue to hold true for any value of
\theta
.
Hence, the presumption that the moduli space of
\theta
-deformed
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-BF configurations on
M_{3}
will be made of isolated and non-degenerate points, is justified.

In short, from the equivalent 1d SQM of GM theory on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, the theory localizes onto
\tau
-invariant,
\theta
-deformed, non-constant paths in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{B}_{3})
, which, in turn, will correspond to
\mathfrak{B}^{\theta}_{3}
-strings in the 2d gauged sigma model whose endpoints correspond to
\theta
-deformed
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-BF configurations on
M_{3}
.

5.3
The 2d Model and an Open String Theory

Flow Lines of the SQM as BPS Worldsheets of the 2d Model

The classical trajectories or flow lines of the equivalent SQM are governed by the gradient flow equations (defined by setting to zero the expression within the squared terms in (
5.9
)), i.e.,

\frac{d\varphi_{+}^{\alpha}}{d\tau}=-g^{\alpha\beta}_{\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{B}_{3})}\frac{\partial{h_{3}}}{\partial{\varphi_{+}^{\beta}}}\,,\qquad\frac{d\varphi_{-}^{\alpha}}{d\tau}=-g^{\alpha\beta}_{\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{B}_{3})}\frac{\partial{h_{3}}}{\partial{\varphi_{-}^{\beta}}}\,,

(5.15)

and they go from one
\tau
-invariant critical point of
h_{3}
to another in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{B}_{3})
.
In the 2d gauged sigma model with target
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
, these flow lines will correspond to worldsheets that have, at
\tau=\pm\infty
,
\mathfrak{B}^{\theta}_{3}
-strings.
23
23

23

The
\mathfrak{B}^{\theta}_{3}
-string can translate in the
\tau
-direction due to its “center of mass” motion, and because it is
\tau
-invariant, it is effectively degenerate.
This reflects the fact that generically, each critical point of
h_{3}
is degenerate and does not correspond to a point but a
real line
in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{B}_{3})
.
Nonetheless, one can perturb
h_{3}
via the addition of physically-inconsequential
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-exact terms to the SQM action, and collapse the degeneracy such that the critical points really correspond to points in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{B}_{3})
.
This is equivalent to factoring out the center of mass degree of freedom of the
\mathfrak{B}^{\theta}_{3}
-string, and fixing it at
\tau=\pm\infty
.

These strings shall be denoted as
\gamma_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{3})
, and are defined by (
5.11
) with finite-energy
real
gauge fields
A_{t},A_{\tau}\rightarrow 0
, i.e.,

e^{j\theta}\sin(\theta)\frac{d\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}}{dt}+j\frac{d\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}}{dt}=-q^{a}(\theta)\,,\qquad j\frac{d\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}}{dt}-e^{j\theta}\sin(\theta)\frac{d\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}}{dt}=-p^{a}(\theta)\,.

(5.16)

Their endpoints
\gamma(\pm\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{3})
at
t=\pm\infty
are defined by

q^{a}(\theta)=0\,,\qquad p^{a}(\theta)=0\,,

(5.17)

which is simply (
5.16
) with
d_{t}\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}=0=d_{t}\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}
.

Note that the gradient flow equations which govern the flow lines are actually the BPS equations of the 1d SQM.
This means that the worldsheets which the flow lines will correspond to are governed by the BPS equations of the equivalent 2d gauged sigma model with target
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
.
They are defined by setting to zero the expression within the squared terms in (
5.7
), i.e., (via (
5.8
))

\displaystyle\frac{D}{D\tau}\left(e^{j\theta}\cos(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}+\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}\right)+\frac{D}{Dt}\left(e^{j\theta}\sin(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}+j\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}\right)

\displaystyle=-je^{j\theta}\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}^{a}\,,

(5.18)

\displaystyle\frac{D}{D\tau}\left(\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}-e^{j\theta}\cos(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}\right)+\frac{D}{Dt}\left(j\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}-e^{j\theta}\sin(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}\right)+e^{j\theta}F_{t\tau}

\displaystyle=je^{j\theta}(\widetilde{\mathscr{D}}\widetilde{\mathscr{B}})^{a}\,.

Such worldsheets which correspond to the classical trajectories of 2d gauged sigma models were coined as BPS worldsheets in
[
10
,
\lx@sectionsign
9.3]
; we shall do the same here.

In short, the BPS worldsheets of the 2d gauged sigma model with target
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
will be defined by (
5.18
).

BPS Worldsheets with Boundaries Corresponding to
\theta
-deformed
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-BF Configurations on
M_{3}

The boundaries of the BPS worldsheets are traced out by the endpoints of the
\mathfrak{B}^{\theta}_{3}
-strings as they propagate in
\tau
.
As we have seen at the end of

§

5.2
, at
\theta=0
, these endpoints correspond to
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-BF configurations on
M_{3}
.
If there are ‘
v
’ such configurations
\{\mathfrak{C}^{1}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}(0),\mathfrak{C}^{2}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}(0),\dots,\mathfrak{C}^{v}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}(0)\}
, we can further specify the undeformed
\mathfrak{B}^{0}_{3}
-strings at
\tau=\pm\infty
as
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,0,\mathfrak{B}_{3})
, where
I,J\in\{1,\dots,v\}
indicates that its left and right endpoints, given by
\gamma^{I}(-\infty,0,\mathfrak{B}_{3})
and
\gamma^{J}(+\infty,0,\mathfrak{B}_{3})
, would correspond to the configurations
\mathfrak{C}^{I}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}(0)
and
\mathfrak{C}^{J}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}(0)
, respectively.
As the physical theory is symmetric under a variation of
\theta
, this would be true at any value of
\theta
.
In other words, we can also further specify any
\mathfrak{B}^{\theta}_{3}
-string at
\tau=\pm\infty
as
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{3})
, where its left and right endpoints, given by
\gamma^{I}(-\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{3})
and
\gamma^{J}(+\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{3})
, would correspond to the configurations
\mathfrak{C}^{I}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}(\theta)
and
\mathfrak{C}^{J}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}(\theta)
, respectively, with the
\mathfrak{C}^{*}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}(\theta)
’s being the
v
number of
\theta
-deformed
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-BF configurations on
M_{3}
.

Since the
\mathfrak{C}^{*}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}(\theta)
’s are
\tau
-independent and therefore, have the same values for all
\tau
, we have BPS worldsheets of the kind shown in
Fig. 3
.

\gamma^{IJ}_{+}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{3})
\mathfrak{C}^{I}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}(\theta)
\gamma^{IJ}_{-}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{3})
\mathfrak{C}^{J}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}(\theta)
t
\tau

Figure 3
:
BPS worldsheet with strings
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{3})
and boundaries corresponding to
\mathfrak{C}^{I}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}(\theta)
and
\mathfrak{C}^{J}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}(\theta)
.

The 2d Model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
and an Open String Theory in
\mathfrak{B}_{3}

Hence, once can understand the 2d gauged sigma model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
with target
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
to define an open string theory in
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
with
effective
worldsheet and boundaries shown in
Fig. 3
, where
\tau
and
t
are the temporal and spatial directions, respectively.

5.4
String Theory, the GM Partition Function, and an Orlov-type
A_{\infty}
-1-category Categorifying the
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-flat Floer Homology of
M_{3}

The 2d Model as a 2d Gauged B-twisted LG Model

Notice that we can also express the action of the 2d gauged sigma model with target
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
in (
5.7
) as

\displaystyle S_{\text{2d-LG},\mathfrak{B}_{3}}

(5.19)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}dtd\tau\,\bigg(\left|\frac{D}{D\tau}\left(e^{j\theta}\cos(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}+\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}\right)+\frac{D}{Dt}\left(e^{j\theta}\sin(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}+j\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}\right)+je^{j\theta}\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}^{a}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+\left|\frac{D}{D\tau}\left(\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}-e^{j\theta}\cos(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}\right)+\frac{D}{Dt}\left(j\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}-e^{j\theta}\sin(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}\right)+e^{j\theta}F_{t\tau}-je^{j\theta}(\widetilde{\mathscr{D}}\widetilde{\mathscr{B}})^{a}\right|^{2}+\dots\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}dtd\tau\,\bigg(\left|\frac{D}{D\tau}\left(e^{j\theta}\cos(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}+\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}\right)+\frac{D}{Dt}\left(e^{j\theta}\sin(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}+j\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}\right)+je^{j\theta}g^{ab}_{\mathfrak{B}_{3}}\frac{\partial{V_{3}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{b}}}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+\left|\frac{D}{D\tau}\left(\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}-e^{j\theta}\cos(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}\right)+\frac{D}{Dt}\left(j\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}-e^{j\theta}\sin(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}\right)+e^{j\theta}F_{t\tau}+je^{j\theta}g^{ab}_{\mathfrak{B}_{3}}\frac{\partial{V_{3}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{b}}}\right|^{2}+\dots\bigg)\,,

where
V_{3}(\widetilde{\mathscr{A}},\widetilde{\mathscr{B}})
is given in (
4.19
).

Applying specific values of
\theta
(in particular, applying
\theta=0
,
\pi/2
,
\pi
, and
3\pi/2
) to the conditions within the squared terms of (
5.19
) whilst noting that the physical theory ought to be symmetric under a variation of
\theta
, we find that (
5.19
) can also be equivalently expressed as

S_{\text{2d-LG},\mathfrak{B}_{3}}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\bigg(\left|D\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}\right|^{2}+\left|D\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}\right|^{2}+\left|jF-g^{ab}_{\mathfrak{B}_{3}}\frac{\partial{V_{3}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{b}}}\right|^{2}+\left|g^{ab}_{\mathfrak{B}_{3}}\frac{\partial{V_{3}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{b}}}\right|^{2}+\dots\bigg)\,,

(5.20)

where
D
is the covariant derivative in the real 2d gauge fields, and
F
is field strength of the real 2d gauge fields.
In other words, the 2d gauged sigma model with target
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
can also be interpreted as a 2d gauged
B-twisted
Landau-Ginzburg (LG) model in
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
with holomorphic superpotential
V_{3}(\widetilde{\mathscr{A}},\widetilde{\mathscr{B}})
.
24
24

24

Note that the conditions that minimize the action (
5.20
) correspond to configurations that set to zero the expression within the squared terms therein.
These are
gauge-theoretic generalizations
of the conditions that minimize the action of a 2d B-twisted LG model with holomorphic superpotential
V_{3}(\widetilde{\mathscr{A}},\widetilde{\mathscr{B}})
, i.e.,
d\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}=0=d\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}
and
\partial V_{3}/\partial\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}=0=\partial V_{3}/\partial\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}

[
24
]
.

By setting
d_{\tau}\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}=0=d_{\tau}\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}
and
A_{t},A_{\tau}\rightarrow 0
in the expression within the squared terms in (
5.19
), we can read off the LG
\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-string equations corresponding to
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{3})
(that re-expresses (
5.16
)) as

e^{j\theta}\sin(\theta)\frac{d\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}}{dt}+j\frac{d\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}}{dt}=-je^{j\theta}g^{ab}_{\mathfrak{B}_{3}}\frac{\partial{V_{3}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{b}}}\,,\quad j\frac{d\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}}{dt}-e^{j\theta}\sin(\theta)\frac{d\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}}{dt}=-je^{j\theta}g^{ab}_{\mathfrak{B}_{3}}\frac{\partial{V_{3}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{b}}}\,.

(5.21)

By setting
d_{t}\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}=0=d_{t}\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}
in (
5.21
), we get the LG
\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-string endpoint equations corresponding to
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(\pm\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{3})
(that re-expresses (
5.17
)) as

je^{j\theta}g^{ab}_{\mathfrak{B}_{3}}\frac{\partial{V_{3}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{b}}}=0\,,\qquad je^{j\theta}g^{ab}_{\mathfrak{B}_{3}}\frac{\partial{V_{3}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{b}}}=0\,.

(5.22)

Recall from the end of

§

5.2
that we are only considering
M_{3}
and
G
such that the endpoints
\gamma^{IJ}(\pm\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{3})
are isolated and non-degenerate.
Therefore, from their definition in (
5.22
) as critical points of
V_{3}(\widetilde{\mathscr{A}},\widetilde{\mathscr{B}})
, we conclude that
V_{3}(\widetilde{\mathscr{A}},\widetilde{\mathscr{B}})
is a (holomorphic) Morse function in
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
.

The Gauged LG Model as an LG SQM

Last but not least, after suitable rescalings, we can recast (
5.19
) as a 1d LG SQM (that re-expresses (
5.9
)), where its action will be given by
25
25

25

Just as in
footnote 21
, we have integrated out
A_{\tau}
in the resulting SQM.

\displaystyle S_{\text{2d-LG SQM},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{B}_{3})}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int d\tau\left(\left|\frac{d\varphi_{+}^{\alpha}}{d\tau}+g^{\alpha\beta}_{\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{B}_{3})}\frac{\partial{H_{3}}}{\partial{\varphi_{+}^{\beta}}}\right|^{2}+\left|\frac{d\varphi_{-}^{\alpha}}{d\tau}+g^{\alpha\beta}_{\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{B}_{3})}\frac{\partial{H_{3}}}{\partial{\varphi_{-}^{\beta}}}\right|^{2}\right)^{2}\,,

(5.23)

where
H_{3}(\varphi_{+},\varphi_{-})
is the
real-valued
potential in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{B}_{3})
, with
\varphi_{+}
and
\varphi_{-}
defined as in

§

5.1
, and the subscript “2d-LG SQM,
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
” is to specify that it is a 1d SQM in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
obtained from the equivalent 2d LG model.
We will also refer to this
1d
LG SQM as “2d-LG SQM” in the rest of this section.

The 2d-LG SQM will localize onto configurations that
simultaneously
set to zero the LHS and RHS of the expression within the squared terms in (
5.23
).
In other words, it will localize onto
\tau
-invariant critical points of
H_{3}(\varphi_{+},\varphi_{-})
that will correspond to the LG
\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-strings defined by (
5.21
).
For our choice of
M_{3}
and
G
, just like their endpoints, the LG
\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-strings will be isolated and non-degenerate.
Thus,
H_{3}(\varphi_{+},\varphi_{-})
can be regarded as a real-valued Morse functional in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{B}_{3})
.

String Theory from the 2d Gauged LG Model

Just like the 2d gauged sigma model, the equivalent 2d gauged B-twisted LG model will define an open string theory in
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
with effective worldsheets and boundaries shown in
Fig. 3
, where
\tau
and
t
are the temporal and spatial directions, respectively.

The dynamics of this open string theory in
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
will be governed by the BPS worldsheet equations determined by setting to zero the expression within the squared terms in (
5.19
), where
(\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a},\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a})
are scalars on the worldsheet corresponding to the holomorphic coordinates of
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
.
At an arbitrary instant in time whence
d_{\tau}\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}=0=d_{\tau}\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}
and
d_{\tau}A_{t}=0
therein, the dynamics of
(\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a},\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a})
along
t
will be governed by the string equations

\displaystyle e^{j\theta}\sin(\theta)\frac{d\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}}{dt}+j\frac{d\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}}{dt}=

\displaystyle-[A_{\tau},e^{j\theta}\cos(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}+\widetilde{\mathscr{B}^{a}}]-[A_{t},e^{j\theta}\sin(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}+j\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}]

(5.24)

\displaystyle-je^{j\theta}g^{ab}_{\mathfrak{B}_{3}}\frac{\partial{V_{3}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{b}}}\,,

\displaystyle j\frac{d\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}}{dt}-e^{j\theta}\sin(\theta)\frac{d\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}}{dt}+e^{j\theta}\frac{dA_{\tau}}{dt}=

\displaystyle-[A_{\tau},\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}-e^{j\theta}\cos(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}]-[A_{t},j\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{a}-e^{j\theta}\sin(\theta)\widetilde{\mathscr{B}}^{a}+A_{\tau}]

\displaystyle-je^{j\theta}g^{ab}_{\mathfrak{B}_{3}}\frac{\partial{V_{3}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathscr{A}}^{b}}}\,.

Morphisms from
\mathfrak{C}^{I}_{G_{\mathbb{H}}\text{-BF}}(\theta)
to
\mathfrak{C}^{I}_{G_{\mathbb{H}}\text{-BF}}(\theta)
as Ext-groups

It will now be useful to describe the LG
\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-string endpoint solutions satisfying (
5.22
) as
singular
fibers of
V_{3}
over 0, which we shall henceforth denote as
V_{3,\text{sg}}^{-1}(0)
.
26
26

26

The fiber space
V_{3}^{-1}
over the
V_{3}
-plane is
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
, and the subset of singular fibers is a submanifold of
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
containing the critical points of
V_{3}
.
In our case where
V_{3}
is given by (
4.19
), its critical points will correspond to the value 0 in the
V_{3}
-plane, i.e., we will only have singular fibers over 0.

In particular, to an LG
\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-string
\gamma^{IJ}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{3})
(illustrated in
Fig. 3
), its left endpoint
\gamma^{I}(-\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{3})\eqcolon\mathcal{T}^{I}_{V_{3}}(\theta)\in V_{3,\text{sg}}^{-1}(0)
can be described as a singular fiber which is also a zero-dimensional submanifold of
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
, and it corresponds to a
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-BF configuration
\mathfrak{C}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}^{I}(\theta)
on
M_{3}
.
Likewise, its right endpoint
\gamma^{J}(+\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{3})\eqcolon\mathcal{T}^{J}_{V_{3}}(\theta)\in V_{3,\text{sg}}^{-1}(0)
can also be described as a singular fiber which is a zero-dimensional submanifold of
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
, and it corresponds to a
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-BF configuration
\mathfrak{C}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}^{J}(\theta)
on
M_{3}
.

Let us denote by
S^{IJ}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}
the collection of all such strings whose left and right endpoints correspond to
\mathfrak{C}^{I}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}(\theta)
and
\mathfrak{C}^{J}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}(\theta)
.
Each LG
\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-string pair
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{3})
will therefore correspond to a pair of strings
p^{IJ}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}},\pm}(\theta)\in S^{IJ}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}
.

As in

§

4
, the 2d-LG SQM in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{B}_{3})
with action (
5.23
) will physically realize a Floer homology that we shall name a
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
-2d-LG Floer homology.
The chains of the
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
-2d-LG Floer complex will be generated by LG
\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-strings which we can identify with
p^{**}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}},\pm}(\theta)
, and the
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
-2d-LG Floer differential will be realized by the flow lines governed by the gradient flow equations satisfied by
\tau
-varying configurations which set the expression within the squared terms in (
5.23
) to zero.
In particular, the SQM partition function of the 2d-LG SQM in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{B}_{3})
will be given by
27
27

27

The ‘
\theta
’ label is omitted in the LHS of the following expression, as the physical theory is actually equivalent for all values of
\theta
.
We will henceforth omit the ‘
\theta
’ label in equations wherever relevant.

\mathcal{Z}_{\text{2d-LG SQM,}\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{B}_{3})}(G)=\sum_{I\neq J}^{v}\;\sum_{p^{IJ}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}},\pm}(\theta)\in S^{IJ}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}}\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{v}}\left(p^{IJ}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}},\pm}(\theta)\right)\,,

(5.25)

where the contribution
\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{k}}(p^{IJ}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}},\pm}(\theta))
can be identified with a homology class in a
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
-2d-LG Floer homology generated by LG
\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-strings whose endpoints correspond to singular fibers
V_{3,\text{sg}}^{-1}(0)
.
The degree of each chain is
d_{v}
, and is counted by the number of outgoing flow lines from the fixed critical points of
H_{3}(\widetilde{\mathscr{A}},\widetilde{\mathscr{B}})
in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{B}_{3})
that can be identified as
p^{IJ}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}},\pm}(\theta)
.

Note that
p^{IJ}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}},\pm}(\theta)
, which corresponds to an LG
\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-string, will be defined by (i) (
5.21
) with (ii) endpoints (
5.22
).
In other words, we can write

p^{IJ}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}},\pm}(\theta)\Longleftrightarrow\text{Ext}\left(\mathcal{T}^{I}_{V_{3}}(\theta),\mathcal{T}^{J}_{V_{3}}(\theta)\right)_{\pm}\,,

(5.26)

where
\text{Ext}(\mathcal{T}^{I}_{V_{3}}(\theta),\mathcal{T}^{J}_{V_{3}}(\theta))_{\pm}
represents an LG
\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-string whose start and endpoints are described as singular fibers
\mathcal{T}^{I}_{V_{3}}(\theta)
and
\mathcal{T}^{J}_{V_{3}}(\theta)
, respectively.

Equivalently, it is a
\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-string defined by (i) (
5.11
) and
A_{t},A_{\tau}\rightarrow 0
with (ii) endpoints (
5.13
).
Note that a string can be regarded as a morphism between its endpoints.
Specifically, the
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{3})

\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-string pair can be regarded as a pair of morphisms
\text{Hom}(\mathfrak{C}^{I}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}(\theta),\mathfrak{C}^{J}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}(\theta))_{\pm}
from
\mathfrak{C}^{I}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}(\theta)
to
\mathfrak{C}^{J}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}(\theta)
.
Thus, we have the following one-to-one identification

\boxed{\text{Hom}\left(\mathfrak{C}^{I}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}},\mathfrak{C}^{J}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}\right)_{\pm}\Longleftrightarrow\text{Ext}\left(\mathcal{T}^{I}_{V_{3}},\mathcal{T}^{J}_{V_{3}}\right)_{\pm}}

(5.27)

Note that the RHS of (
5.27
) can be regarded as morphisms in an Orlov-type triangulated category of singularities of
V_{3}

[
19
]
, i.e., we can write

\text{Hom}\left(\mathfrak{C}^{I}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}},\mathfrak{C}^{J}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}\right)\Longleftrightarrow\text{Hom}_{\mathbf{D}_{\text{sg}}(V_{3}^{-1}(0))}\left(\mathcal{T}^{I}_{V_{3}}(\theta),\mathcal{T}^{J}_{V_{3}}(\theta)\right)\,.

(5.28)

The Normalized GM Partition Function, LG
\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-string Scattering, and Maps of an
A_{\infty}
-structure

The spectrum of GM theory is given by the
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-cohomology of operators.
In particular, its normalized 5d partition function will be a sum over free-field correlation functions of these operators (see
[
10
, footnote 37]
).
As our GM theory is semi-classical, these correlation functions will correspond to tree-level scattering only.
From the equivalent 2d-LG SQM and 2d gauged B-twisted LG model perspective, the
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-cohomology will be spanned by LG
\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-strings defined by (
5.21
).
In turn, this means that the normalized GM partition function can also be regarded as a sum over tree-level scattering amplitudes of these LG
\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-strings.
The BPS worldsheet underlying such a tree-level scattering amplitude is shown in
Fig. 4
.
28
28

28

Here, we have exploited the topological and hence conformal invariance of the string theory to replace the outgoing LG
\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-strings with their vertex operators on the disc, then used their coordinate-independent operator products to reduce them to a single vertex operator, before finally translating it back as a single outgoing LG
\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-string.

\mathfrak{C}^{I_{n_{k}+1}}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}
+
\mathfrak{C}^{I_{1}}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}
\mathfrak{C}^{I_{1}}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}
-
\mathfrak{C}^{I_{2}}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}
\mathfrak{C}^{I_{2}}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}
-
\mathfrak{C}^{I_{3}}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}
\mathfrak{C}^{I_{n_{k}}}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}
-
\mathfrak{C}^{I_{n_{k}+1}}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}

Figure 4
:
Tree-level scattering BPS worldsheet of incoming (
-
) and outgoing (
+
) LG
\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-strings.

In other words, we can express the normalized GM partition function as

\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{\text{GM},M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}(G)=\sum_{n_{v}}\mu^{n_{v}}_{\mathfrak{B}_{3}}\,,\qquad n_{v}\in\{1,2,\dots,v-1\}

(5.29)

where each

\boxed{\mu^{n_{v}}_{\mathfrak{B}_{3}}:\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n_{v}}\text{Hom}\left(\mathfrak{C}^{I_{i}}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}},\mathfrak{C}^{I_{i+1}}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}\right)_{-}\longrightarrow\text{Hom}\left(\mathfrak{C}^{I_{1}}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}},\mathfrak{C}^{I_{n_{v}+1}}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}\right)_{+}}

(5.30)

is a scattering amplitude of
n_{v}
incoming LG
\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-strings
\text{Hom}(\mathfrak{C}^{*}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}},\mathfrak{C}^{*}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}})_{-}
, and a single outgoing LG
\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-string
\text{Hom}(\mathfrak{C}^{I_{1}}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}},\mathfrak{C}^{I_{n_{v}+1}}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}})_{+}
, with left and right boundaries as labeled, whose underlying worldsheet is shown in
Fig. 4
.
That is,
\mu^{n_{v}}_{\mathfrak{B}_{3}}
counts constant discs with
n_{v}+1
punctures at the boundary that are mapped to
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
according to the BPS worldsheet equations (determined by setting to zero the expression within the squared terms in (
5.19
)) that are given by (
5.18
).

In turn, this means that
\mu^{n_{v}}_{\mathfrak{B}_{3}}
counts the moduli of solutions to (
5.4
) (or equivalently (
5.2
)) with
n_{v}+1
boundary conditions that can be described as follows.
First, note that we can regard
\mathbb{R}^{2}
as the effective worldsheet in
Fig. 4
that we shall denote as
\Omega
, so
M_{5}
can be interpreted as a trivial
M_{3}
fibration over
\Omega
.
Then, at the
n_{v}+1
LG
\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-strings on
\Omega
where
\tau=\pm\infty
, (
5.4
) will become (
5.12
) with
A_{t},A_{\tau}\rightarrow 0
,
and over the LG
\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-string boundaries on
\Omega
where
t=\pm\infty
, (
5.4
) will become (
5.14
) which defines
\theta
-deformed
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-BF configurations on
M_{3}
.

Note that the above description of the map
\mu^{n_{v}}_{\mathfrak{B}_{3}}
is similar to the definition of the map ‘
\mu^{d}
’ in
[
15
,
\lx@sectionsign 5
]
that our previous work in
[
10
]
physically realizes. Indeed, there is a mirror symmetry and Langlands duality which relates them, as we shall elucidate in

§

9
.

At any rate, the collection of
\mu^{n_{v}}_{\mathfrak{B}_{3}}
maps defined in (
5.30
) can be regarded as composition maps defining an
A_{\infty}
-structure.

An Orlov-type
A_{\infty}
-1-category of Three-Manifolds

Altogether, this means that the normalized partition function of GM theory on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
as expressed in (
5.29
), manifests a
novel
gauge-theoretic Orlov-type
A_{\infty}
-1-category whose (i) 1-objects are singular fibers
V_{3,\text{sg}}^{-1}(0)
corresponding to
\theta
-deformed
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-BF configurations on
M_{3}
, thereby categorifying the
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-flat Floer homology of
M_{3}
, (ii) 1-morphisms are
Ext
-groups corresponding to LG
\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-strings via the one-to-one identification (
5.27
), and (iii)
A_{\infty}
-structure is defined by the
\mu^{n_{v}}_{\mathfrak{B}_{3}}
maps (
5.30
).

5.5
An RW
A_{\infty}
-2-category of Complex-Lagrangian Branes

Let us now consider the case where
M_{3}
can be Heegaard split along a Heegaard surface
C
.
Via

§

3.3
with
M_{1}=\mathbb{R}
, we find that this GM theory on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
is equivalent to the 3d B-model, i.e., the 3d RW model, on
I\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
with target the
\theta
-generalized
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J})
, and with boundaries being complex-Lagrangian branes
\widehat{L}_{*}^{*}(\theta)
of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J})
.
Here
\theta
is the angle of rotation about the origin of the
\mathbb{R}^{2}
-plane as per our formulation in this section thus far.

An Orlov
A_{\infty}
-1-category of Branes in Path Space

In the same way that GM theory on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
is equivalent to a 2d model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
with target
\mathfrak{B}_{3}
, the 3d RW model on
I\times\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau}
with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J})
is also equivalent to a 2d B-model on
I\times R_{\tau}
with target the affine path space
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J}))
.
It is well known that such a model would physically realize the derived 1-category
\mathbf{D}\big(\text{coh}(\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J})))\big)
of coherent sheaves on
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J}))
.
In particular, the 1-objects are (complexes of these) coherent sheaves, corresponding to the branes
\mathscr{P}_{0}(\theta)
and
\mathscr{P}_{1}(\theta)
of
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J}))
at the start and end of the interval, respectively.
The 1-morphisms are Ext-groups amongst these 1-objects, corresponding to B-strings that start and end on
\mathscr{P}_{0}(\theta)
and
\mathscr{P}_{1}(\theta)
, respectively; we shall call these
\mathscr{P}^{\theta}
-strings.
This is illustrated in
Fig. 5
.

\mathscr{P}_{0}(\theta)
L_{0}^{I}(\theta)

-string
\mathscr{P}_{0}(\theta)
L^{\theta}
-string
L^{\theta}
-string
L_{1}^{I}(\theta)
L_{1}^{J}(\theta)
R_{t}
R_{\tau}
I

:
3d RW model on
I\times\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau}
physically realizing

\mathscr{P}_{\{0,1\}}(\theta)
,
and (b) 1-morphisms being

as well as (II) an RW 2-category with (i) 2-objects being complex-Lagrangian branes

,

\widehat{L}^{\theta}
-strings,
and (iv) 1-morphisms being
\mathscr{P}^{\theta}
-strings with edges being the 1-objects.

Since GM theory on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
and 3d RW theory on
I\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
are equivalent via a Heegaard split of
M_{3}=M_{3}^{\prime}\bigcup_{C}M_{3}^{\prime\prime}
, we have a correspondence between
\mathbf{D}^{b}\big(\text{coh}(\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J})))\big)
and the Orlov-type
A_{\infty}
-1-category from the previous subsection (derived from the aforementioned 2d model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
).
Thus, we can compare the two, and in doing so, we find that a
\mathscr{P}^{\theta}
-string would correspond to a
\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-string.
In other words, we have the one-to-one correspondence

\text{Hom}^{(1)}_{\mathbf{D}\big(\text{coh}(\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J})))\big)}\big(\mathscr{P}_{0}(\theta),\mathscr{P}_{1}(\theta)\big)\Longleftrightarrow\text{Hom}\left(\mathfrak{C}^{I}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}(\theta),\mathfrak{C}^{J}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}(\theta)\right)\,.

(5.31)

That is, we have
(i) 1-objects which are complex-Lagrangian branes
\mathscr{P}_{*}(\theta)
of
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J}))
that are related to
\{\mathfrak{C}^{1}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}(\theta),\dots,\mathfrak{C}^{v}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}(\theta)\}
which correspond to
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-BF configurations on
M_{3}
,
(ii) 1-morphisms which are
\mathscr{P}^{\theta}
-strings that are related to
\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-strings,
and (iii) an
A_{\infty}
-structure that is defined by applying (
5.31
) to (
5.30
).

In short, we have an Orlov
A_{\infty}
-1-category of branes in path space.

An RW
A_{\infty}
-2-category

Moreover, we can also interpret the
\mathscr{P}^{\theta}
-strings as open membranes states of the 3d B-model, which, in turn, can be interpreted as morphisms between open B-string states of two 2d B-models on
I\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau}
with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J})
and branes
\widehat{L}_{0}^{*}(\theta)
and
\widehat{L}_{1}^{*}(\theta)
at the start and end of the interval, respectively; we shall call these
\widehat{L}^{\theta}
-strings.
Since these
\widehat{L}^{\theta}
-strings can be regarded as 1-morphisms in
\mathbf{D}^{b}\big(\text{coh}(\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J}))\big)
, the
\mathscr{P}^{\theta}
-strings can then be regarded as 1-morphisms of a 2-category whose 1-objects are the
\widehat{L}^{\theta}
-strings.

In other words, the 3d RW model on
I\times\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}_{\tau}
with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J})
physically realizes a 2-category, that we shall name an RW 2-category, whose
(i) 2-objects are complex-Lagrangian branes
\widehat{L}_{*}^{*}(\theta)
,
(ii) 2-morphisms
\text{Hom}^{(2)}_{\text{RW}}\left(\text{Hom}\big(\widehat{L}_{0}^{I}(\theta),\widehat{L}_{0}^{J}(\theta)\big),\text{Hom}\big(\widehat{L}_{1}^{I}(\theta),\widehat{L}_{1}^{J}(\theta)\big)\right)
are
\mathscr{P}^{\theta}
-strings as open membranes with vertices being the 2-objects,
(iii) 1-objects are
\widehat{L}^{\theta}
-strings that can be represented as morphisms
\text{Hom}\big(\widehat{L}_{0}^{*}(\theta),\widehat{L}_{1}^{*}(\theta)\big)
between their endpoints,
and (iv) 1-morphisms
\text{Hom}^{(1)}_{\text{RW}}\big(\text{Hom}\big(\widehat{L}_{0}^{I}(\theta),

\widehat{L}_{1}^{I}(\theta)\big),\text{Hom}\big(\widehat{L}_{0}^{J}(\theta),\widehat{L}_{1}^{J}(\theta)\big)\big)
are
\mathscr{P}^{\theta}
-strings as open membranes with edges being the 1-objects.
This is illustrated in
Fig. 5
.

Once again, we can compare RW 2-category with the Orlov-type
A_{\infty}
-1-category from the previous subsection; we find that a
\mathscr{P}^{\theta}
-string would correspond to a
\mathfrak{B}_{3}^{\theta}
-string, and a
\widehat{L}^{\theta}
-string would correspond to a singular fiber
V_{3,\text{sg}}^{-1}(0)
.
In other words, we have the one-to-one correspondence

\text{Hom}^{(2)}_{\text{RW}}\left(\text{Hom}\big(\widehat{L}_{0}^{I}(\theta),\widehat{L}_{1}^{I}(\theta)\big),\text{Hom}\big(\widehat{L}_{0}^{J}(\theta),\widehat{L}_{1}^{J}(\theta)\big)\right)\Longleftrightarrow\text{Hom}\left(\mathfrak{C}^{I}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}(\theta),\mathfrak{C}^{J}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}(\theta)\right)\,.

(5.32)

That is, we have (i) 1-objects which are
\widehat{L}^{\theta}
-strings,
(ii) 1-morphisms which are
\mathscr{P}^{\theta}
-strings,
and (iii) an
A_{\infty}
-structure that is defined by applying (
5.32
) to (
5.30
).

In short, the RW 2-category is actually an
A_{\infty}
-2-category!

A KRS 2-category

Note that a 2-category of complex-Lagrangian branes realized by the 3d RW model was first constructed by KRS in
[
16
]
.
However, as the morphisms of this KRS 2-category are realized by surface and line defects, an
A_{\infty}
-structure cannot defined.
It is therefore different from our RW
A_{\infty}
-2-category.

Corresponding Categories

In summary, we have correspondences amongst
(i)
\text{Orlov}\left(\text{HF}^{\text{flat}}(M_{3},G_{\mathbb{H}})\right)
, a gauge-theoretic Orlov-type
A_{\infty}
-1-category of
\text{HF}^{\text{flat}}(M_{3},G_{\mathbb{H}})
,
(ii)
\text{RW}_{(\sigma)}\big(\widehat{L},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J})\big)
, an RW
A_{\infty}
-2-category of complex-Lagrangian branes
\widehat{L}(\theta)\subset\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J})
,
(iii)
\text{KRS}_{(\sigma)}\big(\widehat{L},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J})\big)
, a KRS 2-category of complex-Lagrangian branes
\widehat{L}(\theta)\subset\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J})
,
and (iv)
\text{Orlov}_{(\sigma)}\big(\mathscr{P},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J}))\big)
, an Orlov-type
A_{\infty}
-1-category of path space branes
\mathscr{P}(\theta)\subset\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J}))
:

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}}}}}}

(5.33)

A Physical Proof of Doan-Rezchikov’s Mathematical Conjecture

Note that a correspondence between a KRS 2-category of complex-Lagrangian branes in a hyperkähler manifold
X
and an Orlov-type
A_{\infty}
-1-category of branes in the path space
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},X)
, was conjectured by Doan-Rezchikov (DR) in
[
9
,
\lx@sectionsign
2.6]
.
Since
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J})
is hyperkähler, this correspondence is exactly what we have in the bottom line of (
5.33
) for
X=\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J})
.

Thus, we have furnished a purely physical proof of their mathematical conjecture!

An Atiyah-Floer-type Correspondence between
A_{\infty}
-categories

Note that the Atiyah-Floer (AF) correspondence between a gauge-theoretic
G
-instanton Floer homology of
M_{3}
and a symplectic intersection Floer homology of Lagrangian branes in
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{flat}}(C)
, is actually a correspondence between (0-)categories defined via a Heegaard split of
M_{3}=M_{3}^{\prime}\bigcup_{C}M_{3}^{\prime\prime}
. Since (
5.33
) involves correspondences between a gauge-theoretic category and symplectic categories via a Heegaard split of
M_{3}=M_{3}^{\prime}\bigcup_{C}M_{3}^{\prime\prime}
, we can thus regard them as AF-type correspondences.

In other words, we have
novel
AF-type correspondences between the gauge-theoretic Orlov-type
A_{\infty}
-1-category of
\text{HF}^{\text{flat}}(M_{3},G_{\mathbb{H}})
and
(i) the RW
A_{\infty}
-2-category of complex-Lagrangian branes in symplectic
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J})
,
(ii) the KRS 2-category of complex-Lagrangian branes in symplectic
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J})
,
and (iii) the Orlov-type
A_{\infty}
-1-category of branes in symplectic
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J}))
!

A Novel RW-type
A_{\infty}
-2-category of Two-Manifolds

In this section, we will study GM theory on
M_{5}=M_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, where
M_{2}
is a closed two-manifold.
We will recast it as a 3d gauged B-twisted LG model on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, a 2d gauged B-twisted LG model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and a 1d LG SQM.
Following what we did in

§

5
, we will, via the 5d GM partition function and its equivalent 2d gauged B-twisted LG model, be able to physically realize an Orlov-type
A_{\infty}
-1-category of strings whose endpoints correspond to
G_{\mathbb{O}}
-BF configurations on
M_{2}
that generate the
G_{\mathbb{O}}
-flat Floer homology of
M_{2}
from

§

4.3
.
Next, following what we did in our previous work
[
12
]
, we will, via the 5d GM partition function and its equivalent 3d gauged B-twisted LG model, be able to also physically realize a novel gauge-theoretic RW-type
A_{\infty}
-2-category that 2-categorifies the aforementioned
G_{\mathbb{O}}
-flat Floer homology of
M_{2}
.

6.1
GM Theory on
M_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
as a 3d Model on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, 2d Model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, or 1d SQM

Let
t,\tau,\xi
be the coordinates along
\mathbb{R}^{3}
.
The action of this theory is given by

S_{\text{GM}}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{M_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}dtd\tau d\xi d^{2}x\,\mathrm{Tr}\bigg(L_{1}+L_{2}+\dots\bigg)\,,

(6.1)

where

\displaystyle L_{1}

\displaystyle=\left|F_{t\tau}-iD_{t}\phi_{\tau}+iD_{\tau}\phi_{t}-[\phi_{t},\phi_{\tau}]\right|^{2}+\left|F_{t\xi}-iD_{t}\phi_{\xi}+iD_{\xi}\phi_{t}-[\phi_{t},\phi_{\xi}]\right|^{2}

(6.2)

\displaystyle+\left|F_{\tau\xi}-iD_{\tau}\phi_{\xi}+iD_{\xi}\phi_{\tau}-[\phi_{\tau},\phi_{\xi}]\right|^{2}\,,

and

\displaystyle L_{2}

\displaystyle=\left|D_{t}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{M}-\partial_{M}A_{t}+i\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{M}\phi_{t}\right|^{2}+\left|D_{\tau}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{M}-\partial_{M}A_{\tau}+i\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{M}\phi_{\tau}\right|^{2}

(6.3)

\displaystyle+\left|D_{\xi}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{M}-\partial_{M}A_{\xi}+i\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{M}\phi_{\xi}\right|^{2}+\left|\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{MN}\right|^{2}\,.

GM Theory as a 3d Model

We now want to recast GM theory as a 3d model on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
.
To this end, first note that the conditions that minimize the action (
6.1
) can be octonionified in a similar manner as was done for the BPS equations in

§

4.3
.
Using the same notation for imaginary numbers as was used there, the octonionified conditions are

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle(D_{\xi}+\mathbf{e}_{0}D_{t}+\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}D_{\tau})\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{N}\mathsf{E}_{NM}+(D_{\xi}+\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}D_{\tau})\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}\mathsf{H}^{N}\mathsf{E}_{NM}+(F_{t\xi}+\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}F_{t\tau})\mathsf{H}^{N}\mathsf{E}_{NM}

(6.4)

\displaystyle-\partial^{N}(A_{\xi}+\mathbf{e}_{0}A_{t}+\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}A_{\tau})\mathsf{E}_{NM}-\mathbf{e}_{0}\epsilon_{MN}\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{N}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}\,,

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle(D_{\xi}+\mathbf{e}_{0}D_{t}+\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}D_{\tau})\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}\mathsf{E}^{M}_{\;N}\mathsf{E}^{N}_{\;P}\mathsf{E}^{P}_{\;M}-(D_{\xi}+\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}D_{\tau})\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{M}\widetilde{\mathsf{H}}_{M}+\partial^{M}(A_{\xi}+\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}A_{\tau})\widetilde{\mathsf{H}}_{M}

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathbf{e}_{0}}{2}\epsilon_{MN}\widetilde{\mathsf{F}}^{MN}\,,

where
(i)
\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{j}\coloneq\mathbf{e}_{j}-\mathbf{e}_{0j}
,
(ii)
\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}_{M}\coloneq\widetilde{\mathscr{A}_{M}}-(\mathbf{e}_{1}+\mathbf{e}_{3})\phi_{\tau}\mathsf{H}_{M}
,
(ii)
\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}\coloneq\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{e}_{01}\phi_{t}+\mathbf{e}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{3}\phi_{\tau}+\mathbf{e}_{1}\phi_{\xi})\epsilon_{MN}\mathsf{E}^{MN}
,
and (iii)
\widetilde{\mathsf{H}}_{M}=\epsilon_{MN}\mathsf{H}^{N}
.
29
29

29

Note that when compared to the fields in

§

4.3
,
\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}_{M}=\overline{\mathsf{A}}_{M}+(\mathbf{e}_{0}A_{\xi}+\mathbf{e}_{02}A_{\tau})\mathsf{H}_{M}
and
\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}=\overline{\mathsf{B}}+\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{MN}(\mathbf{e}_{0}A_{\xi}+\mathbf{e}_{02}A_{\tau})\mathsf{E}_{MN}
.
These
\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}
and
\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}
fields are used in this section as we want to make explicit the real gauge fields of
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, i.e.,
A_{t}
,
A_{\tau}
, and
A_{\xi}
, in the equations.

Next, note that we are physically free to rotate the
(\xi,t)
-subplane of
\mathbb{R}^{3}
about the origin by an angle
\theta
, whence (
6.4
) becomes
X_{M}=0
and
Y=0
, where

\displaystyle X_{M}=

\displaystyle(D_{\xi}+\mathbf{e}_{0}D_{t}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}D_{\tau})\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{N}\mathsf{E}_{NM}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\left(\cos(\theta)D_{\xi}+\sin(\theta)D_{t}+\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}D_{\tau}\right)\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}\mathsf{H}^{N}\mathsf{E}_{NM}

(6.5)

\displaystyle+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\left(F_{t\xi}+\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}(\cos(\theta)F_{t\tau}-\sin(\theta)F_{\xi\tau})\right)\mathsf{H}^{N}\mathsf{E}_{NM}+P_{M}(\theta)\,,

\displaystyle Y=

\displaystyle(D_{\xi}+\mathbf{e}_{0}D_{t}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}D_{\tau})\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}\mathsf{E}^{M}_{\;N}\mathsf{E}^{N}_{\;P}\mathsf{E}^{P}_{\;M}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}(\cos(\theta)D_{\xi}+\sin(\theta)D_{t}+\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}D_{\tau})\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{M}\widetilde{\mathsf{H}}_{M}+Q(\theta)\,,

and

\displaystyle P_{M}(\theta)

\displaystyle=-\partial^{N}(A_{\xi}+\mathbf{e}_{0}A_{t}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}A_{\tau})\mathsf{E}_{NM}-\mathbf{e}_{0}e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\epsilon_{MN}\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{N}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}\,,

(6.6)

\displaystyle Q(\theta)

\displaystyle=e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\partial^{M}(A_{\xi}\cos(\theta)+A_{t}\sin(\theta)+\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}A_{\tau})\widetilde{\mathsf{H}}_{M}+\frac{\mathbf{e}_{0}e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}}{2}\epsilon_{MN}\widetilde{\mathsf{F}}^{MN}\,.

Thus, we can also write (
6.1
) as

S_{\text{GM}}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{M_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}dtd\tau d\xi d^{2}x\,\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left|X_{M}\right|^{2}+\left|Y\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,.

(6.7)

Lastly, after suitable rescalings, we can recast (
6.7
) as a 3d model, where the action is
30
30

30

To arrive at the following expression, we have employed the fact that
M_{2}
has no boundary to omit terms with
\partial_{M}A_{\{t,\tau,\xi\}}
as they will vanish when integrated over
M_{2}
.

S_{\text{3d},\mathfrak{B}_{2}}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dtd\tau d\xi\,\left(\left|X^{a}\right|^{2}+\left|Y^{a}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

(6.8)

where

\displaystyle X^{a}=

\displaystyle(D_{\xi}+\mathbf{e}_{0}D_{t}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}D_{\tau})\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\left(\cos(\theta)D_{\xi}+\sin(\theta)D_{t}+\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}D_{\tau}\right)\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}

(6.9)

\displaystyle+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\left(F_{t\xi}+\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}(\cos(\theta)F_{t\tau}-\sin(\theta)F_{\xi\tau})\right)+P^{a}(\theta)\,,

\displaystyle Y^{a}=

\displaystyle(D_{\xi}+\mathbf{e}_{0}D_{t}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}D_{\tau})\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}(\cos(\theta)D_{\xi}+\sin(\theta)D_{t}+\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}D_{\tau})\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}+Q^{a}(\theta)\,,

and

P^{a}(\theta)=-\mathbf{e}_{0}e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}(\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}})^{a}\,,\qquad Q^{a}(\theta)=\mathbf{e}_{0}e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{F}}^{a}\,.

(6.10)

Here,
(\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a},\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a})
and
a
are coordinates and indices on the space
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
of irreducible
(\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}_{M},\widetilde{\mathsf{B}})
fields on
M_{2}
, with (i)
(\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}})^{a}
and (ii)
\widetilde{\mathsf{F}}^{a}
corresponding to (i)
\epsilon_{MN}\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}^{N}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}
and (ii)
\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{MN}\widetilde{\mathsf{F}}^{MN}
, respectively, in the underlying 5d theory.

In other words, GM theory on
M_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
can be regarded as a 3d gauged sigma model along the
(t,\tau,\xi)
-directions with target
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
and action (
6.8
).

GM Theory as a 2d Model

Note that (
6.8
), after suitable rescalings, can be recast as the following equivalent 2d model action
31
31

31

To arrive at the following expression, we have employed Stokes’ theorem and the fact that the finite-energy 3d real gauge fields
A_{\{t,\tau,\xi\}}
would vanish at
\tau\rightarrow\pm\infty
.

S_{\text{2d},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}dtd\xi\left(\left|X^{u}\right|^{2}+\left|Y^{u}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

(6.11)

where

\displaystyle X^{u}=

\displaystyle(D_{\xi}+\mathbf{e}_{0}D_{t})\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{u}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\left(\cos(\theta)D_{\xi}+\sin(\theta)D_{t}\right)\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{u}

(6.12)

\displaystyle+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\left(F_{t\xi}+\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}(\cos(\theta)D_{t}-\sin(\theta)D_{\xi})(\mathring{A}_{\tau})^{u}\right)+p^{u}(\theta)+P^{u}(\theta)\,,

\displaystyle Y^{u}=

\displaystyle(D_{\xi}+\mathbf{e}_{0}D_{t})\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{u}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}(\cos(\theta)D_{\xi}+\sin(\theta)D_{t})\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{u}+q^{u}(\theta)+Q^{u}(\theta)\,.

Here,
(\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{u},\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{u},(\mathring{A}_{\tau})^{u})
and
u
are coordinates and indices on the path space
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
of maps from
\mathbb{R}_{\tau}
to
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
, and

\displaystyle p^{u}(\theta)

\displaystyle=e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}(\mathring{D}_{\tau}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}+\mathring{D}_{\tau}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}})^{u}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad q^{u}(\theta)

\displaystyle=e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}(\mathring{D}_{\tau}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}-\mathring{D}_{\tau}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}})^{u}\,,

(6.13)

\displaystyle P^{u}(\theta)

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{e}_{0}e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}(\widetilde{\mathsf{D}}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}})^{a}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad Q^{u}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{e}_{0}e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{F}}^{u}\,,

with
(\mathring{A}_{\tau},\mathring{D}_{\tau})
in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
corresponding to
(A_{\tau},D_{\tau})
in the underlying 3d model.

In other words, GM theory on
M_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
can also be regarded as a 2d gauged sigma model along the
(t,\xi)
-directions with target
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
and action (
6.11
).

GM Theory as a 1d SQM

Singling out
\xi
as the direction in “time”, one can, after suitable rescalings, recast (
6.11
) as the following equivalent SQM action
32
32

32

The resulting SQM will not involve the non-dynamical gauge field
A_{\xi}
for the same reason as explained in
footnote 21
(where there, the non-dynamical gauge field was
A_{\tau}
).
We have again applied Stokes’ theorem and the fact that the fields corresponding to the finite-energy real gauge fields
A_{\{t,\tau\}}
would vanish at
t\rightarrow\pm\infty
, to arrive at the following expression.

\displaystyle S_{\text{SQM},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2}))}

(6.14)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int d\xi\left(\left|\partial_{\xi}\varphi_{+}^{\alpha}+g^{\alpha\beta}_{\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2}))}\frac{\partial{h_{2}}}{\partial{\varphi_{+}^{\beta}}}\right|^{2}+\left|\partial_{\xi}\varphi_{-}^{\alpha}+g^{\alpha\beta}_{\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2}))}\frac{\partial{h_{2}}}{\partial{\varphi_{-}^{\beta}}}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

where
(\varphi_{+}^{\alpha},\varphi_{-}^{\alpha})
and
(\alpha,\beta)
are coordinates and indices on the path space
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2}))
of maps from
\mathbb{R}_{t}
to
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
with
(i)
\varphi_{+}^{\alpha}\coloneq\big(\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\big(\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}\cos(\theta)-\mathring{A}_{t}-\mathring{A}_{\tau}\sin(\theta)\big)\big)^{\alpha}

and (ii)
\varphi_{-}^{\alpha}\coloneq\big(\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}\cos(\theta)\big)^{\alpha}

corresponding to
(i)
\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{u}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\big(\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{u}\cos(\theta)-A_{t}-(\mathring{A}_{\tau})^{u}\sin(\theta)\big)

and (ii)
\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{u}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}\cos(\theta)

in the underlying 2d model,
and to (i)
\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\big(\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}\cos(\theta)-A_{t}-A_{\tau}\sin(\theta)\big)

and (ii)
\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}\cos(\theta)

in the underlying 3d model;

g_{\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2}))}
is the metric on
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2}))
;
and
h_{2}(\varphi_{+},\varphi_{-})
is the SQM potential function.
Note that we can also interpret
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2}))
as the double path space
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
of maps from
\mathbb{R}^{2}
to
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
.

In short, GM theory on
M_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
can also be regarded as a 1d SQM along the
\xi
-direction in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
whose action is (
6.14
).

6.2
Non-constant Double Paths, Strings, Membranes, and
G_{\mathbb{O}}
-BF Configurations on
M_{2}

\theta
-deformed, Non-constant Double Paths in the SQM

Applying the squaring argument
[
6
]
to (
6.14
), we find that the path integral of the equivalent SQM will localize onto configurations that set the LHS and RHS of the expression within the squared terms therein
simultaneously
to zero, i.e., the SQM path integral localizes onto
\xi
-invariant
critical points of
h_{2}(\varphi_{+},\varphi_{-})
that obey

\displaystyle\left\{\mathring{\partial}_{t}\left(\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}\sin(\theta)+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\mathring{A}_{\tau}\cos(\theta)\right)\right\}^{\alpha}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\left\{\mathring{\partial}_{\tau}\left(\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}+\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}\right)\right\}^{\alpha}

(6.15)

\displaystyle=-\left(\left[\mathring{A}_{t},\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}\sin(\theta)+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\mathring{A}_{\tau}\cos(\theta)\right]\right)^{\alpha}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\left(\left[\mathring{A}_{\tau},\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}+\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}\right]\right)^{\alpha}-P^{\alpha}(\theta)\,,

\displaystyle\left\{\mathring{\partial}_{t}\left(\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}\sin(\theta)\right)\right\}^{\alpha}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\left\{\mathring{\partial}_{\tau}\left(\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}-\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}\right)\right\}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=-\left(\left[\mathring{A}_{t},\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}\sin(\theta)\right]\right)^{\alpha}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\left(\left[\mathring{A}_{\tau},\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}-\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}\right]\right)^{\alpha}-Q^{\alpha}(\theta)\,.

These are
\xi
-invariant,
\theta
-deformed, non-constant double paths in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2}))
.

\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-strings in the 2d Gauged Model

By comparing (
6.14
) with (
6.11
), we find that such
\xi
-invariant,
\theta
-deformed, non-constant double paths in the SQM defined by (
6.15
), will correspond, in the 2d gauged sigma model with target
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
, to configurations defined by

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}\sin(\theta)+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\mathring{A}_{\tau}\cos(\theta)\right)^{u}

\displaystyle=-\left[A_{t},\left(\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}\sin(\theta)+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\mathring{A}_{\tau}\cos(\theta)\right)^{u}\right]

(6.16)

\displaystyle-\left[A_{\xi},\left(\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\left(\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}\cos(\theta)-A_{t}-\mathring{A}_{\tau}\sin(\theta)\right)\right)^{u}\right]

\displaystyle-p^{u}(\theta)-P^{u}(\theta)\,,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}\sin(\theta)\right)^{u}

\displaystyle=-\left[A_{t},\left(\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}\sin(\theta)\right)^{u}\right]-q^{u}(\theta)-Q^{u}(\theta)\,.

These are
\xi
-invariant,
\theta
-deformed strings along the
t
-direction of the 2d gauged sigma model with target
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
.
We shall henceforth refer to such strings as
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-strings.

\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-membranes in the 3d Gauged Model

By further comparing (
6.11
) with (
6.8
), we find that such
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-strings in the 2d gauged sigma model defined by (
6.16
), will correspond, in the 3d gauged sigma model with target
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
, to configurations defined by

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\left(\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}\sin(\theta)+A_{\xi}+\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}A_{\tau}\cos(\theta)\right)\right)+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\partial_{\tau}\left(\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}+\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}-\left(A_{t}\cos(\theta)-A_{\xi}\sin(\theta)\right)\right)

(6.17)

\displaystyle=-\left[A_{t},\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\left(\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}\sin(\theta)+A_{\xi}\right)\right]-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\left[A_{\tau},\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}+\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}-\left(A_{t}\cos(\theta)-A_{\xi}\sin(\theta)\right)\right]

\displaystyle-\left[A_{\xi},\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}\cos(\theta)\right]-P^{a}(\theta)\,,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\left(\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}\sin(\theta)\right)+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\partial_{\tau}\left(\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}-\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}\right)

\displaystyle=-\left[A_{t},\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}\sin(\theta)\right]-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\partial_{\tau}\left[A_{\tau},\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}-\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}\right]-\left[A_{\xi},\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}\cos(\theta)\right]-Q^{a}(\theta)\,.

These are
\xi
-invariant,
\theta
-deformed membranes along the
(t,\tau)
-directions in the 3d gauged sigma model with target
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
.
We shall henceforth refer to such membranes as
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-membranes.

\xi
-independent,
\theta
-deformed, GM Configurations in GM Theory

By comparing (
6.8
) with (
6.7
), we find that the 3d configurations defined by (
6.17
) will correspond, in GM theory, to 5d configurations defined by

\displaystyle\Big(\mathbf{e}_{0}\partial_{t}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\partial_{\tau}\Big)\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{N}\mathsf{E}_{NM}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\Big(\sin(\theta)\partial_{t}+\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\partial_{\tau}\Big)\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}\mathsf{H}^{N}\mathsf{E}_{NM}

(6.18)

\displaystyle+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\Big(\partial_{t}A_{\xi}+\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\big(\partial_{t}A_{\tau}\cos(\theta)-\partial_{\tau}A_{t}\cos(\theta)+\partial_{\tau}A_{\xi}\sin(\theta)\big)\Big)\mathsf{H}^{N}\mathsf{E}_{NM}

\displaystyle=-\left[A_{\xi}+\mathbf{e}_{0}A_{t}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}A_{\tau},\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{N}\mathsf{E}_{NM}\right]-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\left[A_{\xi}\cos(\theta)+A_{t}\sin(\theta)+\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}A_{\tau},\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}\mathsf{H}^{N}\mathsf{E}_{NM}\right]

\displaystyle-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\left([A_{t},A_{\xi}]+\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}[A_{t}\cos(\theta)-A_{\xi}\sin(\theta),A_{\tau}]\right)\mathsf{H}^{N}\mathsf{E}_{NM}-P_{M}(\theta)\,,

and

\displaystyle\Big(\mathbf{e}_{0}\partial_{t}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\partial_{\tau}\Big)\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}\mathsf{E}^{M}_{\;N}\mathsf{E}^{N}_{\;P}\mathsf{E}^{P}_{\;M}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\Big(\sin(\theta)\partial_{t}+\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\partial_{\tau}\Big)\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{M}\widetilde{\mathsf{H}}_{M}

(6.19)

\displaystyle=-\left[A_{\xi}+\mathbf{e}_{0}A_{t}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}A_{\tau},\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}\mathsf{E}^{M}_{\;N}\mathsf{E}^{N}_{\;P}\mathsf{E}^{P}_{\;M}\right]+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\left[A_{\xi}\cos(\theta)+A_{t}\sin(\theta)+\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}A_{\tau},\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{M}\widetilde{\mathsf{H}}_{M}\right]-Q(\theta)\,.

These are
\xi
-independent,
\theta
-deformed GM configurations on
M_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

GM Configurations,
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-membranes,
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})

\xi

-deformed GM configurations on

) and (
6.19

).

\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-string Endpoints Corresponding to Non-constant Paths

Consider now the endpoints of the
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-strings at

, where we also expect the fields in the 2d gauged sigma model corresponding to the finite-energy 3d gauge fields

,

(6.20)

These are
(\xi,t)

\theta
-deformed, non-constant paths in

.

\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}

6.3
The 2d Model and Open Strings, the 3d Model and Open Membranes

By following the same analysis in

§

5.3
, we find that the 2d gauged sigma model with target
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
whose action is (
6.11
), will define an open
string
theory in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
.
Likewise, we find that the 3d gauged sigma model with target
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
whose action is (
6.8
), will define an open
membrane
theory in
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
.
We will now work out the details of the BPS worldsheets and their 3d BPS worldvolume analogues that would be necessary to define this open string and open membrane theory, respectively.

BPS Worldsheets of the 2d Model

The BPS worldsheets of the 2d gauged sigma model with target
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
correspond to its classical trajectories.
Specifically, these are defined by setting to zero the expression within the squared terms in (
6.11
), i.e.,

\displaystyle-p^{u}-P^{u}=

\displaystyle\frac{D}{D\xi}\left(\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{u}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{u}\cos(\theta)-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}(\mathring{A}_{\tau})^{u}\sin(\theta)\right)

(6.24)

\displaystyle+\frac{D}{Dt}\left(\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{u}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{u}\sin(\theta)+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}(\mathring{A}_{\tau})^{u}\cos(\theta)\right)+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}F_{t\xi}\,,

\displaystyle-q^{u}-Q^{u}=

\displaystyle\frac{D}{D\xi}\left(\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{u}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{u}\cos(\theta)\right)+\frac{D}{Dt}\left(\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{u}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{u}\sin(\theta)\right)\,.

BPS Worldsheets with Boundaries Labeled by Non-constant Paths in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})

The boundaries of the BPS worldsheets are traced out by the endpoints of the
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-strings as they propagate in
\xi
; we shall, at
\xi=\pm\infty
, denote such
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-strings as
\Sigma_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
.
As we have seen at the end of

§

6.2
, these endpoints correspond to
(\xi,t)
-invariant,
\theta
-deformed, non-constant paths in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
that we shall denote as
\Gamma(\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
.
In turn, they will correspond, in the 3d gauged sigma model with target
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
, to
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-strings that we shall denote as
\gamma(\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
, whose endpoints will correspond to
\theta
-deformed
G_{\mathbb{O}}
-BF configurations on
M_{2}
.

If there are
w\geq 4
such configurations,
\{\mathfrak{C}^{1}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}(\theta),\mathfrak{C}^{2}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}(\theta),\dots,\mathfrak{C}^{w}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}(\theta)\}
, just as in

§

5.3
, we can further specify any
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-string as
\gamma^{IJ}(\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
, where its start and end would correspond to
\mathfrak{C}^{I}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}(\theta)
and
\mathfrak{C}^{J}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}(\theta)
, respectively.
Consequently, in the 2d gauged sigma model, we can further specify any
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-string endpoint
\Gamma(\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
as
\Gamma^{IJ}(\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
, where the latter will correspond to a
\gamma^{IJ}(\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})

\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-string in the equivalent 3d gauged sigma model.

Since the endpoints of a
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-string are now denoted as
\Gamma^{**}(\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
, we can also denote and specify the former at
\xi=\pm\infty
as
\Sigma^{IJ,KL}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{3})
,

\mathfrak{B}_{2}
will correspond to its classical trajectories.

6.8
), i.e.,

\displaystyle-P^{a}(\theta)=

\displaystyle\frac{D}{D\xi}\left(\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}\cos(\theta)\right)+\frac{D}{Dt}\left(\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}\sin(\theta)\right)+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}_{2}}\frac{D}{D\tau}\left(\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}+\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}\right)

(6.25)

\displaystyle+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}F_{t\xi}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}F_{t\tau}\cos(\theta)-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}F_{\xi\tau}\sin(\theta)\,,

\displaystyle-Q^{a}(\theta)=

\displaystyle\frac{D}{D\xi}\left(\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}\cos(\theta)\right)+\frac{D}{Dt}\left(\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}\sin(\theta)\right)+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\frac{D}{D\tau}\left(\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}-\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}\right)\,.
