6.4
String Theory, the GM Partition Function, and an Orlov-type
A_{\infty}
-1-category of
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-strings

The 2d Model as a 2d Gauged B-twisted LG Model

Notice that we can also express (
6.11
) as

\displaystyle S_{\text{2d-LG},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{B}_{2})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}dtd\xi\left(\left|\mathcal{X}^{u}+p^{u}+P^{u}\right|^{2}+\left|\mathcal{Y}^{u}+q^{u}+Q^{u}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)

(6.26)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}dtd\xi\Bigg(\left|\mathcal{X}^{u}+\mathbf{e}_{0}e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}g_{\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})}^{uv}\left(\frac{\partial{W_{2}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{v}}}+\frac{\partial{W_{2}}}{\partial{(\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}(\mathring{A}_{\tau})^{v})}}\right)\right|^{2}

\displaystyle+\left|\mathcal{Y}^{u}+\mathbf{e}_{0}e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}g_{\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})}^{uv}\left(\frac{\partial{W_{2}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{v}}}+\frac{\partial{W_{2}}}{\partial{(\mathbf{e}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}(\mathring{A}_{\tau})^{v})}}\right)\right|^{2}+\dots\Bigg)\,,

where
W_{2}(\widetilde{\mathsf{A}},\widetilde{\mathsf{B}},\mathring{A}_{\tau})
is a holomorphic superpotential, and

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}^{u}

\displaystyle=\frac{D}{D\xi}\left(\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{u}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{u}\cos(\theta)-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}(\mathring{A}_{\tau})^{u}\sin(\theta)\right)

(6.27)

\displaystyle+\frac{D}{Dt}\left(\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{u}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{u}\sin(\theta)+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}(\mathring{A}_{\tau})^{u}\cos(\theta)\right)+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}F_{t\xi}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}^{u}

\displaystyle=\frac{D}{D\xi}\left(\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{u}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{u}\cos(\theta)\right)+\frac{D}{Dt}\left(\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{u}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{u}\sin(\theta)\right)\,.

Just like in

§

5.4
, when we apply specific values of
\theta
to the conditions obtained by setting to zero the expression within the squared terms of (
6.26
) whilst noting that the physical theory ought to be symmetric under a variation of
\theta
, we find that it can also be equivalently expressed as

\displaystyle S_{\text{2d-LG},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Bigg(

\displaystyle\left|D\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}\right|^{2}+\left|D\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}\right|^{2}+\left|D\mathring{A}_{\tau}\right|^{2}+\left|\mathbf{e}_{0}F-g_{\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})}^{uv}\left(\frac{\partial{W_{2}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{v}}}+\frac{\partial{W_{2}}}{\partial{(\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}(\mathring{A}_{\tau})^{v})}}\right)\right|^{2}

(6.28)

\displaystyle+\left|g_{\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})}^{uv}\left(\frac{\partial{W_{2}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{v}}}+\frac{\partial{W_{2}}}{\partial{(\mathbf{e}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}(\mathring{A}_{\tau})^{v})}}\right)\right|^{2}+\dots\Bigg)\,.

In other words, the 2d gauged sigma model with target
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
can also be interpreted as a 2d gauged
B-twisted
LG model in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
with holomorphic superpotential
W_{2}(\widetilde{\mathsf{A}},\widetilde{\mathsf{B}},\mathring{A}_{\tau})
.

By setting
d_{\xi}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{u}=0=d_{\xi}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{u}
and
A_{t},\mathring{A}_{\tau},A_{\xi}\rightarrow 0
in the expression within the squared terms in (
6.26
), we can (via (
6.27
)) read off the LG
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-string (corresponding to
\Sigma^{IJ,KL}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
) equations (that re-express (
6.16
) with
A_{t},\mathring{A}_{\tau},A_{\xi}\rightarrow 0
) as

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{u}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{u}\sin(\theta)\right)

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{e}_{0}e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}g_{\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})}^{uv}\left(\frac{\partial{W_{2}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{v}}}+\frac{\partial{W_{2}}}{\partial{(\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}(\mathring{A}_{\tau})^{v})}}\right)_{\mathring{A}_{\tau}=0}\,,

(6.29)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{u}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{u}\sin(\theta)\right)

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{e}_{0}e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}g_{\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})}^{uv}\left(\frac{\partial{W_{2}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{v}}}+\frac{\partial{W_{2}}}{\partial{(\mathbf{e}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}(\mathring{A}_{\tau})^{v})}}\right)_{\mathring{A}_{\tau}=0}\,,

where the subscript “
\mathring{A}_{\tau}=0
” means to set
\mathring{A}_{\tau}
to zero in those terms.

By setting
d_{t}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{u}=0=d_{t}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{u}
in (
6.29
), we get the LG
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-string endpoint (corresponding to
\Sigma^{IJ,KL}(\pm\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
) equations (that re-express (
6.20
)) as

\displaystyle\mathbf{e}_{0}e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}g_{\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})}^{uv}\left(\frac{\partial{W_{2}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{v}}}+\frac{\partial{W_{2}}}{\partial{(\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}(\mathring{A}_{\tau})^{v})}}\right)_{\mathring{A}_{\tau}=0}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(6.30)

\displaystyle\mathbf{e}_{0}e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}g_{\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})}^{uv}\left(\frac{\partial{W_{2}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{v}}}+\frac{\partial{W_{2}}}{\partial{(\mathbf{e}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}(\mathring{A}_{\tau})^{v})}}\right)_{\mathring{A}_{\tau}=0}

\displaystyle=0\,.

Recall from the end of

§

6.2
that we are only considering
M_{2}
and
G
such that the
\theta
-deformed
G_{\mathbb{O}}
-BF configurations on
M_{2}
are isolated and non-degenerate.
Next, recall also that such configurations will correspond to the endpoints of the
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-strings; therefore, just like their endpoints, these
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-strings would be isolated and non-degenerate.
As these
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-strings will correspond here, to the endpoints of the LG
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-strings, this means that the endpoints and thus the configurations satisfying (
6.30
) will also be isolated and non-degenerate.
From (
6.30
), one can see that these configurations are critical points of
W_{2}(\widetilde{\mathsf{A}},\widetilde{\mathsf{B}},\mathring{A}_{\tau})
; that they are isolated and non-degenerate means that
W_{2}(\widetilde{\mathsf{A}},\widetilde{\mathsf{B}},\mathring{A}_{\tau})
can be regarded as a (holomorphic) Morse function in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
.

The 2d Gauged LG Model as an LG SQM

With suitable rescalings, we can recast (
6.26
) as a 1d LG SQM (that re-expresses (
6.14
)), where its action will be given by
36
36

36

The same steps in
footnote 32
are applied to arrive at the following expression.

\displaystyle S_{\text{2d-LG SQM},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2}))}

(6.31)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int d\xi\left(\left|\partial_{\xi}\varphi_{+}^{\alpha}+g^{\alpha\beta}_{\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2}))}\frac{\partial{H_{2}}}{\partial{\varphi_{+}^{\beta}}}\right|^{2}+\left|\partial_{\xi}\varphi_{-}^{\alpha}+g^{\alpha\beta}_{\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2}))}\frac{\partial{H_{2}}}{\partial{\varphi_{-}^{\beta}}}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

where
H_{2}(\varphi_{+},\varphi_{-})
is the
real-valued
potential in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2}))
with
\varphi_{+}
and
\varphi_{-}
defined as in

§

6.1
, and the subscript “2d-LG SQM,
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2}))
” is to specify that it is a 1d SQM in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2}))
obtained from the equivalent 2d LG model.
We will also refer to this
1d
LG SQM as “2d-LG SQM” in the rest of this subsection.

The 2d-LG SQM will localize onto configurations that
simultaneously
set to zero the LHS and RHS of the expression within the squared terms in (
6.31
).
In other words, it will localize onto
\xi
-invariant critical points of
H_{2}(\varphi_{+},\varphi_{-})
that will correspond, when
A_{t},\mathring{A}_{\tau},A_{\xi}\rightarrow 0
, to the LG
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-strings defined by (
6.29
).
For our choice of
M_{2}
and
G
, just like their endpoints, the LG
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-strings will be isolated and non-degenerate.
Thus,
H_{2}(\varphi_{+},\varphi_{-})
can be regarded as a real-valued Morse functional in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2}))
.

String Theory from the 2d B-twisted LG Model

Just like in

§

5.4
, the 2d gauged B-twisted LG model in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
with action (
6.26
) can be interpreted as a string theory in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
.
Thus, the LG
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-strings that the 2d-LG SQM localizes onto can be regarded as B-strings.

The dynamics of this string theory in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
will be governed by the BPS worldsheet equations determined by setting to zero the expression within the squared terms in (
6.26
), where
(\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{u},\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{u},(\mathring{A}_{\tau})^{u})
are scalars on the worldsheet corresponding to the holomorphic coordinates of
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
.
At an arbitrary instant in time whence
d_{\xi}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{u}=d_{\xi}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{u}=0=d_{\xi}(\mathring{A}_{\tau})^{u}=d_{\xi}A_{t}
therein, the dynamics of
(\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{u},\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{u},(\mathring{A}_{\tau})^{u})
and the 2d gauge fields
(A_{t},A_{\xi})
along
t
will be governed by the string equations

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{u}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{u}\sin(\theta)+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}(\mathring{A}_{\tau})^{u}\cos(\theta)+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}A_{\xi}\right)

(6.32)

\displaystyle=-\left[A_{\xi},\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{u}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{u}\cos(\theta)-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}(\mathring{A}_{\tau})^{u}\sin(\theta)\right]-\mathbf{e}_{0}e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}g_{\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})}^{uv}\left(\frac{\partial{W_{2}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{v}}}+\frac{\partial{W_{2}}}{\partial{(\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}(\mathring{A}_{\tau})^{v})}}\right)

\displaystyle-\left[A_{t},\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{u}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{u}\sin(\theta)+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}(\mathring{A}_{\tau})^{u}\cos(\theta)+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}A_{\xi}\right]\,,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{u}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{u}\sin(\theta)\right)

\displaystyle=-\left[A_{\xi},\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{u}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{u}\cos(\theta)\right]-\mathbf{e}_{0}e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}g_{\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})}^{uv}\left(\frac{\partial{W_{2}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{v}}}+\frac{\partial{W_{2}}}{\partial{(\mathbf{e}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}(\mathring{A}_{\tau})^{v})}}\right)

\displaystyle-\left[A_{t},\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{u}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{u}\sin(\theta)\right]\,.

Morphisms from
\Gamma^{IJ}(\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
to
\Gamma^{KL}(\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
as Ext-groups

Repeating here the analysis in

§

5.4
with (
6.31
) as the action of the 2d-LG SQM, we find that we can interpret the LG
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-string solution corresponding to
\Sigma^{IJ,KL}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
as Ext-groups.
Specifically, a
\Sigma^{IJ,KL}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-string pair, whose left and right endpoints correspond to
\Gamma^{IJ}(\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
and
\Gamma^{KL}(\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
, respectively, can be identified as an LG
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-string pair
\mathfrak{q}^{IJ,KL}_{W_{2},\pm}(\theta)\in S^{IJ,KL}_{W_{2}}
, starting and ending on
singular
fibers
\mathfrak{T}^{IJ}_{W_{2}}(\theta),\mathfrak{T}^{KL}_{W_{2}}(\theta)\in W_{2,\text{sg}}^{-1}(0)
, respectively, which are also described as zero-dimensional submanifolds of
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
.

This means that the 2d LG-SQM in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2}))
with action (
6.31
), will physically realize a Floer homology that we shall name a
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-2d-LG Floer homology.
The chains of the
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-2d-LG Floer complex will be generated by LG
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-strings which we can identify with
\mathfrak{q}^{**,**}_{W_{2},\pm}(\theta)
, and the
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-2d-LG Floer differential will be realized by the flow lines governed by the gradient flow equations satisfied by the
\xi
-varying configurations which set the expression within the squared terms in (
6.31
) to zero.
The partition function of the 2d-LG SQM in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2}))
will then be given by

\mathcal{Z}_{\text{2d-LG SQM},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2}))}(G)=\sum_{I\neq J\neq K\neq L=1}^{w}\;\sum_{\mathfrak{q}^{IJ,KL}_{W_{2},\pm}\in S^{IJ,KL}_{W_{2}}}\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{w}}\left(\mathfrak{q}^{IJ,KL}_{W_{2},\pm}(\theta)\right)\,.

(6.33)

Here, the contribution
\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{w}}\big(\mathfrak{q}^{IJ,KL}_{W_{2},\pm}(\theta)\big)
can be identified with a homology class in a
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-LG Floer homology generated by LG
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-strings whose endpoints correspond to
\theta
-deformed, non-constant paths in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
.
The degree of each chain in the complex is
d_{w}
, and is counted by the number of outgoing flow lines from the critical points of
H_{2}(\varphi_{+},\varphi_{-})
in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{t},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2}))
which can also be identified as
\mathfrak{q}^{IJ,KL}_{W_{2},\pm}(\theta)
.

Note that
\mathfrak{q}^{IJ,KL}_{W_{2},\pm}(\theta)
, which corresponds to an LG
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-string, will be defined by (i) (
6.29
) with (ii) endpoints defined by (
6.30
).
In other words, we can write

\mathfrak{q}^{IJ,KL}_{W_{2},\pm}(\theta)\Longleftrightarrow\text{Ext}\left(\mathfrak{T}^{IJ}_{W_{2}}(\theta),\mathfrak{T}^{KL}_{W_{2}}(\theta)\right)_{\pm}\,,

(6.34)

where
\text{Ext}\left(\mathfrak{T}^{IJ}_{W_{2}}(\theta),\mathfrak{T}^{KL}_{W_{2}}(\theta)\right)_{\pm}
represents an LG
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-string whose start and end are described as singular fibers
\mathfrak{T}^{IJ}_{W_{2}}(\theta)
and
\mathfrak{T}^{KL}_{W_{2}}(\theta)
, respectively.

Equivalently, it is a
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-string defined by (i) (
6.16
) and
A_{t},\mathring{A}_{\tau},A_{\xi}\rightarrow 0
with (ii) endpoints defined by (
6.20
) corresponding to
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-strings.
This will lead us to the following one-to-one identification

\boxed{\text{Hom}\left(\Gamma^{IJ}(\mathfrak{B}_{2}),\Gamma^{KL}(\mathfrak{B}_{2})\right)_{\pm}\Longleftrightarrow\text{Ext}\left(\mathfrak{T}^{IJ}_{W_{2}},\mathfrak{T}^{KL}_{W_{2}}\right)_{\pm}}

(6.35)

Just like in

§

5.4
, the RHS of (
6.35
) can be regarded as morphisms in an Orlov-type triangulated category of singularities of
W_{2}
.

The Normalized GM Partition Function, LG
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-string Scattering, and Maps of an
A_{\infty}
-structure

Since our GM theory is semi-classical, its normalized 5d partition function will be a sum over tree-level scattering amplitudes of the LG
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-strings defined by (
6.29
).
The BPS worldsheet underlying such a tree-level scattering is similar to
Fig. 4
, where instead of endpoints of each string being labeled by
\mathfrak{C}^{*}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{H}}}(\theta)
, they will now be labeled
\Gamma^{**}(\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
.

In other words, we can, like in (
5.29
), express the normalized GM partition function as

\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{\text{GM},M_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(G)=\sum_{\mathfrak{N}_{w}}\mu_{\mathfrak{B}_{2}}^{\mathfrak{N}_{w}}\,,\qquad\mathfrak{N}_{w}=1,2,\dots,\left\lfloor\frac{w-2}{2}\right\rfloor,

(6.36)

where each

\boxed{\mu_{\mathfrak{B}_{2}}^{\mathfrak{N}_{w}}:\bigotimes_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{N}_{w}}\text{Hom}\left(\Gamma^{I_{2i-1}I_{2i}}(\mathfrak{B}_{2}),\Gamma^{I_{2(i+1)-1}I_{2(i+1)}}(\mathfrak{B}_{2})\right)_{-}\rightarrow\text{Hom}\left(\Gamma^{I_{1}I_{2}}(\mathfrak{B}_{2}),\Gamma^{I_{2\mathfrak{N}_{w}+1}I_{2\mathfrak{N}_{w}+2}}(\mathfrak{B}_{2})\right)_{+}}

(6.37)

is a scattering amplitude of
\mathfrak{N}_{w}
incoming LG
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-strings
\text{Hom}\left(\Gamma^{**}(\mathfrak{B}_{2}),\Gamma^{**}(\mathfrak{B}_{2})\right)_{-}
, and a single outgoing LG
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-string
\text{Hom}\left(\Gamma^{I_{1}I_{2}}(\mathfrak{B}_{2}),\Gamma^{I_{2\mathfrak{N}_{w}+1}I_{2\mathfrak{N}_{w}+2}}(\mathfrak{B}_{2})\right)_{+}
, with left and right boundaries as labeled, whose underlying worldsheet can be regarded as a constant disc with
\mathfrak{N}_{w}+1
vertex operators at the boundary.
In short,
\mu^{\mathfrak{N}_{w}}_{\mathfrak{B}_{2}}
counts constant discs with
\mathfrak{N}_{w}+1
punctures at the boundary that are mapped to
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
according to the BPS worldsheet equations (determined by setting to zero the expression within the squared terms in (
6.26
)) that are given by (
6.24
).

At any rate, just as in

§

5.4
, the collection of
\mu^{\mathfrak{N}_{w}}_{\mathfrak{B}_{2}}
in (
6.37
) can be regarded as composition maps defining an
A_{\infty}
-structure.

An Orlov-type
A_{\infty}
-1-category of
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-strings

Altogether, this means that the normalized partition function of GM theory on
M_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
as expressed in (
6.36
), manifests a
novel
Orlov-type
A_{\infty}
-1-category whose
(i) 1-objects are singular fibers
W_{2,\text{sg}}^{-1}(0)
corresponding to
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-strings,
(ii) 1-morphisms are Ext-groups corresponding to
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-strings ending on the 1-objects via the one-to-one identification (
6.35
),
and (iii)
A_{\infty}
-structure is defined by the
\mu^{\mathfrak{N}_{w}}_{\mathfrak{B}_{2}}
maps (
6.37
).

6.5
Membrane Theory, the GM Partition Function, and an RW-type
A_{\infty}
-2-category Categorifying the
G_{\mathbb{O}}
-flat Floer Homology of
M_{2}

The 3d Model as a 3d Gauged B-twisted LG Model

Note that we can also express (
6.8
) as

\displaystyle S_{\text{3d-LG},\mathfrak{B}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dtd\tau d\xi\,\left(\left|\mathcal{X}^{a}+P^{a}(\theta)\right|^{2}+\left|\mathcal{Y}^{a}+Q^{a}(\theta)\right|^{2}+\dots\right)

(6.38)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}dtd\tau d\xi\,\left(\left|\mathcal{X}^{a}+\mathbf{e}_{0}e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}g_{\mathfrak{B}_{2}}^{ab}\frac{\partial{V_{2}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{b}}}\right|^{2}+\left|\mathcal{Y}^{a}+\mathbf{e}_{0}e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}g_{\mathfrak{B}_{2}}^{ab}\frac{\partial{V_{2}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{b}}}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

where
V_{2}(\widetilde{\mathsf{A}},\widetilde{\mathsf{B}})
is given by (
4.28
), and

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}^{a}=

\displaystyle(D_{\xi}+\mathbf{e}_{0}D_{t}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}D_{\tau})\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\left(\cos(\theta)D_{\xi}+\sin(\theta)D_{t}+\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}D_{\tau}\right)\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}

(6.39)

\displaystyle+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}F_{t\xi}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}F_{t\tau}\cos(\theta)-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}F_{\xi\tau}\sin(\theta)\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}^{a}=

\displaystyle(D_{\xi}+\mathbf{e}_{0}D_{t}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}D_{\tau})\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}(\cos(\theta)D_{\xi}+\sin(\theta)D_{t}+\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}D_{\tau})\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}\,.

Applying specific values of
\theta
to the conditions obtained by setting to zero the expression within the squared terms of (
6.38
) whilst noting that the physical theory ought to be symmetric under a variation of
\theta
, we find that it can also be equivalently expressed as

S_{\text{3d-LG},\mathfrak{B}_{2}}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\left|D\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}\right|^{2}+\left|D\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}\right|^{2}+\left|\mathbf{e}_{0}F-g_{\mathfrak{B}_{2}}^{ab}\frac{\partial{V_{2}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{b}}}\right|^{2}+\left|g_{\mathfrak{B}_{2}}^{ab}\frac{\partial{V_{2}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{b}}}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

(6.40)

where now,
D
is the covariant derivative in the real 3d gauge fields, and
F
is the field strength of the real 3d gauge fields.
As the conditions that minimize (
6.40
) involve constant maps, we can interpret (
6.40
) as the action of a “B-twisted” model.
In other words, the 3d gauged sigma model with target
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
can also be interpreted as a 3d gauged
B-twisted
LG model in
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
with holomorphic superpotential
V_{2}(\widetilde{\mathsf{A}},\widetilde{\mathsf{B}})
.

By setting
d_{\xi}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}=0=d_{\xi}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}
and
A_{t},A_{\tau},A_{\xi}\rightarrow 0
in the expression within the squared terms in (
6.38
), we can read off (via (
6.39
)) the LG
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-membrane (corresponding to
\sigma^{IJ,KL}_{\pm}(t,\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
) equations (that re-express (
6.17
) with
A_{t},A_{\tau},A_{\xi}\rightarrow 0
) as

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}\sin(\theta)\right)+\frac{d}{d\tau}\left(e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}\right)

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{e}_{0}e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}g_{\mathfrak{B}_{2}}^{ab}\frac{\partial{V_{2}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{b}}}\,,

(6.41)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}\sin(\theta)\right)+\frac{d}{d\tau}\left(e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}\right)

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{e}_{0}e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}g_{\mathfrak{B}_{2}}^{ab}\frac{\partial{V_{2}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{b}}}\,.

By setting
d_{t}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}=0=d_{t}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}
in (
6.41
), we can read off the LG
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-string (corresponding to
\gamma^{IJ}(\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
and
\gamma^{KL}(\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
) equations, or equivalently, the LG
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-membrane
edge
(corresponding to
\sigma^{IJ,KL}(\pm\infty,\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
) equations (that re-express (
6.21
)) as

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\tau}\left(e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}\right)

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{e}_{0}e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}g_{\mathfrak{B}_{2}}^{ab}\frac{\partial{V_{2}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{b}}}\,,

(6.42)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\tau}\left(e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}\right)

\displaystyle=-\mathbf{e}_{0}e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}g_{\mathfrak{B}_{2}}^{ab}\frac{\partial{V_{2}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{b}}}\,.

By setting
d_{\tau}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}=0=d_{\tau}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}
in (
6.42
), we can read off the LG
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-string endpoint (corresponding to
\gamma^{**}(\pm\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
) equations, or equivalently, the LG
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-membrane
vertex
(corresponding to
\sigma^{IJ,KL}(\pm\infty,\pm\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
and
\sigma^{IJ,KL}(\pm\infty,\mp\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
) equations (that re-express (
6.22
)) as

\mathbf{e}_{0}e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}g_{\mathfrak{B}_{2}}^{ab}\frac{\partial{V_{2}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{b}}}=0\,,\qquad\mathbf{e}_{0}e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}g_{\mathfrak{B}_{2}}^{ab}\frac{\partial{V_{2}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{b}}}=0\,.

(6.43)

Recall from the end of

§

6.2
that we are only considering
M_{2}
and
G
such that the vertices
\sigma^{IJ,KL}(\pm\infty,\pm\infty,

\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
and
\sigma^{IJ,KL}(\pm\infty,\mp\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
are isolated and non-degenerate.
Therefore, from their definition in (
6.43
) as critical points of
V_{2}(\widetilde{\mathsf{A}},\widetilde{\mathsf{B}})
, it would mean that
V_{2}(\widetilde{\mathsf{A}},\widetilde{\mathsf{B}})
is a (holomorphic) Morse function in
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
.

The 3d Gauged B-twisted LG Model as an LG SQM

Last but not least, after suitable rescalings, we can recast (
6.38
) as a 1d LG SQM (that re-expresses (
6.14
)), where its action will be given by
37
37

37

The same steps in
footnote 32
are applied to arrive at the following expression.

\displaystyle S_{\text{3d-LG SQM},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{B}_{2})}

(6.44)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int d\xi\left(\left|\partial_{\xi}\varphi_{+}^{\alpha}+g^{\alpha\beta}_{\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{B}_{2})}\frac{\partial{\mathfrak{h}_{2}}}{\partial{\varphi_{+}^{\beta}}}\right|^{2}+\left|\partial_{\xi}\varphi_{-}^{\alpha}+g^{\alpha\beta}_{\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{B}_{2})}\frac{\partial{\mathfrak{h}_{2}}}{\partial{\varphi_{-}^{\beta}}}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

where
\mathfrak{h}_{2}(\varphi_{+},\varphi_{-})
is the
real-valued
potential in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
, with
\varphi_{+}
and
\varphi_{-}
defined as in

§

6.1
, and the subscript “3d-LG SQM,
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
” is to specify that it is a 1d SQM with target
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
obtained from the equivalent 3d LG model.
We will also refer to this
1d
LG SQM as “3d-LG SQM” in the rest of this subsection.

The 3d-LG SQM will localize onto configurations that
simultaneously
set to zero the LHS and RHS of the expression within the squared terms in (
6.44
).
In other words, it will localize onto
\xi
-invariant critical points of
\mathfrak{h}_{2}(\varphi_{+},\varphi_{-})
that will correspond, when
A_{t},A_{\tau},A_{\xi}\rightarrow 0
, to the LG
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-membranes defined by (
6.41
).
For our choice of
M_{2}
and
G
, just like their vertices, the LG
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-membranes will be isolated and non-degenerate.
Thus,
\mathfrak{h}_{2}(\varphi_{+},\varphi_{-})
can be regarded as a
real-valued
Morse functional in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
.

Membrane Theory from the 3d LG Model

Just like in

§

6.3
, the equivalent 3d gauged B-twisted LG model will define an open membrane theory in
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
with effective worldvolumes and boundaries shown in
Fig. 8
, where
\xi
is the temporal direction and
(t,\tau)
are the spatial directions.

The dynamics of this open membrane theory in
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
will be governed by the BPS worldvolume equations determined by setting to zero the expression within the squared terms in (
6.38
), where
(\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a},\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a})
are scalars on the worldvolume corresponding to the holomorphic coordinates of
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
.
At an arbitrary instant in time whence
d_{\xi}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}=d_{\xi}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}=0=d_{\xi}A_{\{t,\tau\}}
therein, the dynamics of
(\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a},\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a})
and the 3d gauge fields along
(t,\tau)
will be governed by the 2d membrane equations

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}\sin(\theta)+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}A_{\xi}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}A_{\tau}\cos(\theta)\right)+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}_{2}}\frac{d}{d\tau}\left(\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}+\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}+A_{\xi}\sin(\theta)-A_{t}\cos(\theta)\right)

(6.45)

\displaystyle=-\left[A_{t},\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}\sin(\theta)+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}A_{\xi}\right]+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}_{2}}\left[A_{\tau},\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}+\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}+A_{\xi}\sin(\theta)\right]

\displaystyle-\left[A_{\xi},\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}\cos(\theta)\right]-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}[A_{t},A_{\tau}]\cos(\theta)-\mathbf{e}_{0}e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}g_{\mathfrak{B}_{2}}^{ab}\frac{\partial{V_{2}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{b}}}\,,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}\sin(\theta)\right)+e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\frac{d}{d\tau}\left(\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}-\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}\right)

\displaystyle=-\left[A_{t},\mathbf{e}_{0}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}\sin(\theta)\right]-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\hat{\mathbf{e}}_{2}\left[A_{\tau}\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}-\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}\right]-\left[A_{\xi},\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{a}-e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}\widetilde{\mathsf{A}}^{a}\cos(\theta)\right]-\mathbf{e}_{0}e^{\mathbf{e}_{0}\theta}g_{\mathfrak{B}_{2}}^{ab}\frac{\partial{V_{2}}}{\partial{\widetilde{\mathsf{B}}^{b}}}\,.

Morphisms between LG
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-strings as Ext-groups

At this juncture, note that the endpoints of an LG
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-string (defined by (
6.43
)) can be described as
singular
fibers
V_{2,\text{sg}}^{-1}(0)
that correspond to
G_{\mathbb{O}}
-BF configurations on
M_{2}
.
In particular, to an LG
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-string
\gamma^{IJ}(\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
(illustrated as the left edge in
Fig. 7
), its (i) bottom endpoint
\gamma^{IJ}(-\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})\eqcolon\mathcal{T}^{I}_{V_{2}}(\theta)\in V_{2,\text{sg}}^{-1}(0)
and (ii) top endpoint
\gamma^{IJ}(+\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})\eqcolon\mathcal{T}^{J}_{V_{2}}(\theta)\in V_{2,\text{sg}}^{-1}(0)
, are described as singular fibers which are also zero-dimensional submanifolds of
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
, and correspond to
\theta
-deformed
G_{\mathbb{O}}
-BF configurations (i)
\mathfrak{C}^{I}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}(\theta)
and (ii)
\mathfrak{C}^{J}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}(\theta)
on
M_{2}
.
We shall denote by
\mathcal{T}^{IJ}_{V_{2}}(\theta)
an LG
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-string
\gamma^{IJ}(\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
so as to indicate that its endpoints are singular fibers
V_{2,\text{sg}}^{-1}(0)
.

Let
(i)
S^{IJ}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}
and (ii)
S^{KL}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}

be the collection of all such strings whose bottom and top endpoints correspond to
(i)
\mathfrak{C}^{I}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}(\theta)
and
\mathfrak{C}^{J}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}(\theta)
and (ii)
\mathfrak{C}^{K}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}(\theta)
and
\mathfrak{C}^{L}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}(\theta)
.
Each
(i) LG
\gamma^{IJ}(\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{3})
-string and (ii) LG
\gamma^{KL}(\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{3})
-string,
will then correspond to a string
(i)
p^{IJ}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}(\theta)\in S^{IJ}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}
and (ii)
p^{KL}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}(\theta)\in S^{KL}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}
.
This means that an LG
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-membrane pair
\sigma^{IJ,KL}_{\pm}(t,\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
with left edge
\sigma^{IJ,KL}_{\pm}(-\infty,\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})=\gamma^{IJ}(\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
and right edge
\sigma^{IJ,KL}_{\pm}(+\infty,\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})=\gamma^{KL}(\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
, can be identified by a pair of strings
q^{IJ,KL}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}},\pm}\coloneq\{p^{IJ}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}},p^{KL}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}\}_{\pm}\in S^{IJ}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}\otimes S^{KL}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}
that define their edges.

At any rate, the 3d-LG SQM in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
with action (
6.44
) will physically realize a Floer homology that we shall name a
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
-3d-LG Floer homology.
The chains of the
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
-3d-LG Floer complex are generated by LG
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-membranes which, as detailed above, can be identified by
q^{**,**}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}},\pm}(\theta)
, and the
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
-3d-LG Floer differential will be realized by the flow lines governed by the gradient flow equations satisfied by the
\xi
-varying configurations which set the expression within the squared terms of (
6.44
) to zero.
The partition function of the 3d-LG SQM in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
will be given by

\mathcal{Z}_{\text{3d-LG SQM},\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{B}_{2})}(G)=\sum_{I\neq J\neq K\neq L=1}^{w}\;\sum_{q^{IJ,KL}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}},\pm}\in S^{IJ}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}\otimes S^{KL}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}}\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{w}}\left(q^{IJ,KL}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}},\pm}(\theta)\right)\,.

(6.46)

Here, the contribution
\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{w}}\big(q^{IJ,KL}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}},\pm}(\theta)\big)
can be identified with a homology class in a
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
-3d-LG Floer homology generated by LG
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-membranes, whose edges correspond to LG
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-strings, and whose vertices are described as singular fibers
V_{2,\text{sg}}^{-1}(0)
.
The degree of each chain is
d_{w}
, and is counted by the number of outgoing flow lines from the critical points of
\mathfrak{h}_{2}(\varphi_{+},\varphi_{-})
in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R}^{2},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
which can also be identified by
q^{IJ,KL}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}},\pm}(\theta)
.

Note that an LG
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-membrane in the 3d LG model defined by (i) (
6.41
) with (ii) edges being LG
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-strings (
6.42
) and (iii) vertices (
6.43
), will correspond to an LG
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-string in the 2d LG model defined by (
6.29
) with (ii) endpoints (
6.30
) being singular fibers
W_{2,\text{sg}}^{-1}(0)
, accordingly.
This means that just as how an LG
\mathfrak{P}^{\theta}(\mathbb{R}_{\tau},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-string pair
\Sigma^{IJ,KL}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
can be identified as an Ext-group between singular fibers
\mathfrak{T}^{IJ}_{W_{2}}(\theta)
and
\mathfrak{T}^{KL}_{W_{2}}(\theta)
describing the endpoints, an LG
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-membrane pair
\sigma^{IJ,KL}_{\pm}(t,\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{3})
can be identified as an Ext-group between
\mathcal{T}^{IJ}_{V_{2}}(\theta)
and
\mathcal{T}^{KL}_{V_{2}}(\theta)
describing the edges.
In other words, we can write

q^{IJ,KL}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}},\pm}(\theta)\Longleftrightarrow\text{Ext}\left(\mathcal{T}^{IJ}_{V_{2}}(\theta),\mathcal{T}^{KL}_{V_{2}}(\theta)\right)_{\pm}\,,

(6.47)

where
\text{Ext}\left(\mathcal{T}^{IJ}_{V_{2}}(\theta),\mathcal{T}^{KL}_{V_{2}}(\theta)\right)_{\pm}
is an Ext group representing a
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-membrane, whose left and right edges correspond to
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-strings described by
\mathcal{T}^{IJ}_{V_{2}}(\theta)
and
\mathcal{T}^{KL}_{V_{2}}(\theta)
, respectively.

Equivalently, it is a
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-membrane defined by (i) (
6.17
) with (ii) edges (
6.21
) and (iii) vertices (
6.22
).
Note that a membrane can be regarded as a morphism between its edges, which, in turn, can be regarded as morphisms between their endpoints.
In other words, a
\sigma^{IJ,KL}(t,\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-membrane can be regarded as a 1-morphism
\text{Hom}\big(\gamma^{IJ}(\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2}),\gamma^{KL}(\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})\big)
from its left edge to its right edge, where the (A)
\gamma^{IJ}(\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-string and (B)
\gamma^{KL}(\tau,\theta,\mathfrak{B}_{2})
-string can themselves be regarded as 1-morphisms (A)
\text{Hom}\big(\mathfrak{C}^{I}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}(\theta),\mathfrak{C}^{J}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}(\theta)\big)
and (B)
\text{Hom}\big(\mathfrak{C}^{K}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}(\theta),\mathfrak{C}^{L}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}(\theta)\big)
, from their bottom to top endpoints.
This is illustrated in
Fig. 7
.
Thus, we have the following one-to-one identifications

\boxed{\begin{gathered}\text{Ext}\left(\mathcal{T}^{IJ}_{V_{2}},\mathcal{T}^{KL}_{V_{2}}\right)_{\pm}\\
\Updownarrow\\
\text{Hom}\left(\gamma^{IJ}(\tau,\mathfrak{B}_{2}),\gamma^{KL}(\tau,\mathfrak{B}_{2})\right)_{\pm}\\
\Updownarrow\\
\text{Hom}\left(\text{Hom}\left(\mathfrak{C}^{I}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}},\mathfrak{C}^{J}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}\right),\text{Hom}\left(\mathfrak{C}^{I}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}},\mathfrak{C}^{J}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}\right)\right)_{\pm}\end{gathered}}

(6.48)

\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}

Just like in

In turn, this means that the normalized GM partition function can also be regarded as a sum over tree-level scattering amplitudes of LG
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-membranes as shown in

.

\mathfrak{C}^{I_{1}}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}
\mathfrak{C}^{I_{2\mathfrak{N}_{w}+2}}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}
+
\mathfrak{C}^{I_{1}}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}

:

In other words, we can express the normalized GM partition function as

\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{\text{GM},M_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(G)=\sum_{\mathfrak{N}_{w}}\varPi^{\mathfrak{N}_{w}}_{\mathfrak{B}_{2}}\,,\qquad\mathfrak{N}_{w}=1,2,\dots,\left\lfloor\frac{w-2}{2}\right\rfloor,

where each

\boxed{\begin{aligned} \varPi^{\mathfrak{N}_{w}}_{\mathfrak{B}_{2}}:\bigotimes_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{N}_{w}}&\text{Hom}\left(\text{Hom}\left(\mathfrak{C}^{I_{2i-1}}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}},\mathfrak{C}^{I_{2i}}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}\right),\text{Hom}\left(\mathfrak{C}^{I_{2(i+1)-1}}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}},\mathfrak{C}^{I_{2(i+1)}}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}\right)\right)_{-}\\

(6.50)

is a scattering amplitude of
\mathfrak{N}_{w}
incoming LG
\mathfrak{B}_{2}^{\theta}
-membranes
\text{Hom}\big(\text{Hom}\big(\mathfrak{C}^{*}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}},\mathfrak{C}^{*}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}\big),\text{Hom}\big(\mathfrak{C}^{*}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}},

\mathfrak{C}^{*}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}\big)\big)_{-}
, and a single outgoing LG

-membrane
\text{Hom}\big(\text{Hom}\big(\mathfrak{C}^{I_{1}}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}},\mathfrak{C}^{I_{2}}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}\big),\text{Hom}\big(\mathfrak{C}^{I_{2\mathfrak{N}_{w}+1}}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}},\mathfrak{C}^{I_{2\mathfrak{N}_{w}+2}}_{\text{BF}_{\mathbb{O}}}\big)\big)_{+}

. That is,

punctures at the boundary that are mapped to
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
according to the BPS worldvolume equations (determined by setting to zero the expression within the squared terms in (

6.25

In turn, this means that

counts the moduli space of solutions to (

) set to zero (or equivalently, the expression within the squared terms of (

) set to zero) with

that we shall denote as

An RW-type
A_{\infty}
-2-category

Altogether, this means that the normalized partition function of GM theory on

A Gauge-theoretic Generalization of DR’s Mathematical Conjecture

In

§

5.5
, we furnished a physical proof of DR’s mathematical conjecture in
[
9
,
\lx@sectionsign
6]
which (from the bottom line of (
5.33
)) can be interpreted as a correspondence between an RW
A_{\infty}
-2-category of branes in
X
and an Orlov-type
A_{\infty}
-1-category of branes in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},X)
.
Therefore, the correspondence between the gauge-theoretic RW-type
A_{\infty}
-2-category of objects in
\mathfrak{B}_{2}
and the gauge-theoretic Orlov-type
A_{\infty}
-1-category of objects in
\mathfrak{P}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{B}_{2})
,
would mean that we actually have a
gauge-theoretic generalization
of DR’s mathematical conjecture!

A Mirror Duality of Sigma Models and Langlands Duality of Gauge Theories

In this section, we will first explain how the 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
A and B-model in Hitchin moduli spaces can be mirror dual via enhanced Homological Mirror Symmetry (HMS), and why this will imply a Langlands duality between the 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
HW and GM theory.
We will then explain how 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
HW and GM theory can be Langlands dual via 4d
\mathcal{N}=4
S-duality, and why this will lead to the above mirror duality between the 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
A and B-models, thereby furnishing a more fundamental derivation of these dualities.

7.1
A Mirror Duality Between the 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
A-model and B-model in Hitchin Moduli Spaces via Enhanced HMS

The duality between a 2d
\mathcal{N}=(4,4)
A and B-model with mirror hyperkähler target spaces, and its relevance to the geometric Langlands program, was first studied by Kapustin-Witten (KW) in
[
17
]
, where recently, it has also been formulated rigorously as enhanced Homological Mirror Symmetry (HMS) by Hausel in
[
14
]
.
38
38

38

Hausel’s enhanced HMS involves the additional information of (i) how hyperkähler branes are matched under mirror symmetry, and (ii) the symmetry between Wilson and Hecke (or t’Hooft) operators.

In particular, when the hyperkähler target spaces are Hitchin moduli spaces, KW showed that a 2d
\mathcal{N}=(4,4)
A-model with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
is dual to a 2d
\mathcal{N}=(4,4)
B-model with a mirror target
\mathcal{M}^{{}^{L}G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
, where
{}^{L}G
is the Langlands dual of
G
.

Recall from
(i)

§

2.2
and
(ii)

§

3.2
that the spectrum of states of
(i) the 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
A-model with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
on
\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
,
and (ii) the 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
B-model with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
on
\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
, are given by the spectrum of states of
(i) a 2d
\mathcal{N}=(4,4)
A-model with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
on
\Sigma
,
and (ii) the 2d
\mathcal{N}=(4,4)
B-model with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
on
\Sigma
, respectively.

The enhanced HMS of the 2d
\mathcal{N}=(4,4)
A and B-models therefore implies that
the 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
A-model with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
on
\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
is dual to the 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
B-model with target
\mathcal{M}^{{}^{L}G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
on
\Sigma\times\mathbb{R}
.

In other words, we have a mirror duality between the 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
A and B-model in mirror Hitchin moduli spaces.

7.2
A Langlands Duality Between the 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
HW and GM Theory on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
via Enhanced HMS

Recall from (i)

§

2.3
and (ii)

§

3.3
that the
(i) 3d A-model with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
on
I\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
and
(ii) 3d B-model with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
on
I\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
, are equivalent to
(i) HW theory of
G
on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
and
(ii) GM theory of
G
on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
, respectively.

The duality between the 3d A and B-model in mirror Hitchin moduli spaces in

§

7.1
therefore implies a duality between HW theory of
G
on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
and GM theory of
{}^{L}G
on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
.

In other words, we have a Langlands duality between the 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
HW and GM theory on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
of
G
and
{}^{L}G
.

7.3
A Langlands Duality Between the 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
HW and GM Theory on
C\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
via Enhanced HMS

Consider now (i) HW theory of
G
and (ii) GM theory of
G
, on
C\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
.
Performing a BJSV reduction along the Riemann surface
C
results in a
(i) 3d A-model with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
whose action is (
2.5
) (where
\Sigma=\mathbb{R}^{2}
),
and (ii) 3d B-model with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
whose action is (
3.5
) (where
\Sigma=\mathbb{R}^{2}
).

The duality between the 3d A and B-model in mirror Hitchin moduli spaces in

§

7.1
therefore implies a duality between HW theory of
G
on
C\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
and GM theory of
{}^{L}G
on
C\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

In other words, we also have a Langlands duality between the 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
HW and GM theory on
C\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
of
G
and
{}^{L}G
.

7.4
A Langlands Duality Between the 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
HW and GM Theory on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
or
C\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
via 4d
\mathcal{N}=4
S-duality

We will now furnish a more fundamental derivation than that in

§

7.2
and

§

7.3
of the Langlands duality between the 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
gauge theories, from which the mirror duality between 3d sigma models in

§

7.1
can actually be derived (in

§

7.5
).

To this end, let us first consider HW and GM theory on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
, where
M_{3}
is a (not necessarily compact) three-manifold, and
M_{1}
is the direction of time that we shall denote by
\tau
.
39
39

39

Observables in our TQFTs are coordinate-independent; in particular, they are independent of the coordinate directions in Euclidean spacetime. Thus, there is no preferred direction for time, so we are free to redefine the direction of time.

HW Theory on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
and its Effective KW Theory on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
at
t=-1

Recall how we showed, in

§

2.2
, that the spectrum of states of the
topological
3d
\mathcal{N}=4
A-model (given by its
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology) are effectively given by the spectrum of states of the 2d
\mathcal{N}=(4,4)
A-model living on a time-invariant hyperslice of it.
Likewise, the spectrum of states of the
topological
5d
\mathcal{N}=2
HW theory (given by its
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology) will effectively be given by the the spectrum of states of the 4d
\mathcal{N}=4
gauge theory living on a time-invariant hyperslice of it.
We can determine what this 4d theory is through its BPS equations, which, in turn, are given by the BPS equations of HW theory (obtained by setting to zero the
\mathcal{Q}
-variations of the fermions in (
2.1
)) restricted to the hyperslice. Specifically, the 4d BPS equations of interest are obtained by setting
\partial_{\tau}\rightarrow 0
in the BPS equations of HW theory whilst dropping all
\tau
-dependence in the fields, i.e., they are
40
40

40

To arrive at the following expressions, we have chosen an orientation of
M_{3}\times M_{1}
such that it follows the orientation of
M_{3}
, i.e.,
\epsilon_{\tau mpq}\equiv\epsilon_{mpq}
.

\displaystyle F_{mn}-\epsilon_{mnp}D^{p}A^{\tau}-\frac{1}{2}[B_{m\tau},B_{n}^{\;\tau}]-\frac{1}{2}[B_{mp},B_{n}^{\;p}]-D_{t}B_{mn}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad D_{t}\upsilon

\displaystyle=0\,,

(7.1)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{mpq}F^{pq}-D_{m}A_{\tau}-\frac{1}{2}[B_{\tau n},B_{m}^{\;n}]-D_{t}B_{\tau m}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad D_{m}\upsilon

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle F_{tm}+D^{n}B_{nm}+[A^{\tau},B_{\tau m}]

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad[A_{\tau},\upsilon]

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle D_{t}A_{\tau}+D^{m}B_{m\tau}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad[B_{\mu\nu},\upsilon]

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle[\upsilon,\bar{\upsilon}]

\displaystyle=0\,,

where
A_{\tau}
is now a scalar on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
.

Notice that by defining a one-form
\phi\in\Omega^{1}(M_{3}\times\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G))
as
\phi\coloneq-A_{\tau}dt+B_{\tau m}dx^{m}
, (
7.1
) can be written as

\displaystyle F_{\mu\nu}-[\phi_{\mu},\phi_{\nu}]-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}D^{\rho}\phi^{\sigma}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad D_{\mu}\upsilon

\displaystyle=0\,,

(7.2)

\displaystyle D^{\mu}\phi_{\mu}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad[\phi_{\mu},\upsilon]

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle[\upsilon,\bar{\upsilon}]

\displaystyle=0\,,

where
\{\mu,\nu\}\in\{m,t\}
are indices on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
.

These are the BPS equations of a 4d
\mathcal{N}=4
KW theory
[
17
,
\lx@sectionsign
3.2]
, i.e.,

\displaystyle(F-\phi\wedge\phi+tD\phi)^{+}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad D\upsilon+t[\phi,\upsilon]

\displaystyle=0\,,

(7.3)

\displaystyle(F-\phi\wedge\phi-t^{-1}D\phi)^{-}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad D\upsilon-t^{-1}[\phi,\upsilon]

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle D^{*}\phi-t^{-1}[\upsilon,\bar{\upsilon}]

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\qquad D^{*}\phi+t[\upsilon,\bar{\upsilon}]

\displaystyle=0\,,

at
t=-1
.

This therefore means that the spectrum of HW theory on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
is given by the spectrum of KW theory on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
at
t=-1
.
In other words, the
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology of HW theory on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
is
effectively
the
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology of KW theory on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
at
t=-1
.

GM Theory on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
and its Effective KW Theory on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
at
t=-i

We repeat the analysis for 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
GM theory on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
.
The 4d
\mathcal{N}=4
gauge theory living on a time-invariant hyperslice of it can be determined through its BPS equations, which, in turn, are given by the BPS equations of GM theory (obtained by setting to zero the
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-variations of the fermions in (
3.1
)) restricted to the hyperslice. Specifically, the 4d BPS equations of interest are obtained by setting
\partial_{\tau}\rightarrow 0
in the BPS equations of GM theory whilst dropping all
\tau
-dependence in the fields, i.e., they are
41
41

41

The BPS equation in the third line corresponds to the BPS equation involving the auxiliary field, i.e.,
H=0
, which via its equation of motion from (
3.3
), implies that
D^{t}\phi_{t}+D^{m}\phi_{m}+\partial^{\tau}\phi_{\tau}+[A^{\tau},\phi_{\tau}]=0
.

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{tm}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{t}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(7.4)

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{mn}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{m}\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle D^{t}\phi_{t}+D^{m}\phi_{m}+[A^{\tau},\phi_{\tau}]

\displaystyle=0\,.

Notice that by defining a complex scalar
\upsilon\in\Omega^{0}(M_{3}\times\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G))
as
\upsilon\coloneq\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{\tau}/\sqrt{2}
, (
7.4
) can be written as

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad D_{\mu}\upsilon-i[\phi_{\mu},\upsilon]

\displaystyle=0\,,

(7.5)

\displaystyle D^{\mu}\phi_{\mu}+i[\upsilon,\bar{\upsilon}]

\displaystyle=0\,.

These are the BPS equations (
7.3
) of a 4d
\mathcal{N}=4
KW theory at
t=-i
.

This therefore means that the spectrum of GM theory on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
is given by the spectrum of KW theory on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
at
t=-i
.
In other words, the
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-cohomology of GM theory on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
is
effectively
the
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}
-cohomology of KW theory on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
at
t=-i
.

Langlands Duality Between 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
HW and GM Theory on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
via 4d
\mathcal{N}=4
S-duality

Note that as a 4d
\mathcal{N}=4
theory, KW theory enjoys S-duality.
In particular, KW theory of
G
at
t=-1
is S-dual to KW theory of
{}^{L}G
at
t=-i

[
17
]
.
Recall that the spectrum of states of (i) HW theory and (ii) GM theory, on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
, are given by the spectrum of states of KW theory on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
at (i)
t=-1
and (ii)
t=-i
, respectively.
As such, the 4d
\mathcal{N}=4
S-duality of KW theory would imply that 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
HW theory of
G
is Langlands dual to 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
GM theory of
{}^{L}G
, on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
, just as we observed in

§

7.2
.

As this will also allow us to derive, in

§

7.5
, the mirror duality between 3d sigma models in

§

7.1
, we actually have a more fundamental derivation than in

§

7.2
of the Langlands duality between the 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
HW and GM theory on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
of
G
and
{}^{L}G
.

Langlands Duality Between 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
HW and GM Theory on
C\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
via 4d
\mathcal{N}=4
S-duality

Let us now consider our above discussion with
M_{3}=C\times\mathbb{R}
and
M_{1}=\mathbb{R}
.
Then, 4d
\mathcal{N}=4
S-duality of KW theory would imply that 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
HW theory of
G
is Langlands dual to 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
GM theory of
{}^{L}G
, on
C\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, just as we observed in

§

7.3
.

Similarly, we have a more fundamental derivation than in

§

7.3
of the Langlands duality between the 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
HW and GM theory on
C\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
of
G
and
{}^{L}G
.

7.5
A Mirror Duality Between the 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
A-model and B-model in Hitchin Moduli Spaces via 4d
\mathcal{N}=4
S-duality

Recall from (i)

§

2.3
and (ii)

§

3.3
that (i) HW theory of
G
on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
and (ii) GM theory of
G
on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
, are equivalent to a (i) 3d A-model with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
on
I\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
and (ii) 3d B-model with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
on
I\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
, respectively.

The duality in

§

7.4
between the (i) 5d HW theory of
G
and (ii) 5d GM theory of
{}^{L}G
, on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
, therefore implies a duality between the (i) 3d A-model with target
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{K})
and (ii) 3d B-model with target
\mathcal{M}^{{}^{L}G}_{\text{H}}(C,\mathbf{J})
, on
I\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
, just as we observed in

§

7.1
.

Clearly, we have a more fundamental derivation than in

§

7.1
of the mirror duality between the 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
A and B-model in mirror Hitchin moduli spaces.

A Web of Langlands Dual Relations Amongst the Floer Homologies

In this section, we will use the results of the previous sections and our previous work in
[
10
]
, to derive a web of Langlands dual relations amongst the Floer homologies physically realized by 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
HW theory of
G
and GM theory of
{}^{L}G
on
M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
, where
M_{4}
is a possibly decomposable four-manifold.

8.1
Relations from the Topological Invariance of HW and GM Theory

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}

(8.1)

where
\widehat{S}^{1}
denotes a circle of variable radius.

The relations in (
8.1
) are consistent, in that they have a one-to-one correspondence in their summations over ‘
u
’, ‘
v
’, and ‘
w
’.
Specifically, each ‘
u
’, ‘
v
’, and ‘
w
’ corresponds to a solution of (the simultaneous vanishing of the LHS and RHS of) (
4.6
), (
4.13
), and (
4.22
), respectively, where (
4.22
) is obtained via a KK reduction of (
4.13
), which in turn is obtained via a KK reduction of (
4.6
).

Relations Between the Gauge-theoretic Floer Homologies from HW Theory

In
[
10
,
\lx@sectionsign
8.1]
, we showed how the topological invariance of (the
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology of) HW theory in all directions meant that we could relate the partition functions of HW theory and its lower dimensional reductions in
[
10
,
\lx@sectionsign
3–
\lx@sectionsign
5]
as
42
42

42

The notation of the Floer homologies to appear in the following equation differs from the notation used in
[
10
,
\lx@sectionsign
3–
\lx@sectionsign
5]
as follows.
First, the superscript “
\text{HW}_{d}\text{-inst}
” refers to the BPS equation of
\text{HW}_{d}
-theory on
M_{d}\times\mathbb{R}
.

\text{HW}_{5}
is the 5d HW theory as in

§

2
, and “
\text{HW}_{5}\text{-inst}
” are the HW equations (
2.2
) on
M_{4}\times\mathbb{R}
;
\text{HW}_{4}
is the 4d theory obtained via KK reduction of
\text{HW}_{5}
along an
S^{1}
circle within
M_{4}
, and “
\text{HW}_{4}\text{-inst}
” is the BPS equation of said theory; and likewise for
\text{HW}_{3}
and “
\text{HW}_{3}\text{-inst}
”.
These BPS equations can be interpreted as instanton-type equations on
M_{d}\times\mathbb{R}
as they involve an
F^{+}
term.
Second, the Floer homology classes of
M_{2}
in
[
10
,
\lx@sectionsign
5]
are generated by
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-BF configurations on
M_{2}
as critical points of a (real-valued) Morse functional of
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-valued fields; the gauge group datum thus required to denote the classes therein was
G_{\mathbb{C}}
, i.e., we denoted them by
\text{HF}^{\text{HW}_{3}}(M_{2},G_{\mathbb{C}})
.
However, it is straightforward to show that whilst these Floer homology classes are generated by
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-valued configurations on
M_{2}
, the BPS equation on
M_{2}\times\mathbb{R}
need not be complexified. Hence, since the gauge group datum is now meant to specify the BPS equation, it can just be
G
.
That is, the HW
3
-instanton Floer homology used in the following equation and the rest of this paper will be specified by
G
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sum_{k}\text{HF}^{\text{HW}_{5}\text{-inst}}_{d_{k}}(M_{4},G)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sum_{l}\text{HF}_{d_{l}}^{\text{HW}_{4}\text{-inst}}(M_{3},G)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sum_{m}\text{HF}_{d_{m}}^{\text{HW}_{3}\text{-inst}}(M_{2},G)\,,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{4}=M_{3}\times{\hat{S}}^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{3}=M_{2}\times{\hat{S}}^{1}}

(8.2)

where
(i)
\text{HF}^{\text{HW}_{5}\text{-inst}}_{d_{k}}(M_{4},G)
,
(ii)
\text{HF}_{d_{l}}^{\text{HW}_{4}\text{-inst}}(M_{3},G)
,
and (iii)
\text{HF}_{d_{m}}^{\text{HW}_{3}\text{-inst}}(M_{2},G)
,
are gauge-theoretic Floer homologies generated by
(i) VW configurations on
M_{4}
,
(ii) 3d-Hitchin configurations on
M_{3}
,
and (iii)
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-BF configurations on
M_{2}
.
The BPS equations on
M_{D}\times\mathbb{R}
defining the gradient flow equations for these Floer homologies are described in
footnote 42
.

8.2
Relations from the 5d “S-duality” of HW and GM Theory

5d “S-duality” of HW Theory

In
[
10
,
\lx@sectionsign
8.2]
, we showed how, using Tachikawa’s “S-duality” of 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
SYM with an
S^{1}

[
22
]
, one can obtain Langlands duals of the (i)
\text{HW}_{4}
-instanton and (ii)
\text{HW}_{3}
-instanton Floer homologies of (i) loop group
LG
and (ii) toroidal group
LLG
, when
G
is nonsimply-laced.
Let us briefly describe how this is done.

First, note that HW theory of
G
-type on
M_{3}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
can be interpreted as HW
4
-theory of
LG
-type on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
, which physically realizes an
LG
-HW
4
-instanton Floer homology of
M_{3}
.
5d “S-duality” of HW theory then implies an equivalence between the partition functions of HW
4
-theory of
LG
-type and HW
4
-theory of
(LG)^{\vee}
-type, where
(LG)^{\vee}
is Langlands dual to
LG
at the level of their loop algebras
[
10
, footnote 27]
.
Thus, we obtain the following relations amongst Floer homologies involving loop groups
[
10
, eqn. (8.15)]

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sum_{k}\text{HF}_{d_{k}}^{\text{HW}_{5}\text{-inst}}\big(M_{3}\times S^{1},G)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sum_{l}\text{HF}_{d_{l}}^{\text{HW}_{4}\text{-inst}}\big(M_{3},LG\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sum_{l}\text{HF}_{d_{l}}^{\text{HW}_{4}\text{-inst}}\big(M_{3},(LG)^{\vee}\big)\,.}
5d “S-duality”

(8.3)

Next, note that HW theory of
G
-type on
M_{2}\times T^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
can be interpreted as HW
3
-theory of
LLG
-type on
M_{2}\times\mathbb{R}
, which physically realizes an
LLG
-HW
3
-instanton Floer homology of
M_{2}
.
5d “S-duality” of the underlying 5d HW theory then implies an equivalence between the partition functions of HW
3
theory of
LLG
-type and HW
3
theory of
L(LG)^{\vee}
-type.
Thus, we obtain the following relations amongst Floer homologies involving toroidal groups
[
10
, eqn. (8.19)]

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sum_{k}\text{HF}_{d_{k}}^{\text{HW}_{5}\text{-inst}}\big(M_{2}\times T^{2},G)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sum_{m}\left.\text{HF}_{d_{m}}^{\text{HW}_{3}\text{-inst}}\big(M_{2},LLG\big)\right|_{X,Y=0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sum_{m}\left.\text{HF}_{d_{m}}^{\text{HW}_{3}\text{-inst}}\big(M_{2},L(LG)^{\vee}\big)\right|_{X,Y=0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sum_{m}\text{H}^{0}_{\text{dR}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{LLG_{\mathbb{C}}}_{\text{flat}}(M_{2})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sum_{m}\text{H}^{0}_{\text{dR}}\big(\mathcal{M}^{L(LG_{\mathbb{C}})^{\vee}}_{\text{flat}}(M_{2})\big)\,.}
5d “S-duality”
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g(\widehat{M}_{2})\geq 2}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g(\widehat{M}_{2})\geq 2}

(8.4)

Here, (i)
\text{H}^{0}_{\text{dR}}(\mathcal{M}^{LLG_{\mathbb{C}}}_{\text{flat}}(M_{2}))
is a de Rham class of zero-forms in the moduli space
\mathcal{M}^{LLG_{\mathbb{C}}}_{\text{flat}}(M_{2})
of flat
LLG_{\mathbb{C}}
-connections on
M_{2}
,
(ii) the dotted arrows indicate an equivalence due to topological reduction when
\widehat{M}_{2}
is a Riemann surface with genus
g(\widehat{M}_{2})\geq 2
,
and (iii) the subscript “
X,Y=0
” indicates that one is to set to zero, the scalars
X,Y
that would have arisen from KK reduction of HW theory to HW
3
theory.
43
43

43

The reason for setting to zero the scalars is as follows.
Note that the HW
3
-instanton Floer homology, as defined in
[
10
,
\lx@sectionsign
5]
, is generated by
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-BF configurations on
M_{2}
, i.e., it involves an
\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{C}})
-valued scalar field
Z
.
This field is actually defined through a complexification of
\text{ad}(G)
-valued scalar fields
X
and
Y
, i.e.,
Z\coloneq X+iY
, that were obtained via KK reduction of HW theory along two circles.
However, as the interpretation of HW theory of
G
-type as an HW
3
theory of
LLG
-type does not involve any KK reduction along circles, there will be no extra scalar fields that could contribute towards the definition of
Z
.
Thus, the HW
3
-instanton Floer homology of
LLG
-type would actually be generated by
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-flat configurations on
M_{2}
, which is the same as
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-BF configurations on
M_{2}
with
Z=0
, i.e.,
X,Y=0
.
Hence the subscripts in (
8.4
).

In the same way, 5d “S-duality” means that from GM theory on
M_{3}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
of nonsimply-laced
G
, we obtain the following relations amongst Floer homologies involving loop groups:

, we also obtain the following relations amongst Floer homologies involving toroidal groups:
44
44

44

Notice that the group datum in the Floer homologies of (i)
M_{3}
and (ii)
M_{2}
are both
G_{\mathbb{C}}
, and not (i)

and (ii)

-type on

.

8.3
A Web of Langlands Dual Relations Amongst the Floer Homologies

Applying the Langlands duality between HW theory of
G
-type and GM theory of
{}^{L}G
-type on
M_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
in

§

7.4
to the results in

§

8.1
and

§

8.2
, we obtain a web of Langlands dual relations amongst the various gauge-theoretic Floer homologies as shown in
Fig. 11
, where
(i) the radii of the
\widehat{S}^{1}
circles and sizes of
\widehat{M}_{2}
’s (where
g(\widehat{M}_{2})\geq 2
) can be varied;
(ii) horizontal undashed lines indicate a relation that is due to the Langlands duality between HW and GM theory;
(iii) dashed lines indicate a relation that is due to an equivalence under dimensional/topological reduction;
(iv) dotted lines indicate a relation due to an “S-duality” of the underlying 5d theory that realizes the Floer homology;
and (v) finely dotted lines are used to specify the manifolds.

Mirror Symmetry and Langlands Duality of
A_{\infty}
-categories of Floer Homologies

In this section, we will use the results of the previous sections and our previous works in
[
10
,
12
]
to derive a mirror symmetry and Langlands duality of the
A_{\infty}
-categories physically realized by 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
HW theory of
G
and GM theory of
{}^{L}G
on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, where
M_{3}
is a possibly decomposable three-manifold.

9.1
Mirror Symmetry and Langlands Duality Between Fukaya-Seidel-type and Orlov-type
A_{\infty}
-1-categories

HW Theory and a Gauge-theoretic Fukaya-Seidel-type
A_{\infty}
-1-category

In
[
10
,
\lx@sectionsign
9]
, we showed how HW theory of
G
-type on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
physically realizes a gauge-theoretic Fukaya-Seidel-type (FS-type)
A_{\infty}
-1-category of (
\theta
-deformed) 3d-Hitchin configurations on
M_{3}
, thereby 1-categorifying the (
\theta
-deformed)
G
-HW
4
-instanton Floer homology
\text{HF}^{\text{HW}_{4}\text{-inst}}(M_{3},G)
of
M_{3}
.

GM Theory and a Gauge-theoretic Orlov-type
A_{\infty}
-1-category

In

§

5
, we showed how GM theory of
G
-type on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
physically realizes a gauge-theoretic Orlov-type
A_{\infty}
-1-category of (
\theta
-deformed)
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-BF configurations on
M_{3}
, thereby 1-categorifying the (
\theta
-deformed)
G_{\mathbb{H}}
-flat Floer homology
\text{HF}^{\text{flat}}(M_{3},G_{\mathbb{H}})
of
M_{3}
.

A Langlands Duality Between the Gauge-theoretic 1-Categories

Applying the Langlands duality between HW theory of
G
-type and GM theory of
{}^{L}G
-type on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
(see

§

7.4
with
M_{1}=\mathbb{R}
), we obtain a Langlands dual relation between the gauge-theoretic FS-type
A_{\infty}
-1-category that 1-categorifies
\text{HF}^{\text{HW}_{4}\text{-inst}}(M_{3},G)
and the gauge-theoretic Orlov-type
A_{\infty}
-1-category that 1-categorifies
\text{HF}^{\text{flat}}(M_{3},{{}^{L}G_{\mathbb{H}}})
.

Recall from

§

8
, in particular
Fig. 11
, that the objects of these 1-categories can be identified with each other. Thus, this relation between 1-categories is indeed a consistent one.

A Langlands Dual Gauge-theoretic Generalization of HMS for LG Models and a Mirror Symmetry of Gauge-theoretic 1-Categories

At this point, note that a relation between an FS 1-category of
vanishing cycles
and an Orlov 1-category of
singularities
is the statement of HMS for LG models
[
20
]
.
As such, since the objects of our related 1-categories are
gauge-theoretic Floer homologies
, what we actually have is a Langlands dual
gauge-theoretic generalization
of HMS for LG models!

In turn, this means that the Langlands dual relation between the gauge-theoretic FS-type
A_{\infty}
-1-category that 1-categorifies
\text{HF}^{\text{HW}_{4}\text{-inst}}(M_{3},G)
and the gauge-theoretic Orlov-type
A_{\infty}
-1-category that 1-categorifies
\text{HF}^{\text{flat}}(M_{3},{{}^{L}G_{\mathbb{H}}})
, is
also
a mirror symmetric one!

9.2
Mirror Symmetry and Langlands Duality Between Fueter and RW
A_{\infty}
-2-categories

HW Theory and a Fueter
A_{\infty}
-2-category

In
[
12
,
\lx@sectionsign
7]
, we showed how, via a Heegaard split of
M_{3}
along a Heegaard surface
C
, HW theory of
G
-type on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
physically realizes a Fueter
A_{\infty}
-2-category of complex-Lagrangian branes
L
of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{K})
.

GM Theory and an RW
A_{\infty}
-2-category

In

§

5.5
, we showed how, via a Heegaard split of
M_{3}
along a Heegaard surface
C
, GM theory of
G
-type on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
physically realizes an RW
A_{\infty}
-2-category of complex-Lagrangian branes
\widehat{L}
of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J})
.

A Langlands Duality between the 2-Categories

Applying the Langlands duality between HW theory of
G
-type and GM theory of
{}^{L}G
-type on
M_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
(see

§

7.4
with
M_{1}=\mathbb{R}
), we obtain a Langlands dual relation between the Fueter
A_{\infty}
-2-category of complex-Lagrangian branes
L
of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{K})
and the RW
A_{\infty}
-2-category of complex-Lagrangian branes
\widehat{L}
of
\mathcal{M}^{{}^{L}G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J})
.

Since
L
and
\widehat{L}
can be identified under the enhanced HMS of the mirror manifolds
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{K})
and
\mathcal{M}^{{}^{L}G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J})

[
14
]
, this relation between 2-categories is indeed a consistent one.

A Mirror Symmetry of 2-Categories

Note that the above Fueter and RW 2-category of branes, defined by a Fueter and constant map, are just higher-categorical generalizations of a Fukaya and Derived 1-category of branes, defined by a holomorphic and constant map, respectively. Indeed, if we were to dimensionally reduce the 3d A/B topological sigma models which physically realize the former, we would end up with the 2d A/B topological sigma models which physically realize the latter, where the 3d Fueter and constant map BPS equations would reduce to the 2d holomorphic and constant map BPS equations.

Thus, as (enhanced) HMS
[
14
]
relates the Fukaya and Derived 1-category of branes, it would mean that the Langlands dual relation between the Fueter
A_{\infty}
-2-category of complex-Lagrangian branes
L
of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{K})
and the RW
A_{\infty}
-2-category of complex-Lagrangian branes
\widehat{L}
of
\mathcal{M}^{{}^{L}G}_{\text{H},\theta}(C,\mathbf{J})
, can
also
be regarded a mirror symmetric one!

A Physical Proof of Bousseau-Doan-Rezchikov’s Mirror Conjecture

At any rate, a relation between a Fueter
A_{\infty}
-2-category and a KRS 2-category of branes in mirror hyperkähler spaces was conjectured by Bousseau-Doan-Rezchikov (B-DR) in
[
7
,
9
]
.
Via the correspondence between RW
A_{\infty}
-2-categories and KRS 2-categories shown in

§

5.5
, it would also mean that we have a relation between a Fueter
A_{\infty}
-2-category and a KRS 2-category of complex-Lagrangian branes in mirror hyperkähler Hitchin moduli spaces.

In other words, we have a purely physical proof of the B-DR mirror conjecture!

9.3
Mirror Symmetry and Langlands Duality Between Fueter-type and RW-type
A_{\infty}
-2-categories

HW Theory and a Gauge-theoretic Fueter-type
A_{\infty}
-2-category

In
[
12
,
\lx@sectionsign
3]
, we showed how HW theory of
G
-type on
M_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
physically realizes a gauge-theoretic Fueter-type
A_{\infty}
-2-category of (
\theta
-deformed)
G_{\mathbb{C}}
-BF configurations on
M_{2}
, thereby 2-categorifying the (
\theta
-deformed)
G
-HW
3
-instanton Floer homology
\text{HF}^{\text{HW}_{3}{\text{-inst}}}(M_{2},G)
of
M_{2}
.

GM Theory and a Gauge-theoretic RW-type
A_{\infty}
-2-category

In

§

6
, we showed how GM theory of
G
-type on
M_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
physically realizes a gauge-theoretic RW-type
A_{\infty}
-2-category of (
\theta
-deformed)
G_{\mathbb{O}}
-BF configurations on
M_{2}
, thereby 2-categorifying the (
\theta
-deformed)
G_{\mathbb{O}}
-flat Floer homology
\text{HF}^{\text{flat}}(M_{2},G_{\mathbb{O}})
of
M_{2}
.

A Langlands Duality Between the Gauge-theoretic 2-Categories

Applying the Langlands duality between HW theory of
G
-type and GM theory of
{}^{L}G
-type on
M_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
(see

§

7.4
), we obtain a Langlands dual relation between a Fueter-type
A_{\infty}
-2-category that 2-categorifies
\text{HF}^{\text{HW}_{3}{\text{-inst}}}(M_{2},G)
and an RW-type
A_{\infty}
-2-category that 2-categorifies
\text{HF}^{\text{flat}}(M_{2},{{}^{L}G}_{\mathbb{O}})
.

Recall from

§

8
, in particular
Fig. 11
, that the objects of these 2-categories can be identified with each other. Thus, this relation between 2-categories is indeed a consistent one.

A Mirror Symmetry of Gauge-theoretic 2-Categories

Note that the above Fueter and RW-type 2-category of Floer homologies, defined by a Fueter and constant map, are just higher-categorical generalizations of an FS and Orlov-type 1-category of Floer homologies, defined by a holomorphic and constant map, respectively. Indeed, if we were to take a hyperslice of the 3d A/B-twisted LG models which physically realize the former, we would end up with the 2d A/B-twisted LG models which physically realize the latter, where the 3d Fueter and constant map BPS equations would reduce to the 2d holomorphic and constant map BPS equations.

Hence, as a gauge-theoretic generalization of HMS for LG models relates the FS and Orlov-type 1-category of Floer homologies (see

§

9.1
), it would mean that the Langlands dual relation between a Fueter-type
A_{\infty}
-2-category that 2-categorifies
\text{HF}^{\text{HW}_{3}{\text{-inst}}}(M_{2},G)
and an RW-type
A_{\infty}
-2-category that 2-categorifies
\text{HF}^{\text{flat}}(M_{2},{{}^{L}G}_{\mathbb{O}})
, can
also
be regarded as a mirror symmetric one!

A Gauge-theoretic Generalization of the B-DR Mirror Conjecture

Since the objects of our related categories are
gauge-theoretic Floer homologies
, what we actually have is a gauge-theoretic generalization of our result in

§

9.2
. In other words, we have a
gauge-theoretic generalization
of the B-DR mirror conjecture!

, where

\widehat{C}
can be varied,
(ii) dashed lines indicate a relation that is due to an equivalence under dimensional/topological reduction,
and (iii) wavy lines denote the implications of a relation on other relations.
